“I will be your guide, my lord,” replied Caleb, “and this very night I will conduct you. …”

“Where?”

“To the sibyl of Rhacotis, an old sorceress who knows everything.”

“We will go by ourselves, in secret.”

“Very well, my lord; no one shall accompany us. … What do you think: would you not like a cool sherbet after your rest? And divert yourself with looking at the goods which the travelling merchants from distant foreign lands, who happen today to be staying in the diversorium, have to offer for sale? I will have the sherbet prepared and the merchants informed, my lord. And tonight I will lead you through Rhacotis: we will go by ourselves, my lord, and no one shall know anything of our nocturnal expedition.”

Caleb went away; Tarrar drew the curtains aside. Beyond the bedchamber was a pillared portico; and the green shadow of the palm-garden outside fell within doors. Uncle Catullus was still asleep, but Thrasyllus already sat reading his Egyptian guidebooks at a table under a palm-tree. The wonderful, fantastic stories of Herodotus charmed the old tutor’s mind, which was not disinclined to fantasy; but Thrasyllus also took pleasure in the more succinct descriptions of the learned Eratosthenes, Ptolemy Evergetes’ librarian, who lived three centuries before and was a noted astronomer, philosopher and geographer. Thrasyllus loved to consult his splendid maps, which had never yet been bettered and which lay spread in heavy parchment on the table before him; and the tutor followed the cinnabar-traced Nile on these maps down to Ethiopia and the mysterious sources of the sacred stream.

===Chinese===
“我愿做您的向导，老爷，”卡莱布答道，“就在今夜，我就带您去。……”

“去哪儿？”

“去拉科提斯的女巫那儿，一个无所不知的老巫婆。”

“就我们自己，偷偷地去。”

“很好，老爷；谁也不跟着。……您看怎么样：歇过之后，来一杯冰凉的果子露如何？再看看那些远方外邦来的行商今天恰好住在客栈里、拿出来发卖的货色，解解闷？我去叫人备果子露、通知商人，老爷。今夜我就领您穿行拉科提斯：就我们两个人去，老爷，咱们的夜行探险，谁也不会知道半点。”

卡莱布退下了；塔拉尔把帷幔拉开。卧房外面是一道柱廊；外面棕榈园的绿荫投进门里。卡图卢斯叔父还睡着，特拉叙卢斯却已坐在一株棕榈树下的桌旁，读他的埃及旅行指南。希罗多德（Herodotus）那些奇妙而荒诞的故事让老教师的心听得入迷——他本不排斥奇幻；但特拉叙卢斯也乐于读博学的埃拉托色尼那些较为简明的记述——他是托勒密·欧尔格特斯的图书馆长，生活在三百年前，是著名的天文学家、哲学家和地理学家。特拉叙卢斯爱查看他那几幅出色的地图——至今无人超越——厚厚的羊皮地图摊在他面前的桌上；教师顺着地图上朱砂描出的尼罗河，一路看到埃塞俄比亚和那条圣河神秘的源头。

===Original===
Yes, Eratosthenes was the most respectable guide. When he went blind, in his eighty-second year, he starved himself. Thrasyllus honoured him as a martyr of science. But the tutor also consulted Artemidorus and Hypsicrates, for he wished to be well-informed about the country which he was about to visit, that mysterious country of age-old history and colossal art, while also he did not despise the quite modern writings of his contemporary, Strabo: what a contemporary told about a country over the whole of which he had travelled was perhaps most important of all, because of its practical utility and also because of the freshness of the new impressions.

So Thrasyllus sat under his palm-tree at a table strewn with unrolled papyri; more scrolls stood in a case by his side; and his fingers followed the cinnabar-traced Nile. Lucius in the portico smiled in kindly approval. But the travelling merchants, led by Caleb, arrived through the garden. They were Indians, Sabaeans, Arabs, Phoenicians; and their slaves toiled under their heavy bales of merchandise, which were slung on pliant sticks over their shoulders. The merchants bent low in salaams before the wealthy Roman, bowed down to the earth, kissed the ground which his foot had trodden, all eager to sell their exotic wares at a profit above the ordinary to so distinguished a traveller. The Phoenicians made their slaves spread out Damascus tapestry, but Lucius looked at it with scorn and the Phoenicians at once rolled up their inferior tapestry. Then, however, they displayed embroidery from Nineveh and Tyre; and Lucius turned a little pale, because he thought of Ilia. It was all very beautiful in hue and very curious in pattern.

“Call Uncle Catullus here,” he said to Tarrar, who was squatting beside him like a faithful little monkey.

===Chinese===
是的，埃拉托色尼是最可敬的向导。他八十二岁那年双目失明，便绝食自尽。特拉叙卢斯尊他为科学的殉道者。但教师也参看阿尔特米多鲁斯（Artemidorus）和希普西克拉特斯（Hypsicrates）的著述，因为他想把自己将要游历的这个国家摸个透彻——那个有着古老历史与巨大艺术的神秘国度；同时他也不轻视同时代人斯特拉波那些相当时新的著述：一位同时代人讲述他亲身游历过全境的国度，也许最要紧，因为实用，也因为那些新印象的鲜活。

特拉叙卢斯就这样坐在他的棕榈树下，桌摊着展开的纸草卷；更多的卷轴立在手边的书箱里；他的手指顺着朱砂描出的尼罗河抚过去。柱廊里的卢基乌斯微笑着，嘉许地望着。这时行商们在卡莱布引领下穿过园林来了。他们是印度人、萨巴人、阿拉伯人、腓尼基人；他们的奴隶扛着沉重的货物包，负荷在韧性的扁担上，压弯了肩。商人们在那位富裕的罗马人面前深深打躬，一躬到地，亲吻他的脚踏过的尘土，个个急着想把自己异域的货品以高出寻常的利润卖给这样一位显赫的旅人。腓尼基人让奴隶铺开大马士革织锦，卢基乌斯却轻蔑地扫了一眼，腓尼基人立刻卷起他们次等的织锦。可接着他们展示出尼尼微和推罗的刺绣；卢基乌斯微微变了脸色，因为他想起了伊利娅。那些刺绣色彩艳丽，纹样奇巧。

“去把卡图卢斯叔父叫来，”他对塔拉尔说；小家伙正蹲在他身边，像一只忠心的小猴子。

===Original===
Tarrar hastened to Catullus, who thereupon arrived, sleepily rubbing his eyes, in a wide silk indoor simar; his grey hair stood in a tangle around his bald skull.

“Uncle,” said Lucius, aside, “look here, if you please. Those embroideries from Tyre and Nineveh: I want them. Bargain for them.”

For Uncle Catullus knew how to bargain. He began by turning up his nose at the embroideries; and the merchants uttered loud cries of protest and lifted their hands and invoked all the gods. But Uncle Catullus scornfully shook his head and said:

“No, I won’t buy that trash. Show me other things.”

Then the Phoenicians showed gold vessels from Tartessus, but the Arabs offered perfumes and aromatics from Jeddah and Zebid. The Sabaeans displayed wonderful amulets, which bring luck and blissful dreams: the Indians showed tame, trained snakes, as domestic pets: the snakes had a small sardonyx encrusted in their heads, where it had grown into their scaly skin, and they danced on the tips of their tails, to the piping of the Indians’ flutes. They were attractive little creatures and did not cost more than one stater apiece, with the ebony casket in which they were kept; and Lucius impatiently bought them at once, partly because Tarrar found them so attractive and grinned where he squatted and looked on while the snakes danced and twisted one among another.

===Chinese===
塔拉尔飞跑去找卡图卢斯；叔父随即就来了，睡眼惺忪地揉着眼，穿着一件宽大的丝绸家常长袍；灰发乱蓬蓬地支棱在秃顶四周。

“叔父，”卢基乌斯偏过头低声说，“劳驾，瞧这儿。推罗和尼尼微来的那些刺绣：我要。你去讲价。”

因为讲价是卡图卢斯叔父的拿手好戏。他先对那些刺绣嗤之以鼻；商人们大声抗议，举起双手，把满天神佛都请了出来。可卡图卢斯叔父轻蔑地摇头，说：

“不，这种破烂我不买。拿别的给我看。”

于是腓尼基人捧出塔特苏斯的金器，阿拉伯人献上吉达和兹比德的香水和香料。萨巴人展示神奇的护身符，能带来好运和美梦：印度人展出驯养调教过的蛇，当家庭宠物：蛇头里嵌着一小块缟玛瑙，长进了鳞甲皮里；它们随着印度人的笛声，用尾巴尖跳舞。这些小生灵招人喜欢，连装它们的乌木匣子算在内，每条不过一个斯塔特；卢基乌斯不耐烦地立刻全买了下来，一半也是因为塔拉尔觉得它们太好玩了，蹲在一边看着蛇一条缠着一条地扭摆起舞，咧着嘴直乐。

===Original===
But at last a Mongolian merchant arrived, with a pale-yellow face and narrow eyes, which looked as though they were closed, and his hair, around his shaven head, ended in a pigtail of purple silk, with a tassel to it. This merchant offered little black balls, to be smoked in peculiar pipes; he asked Lucius to accept a pipe and a couple of little black pills in a yellow-silk bag, without payment, and to smoke them when he had the opportunity. The intoxication which they produced was something very peculiar, said the merchant.

Meanwhile Uncle Catullus had duly succeeded in acquiring the embroideries from Tyre and Nineveh at a really laughable price and presented them to his nephew, who of course paid for them in his stead. But, when Lucius held them in his hands—they were narrow strips embroidered with Assyrian lions and strange unicorns—he grew sad and said:

“What use are they to me, after all? Time was when I should have given them to Ilia as a border for her stola. Tarrar, put the pretty embroideries away, with the Mongolian pills and all the other rubbish which I have bought without wanting to: the little gold vases and the Sabaean amulets.”

“And the dear little snakes, my lord?” asked Tarrar, with glittering eyes.

“You may keep them … to play with,” said Lucius, carelessly.

Meanwhile Caleb had had the cups of sherbet handed round. Uncle Catullus thought it particularly good and considered that Lucius’ cook ought really to write down this Egyptian recipe; but Lucius gave his to Tarrar, who scooped up the sherbet greedily with his black fingers.

===Chinese===
最后来了一位蒙古商人，一张淡黄的脸，眼睛细得像闭着似的，剃光的头四周留的头发在脑后编成一条紫丝辫子，缀着流苏。这商人拿出一些黑色小球，要用特制的烟斗抽吸；他请卢基乌斯无偿收下一杆烟斗和两颗小黑药丸，装在一只黄绸袋里，得便时抽一抽。他说，它们引起的醉意非常特别。

与此同时，卡图卢斯叔父果然以一个简直叫人笑话的价钱把推罗和尼尼微的刺绣弄到了手，呈给侄儿——钱当然由侄儿替他付。可卢基乌斯把它们捧在手里——那是几条窄幅绣带，绣着亚述狮子和奇异的独角兽——忽然难过起来，说：

“这些到底对我有什么用呢？从前我会把它们送给伊利娅，给她的斯托拉（stola）镶边。塔拉尔，把这几条漂亮刺绣收起来，跟蒙古药丸和我并不想要却买了的那些破烂一起：小金瓶啊，萨巴护身符啊，都收起来。”

“还有那两条亲爱的小蛇呢，老爷？”塔拉尔眼睛亮晶晶地问。

“就归你了……拿去玩吧，”卢基乌斯漫不经心地说。

这其间卡莱布已让人把一杯杯果子露端了上来。卡图卢斯叔父觉得味道特别好，认为卢基乌斯的厨子真该把这个埃及方子记下来；卢基乌斯却把自己那杯给了塔拉尔，小家伙用黑手指贪婪地舀着吃。

## IX / 九

===Original===
In the strange bright summer night of light, lit by the sheen of the stars and the glow of the lamps, Canopus rose amid its slender obelisks and its spreading palm-trees. The barges lay moored to the long quay, one beside the other. One solemn train of pilgrims after another flowed down the street to the temple of Serapis. The town was alive with the whisper of music and aglow with illumination.

It was midnight. From the temple of Serapis heavy gong-strokes sounded, like a divine, golden thunder rolling at regular intervals under the stars. The singing processions, bathed in torchlight, streamed towards the temple.

There was a wide avenue paved with large, square stones. This avenue, or dromos, led to the sanctuary, the temenos, along a double row of immense basalt sphinxes, half woman, half lioness; half man, half bull. They were drawn up like superhuman sentinels that had turned to stone; and their great human faces stared raptly into the night. In between the sphinxes, the coloured lamps and lanterns blossomed like lotus-flowers, glowing blue, red and yellow.

===Chinese===
在这个奇异的、明亮的夏日光之夜——星光与灯光交相辉映的夜里，卡诺珀斯在纤细的方尖碑与舒展的棕榈树之间耸立着。游船一艘挨着一艘，泊在长长的码头边。一队又一队庄重的朝圣行列，沿着街道涌向萨拉皮斯神庙。全城活跃着音乐的低语，辉映着灯火。

正是午夜。萨拉皮斯神庙里响起沉重的锣声，像神圣的、金色的雷鸣，在星辰之下按着节律滚动。唱着颂歌的行列沐在火把光里，涌向神庙。

有一条宽阔的大道，铺着大块的方石。这条大道，即圣道（dromos），沿着两排巨大的玄武岩斯芬克斯（sphinx），通向圣所、圣域（temenos）：那些斯芬克斯半是女人、半是母狮；半是男人、半是公牛。它们列队而立，宛如化为石头的超人卫兵；巨大的人面出神地凝视着夜色。斯芬克斯之间，彩灯与灯笼如莲花朵朵绽放，闪着蓝、红、黄的光。

===Original===
The processions streamed into the dromos at pilgrims’ pace. Through the dromos they reached the first propylaeum, then the second, the third, the fourth. These consisted of a gigantic series of heavy pylons, painted with hieroglyphics: a veritable forest of pylon-trunks rising in serried ranks of frowning columns and crowned with heavy architraves which seemed to support the starry realm of the summer night itself. Through these endless rows of pillars the dense multitude of pilgrims in search of their dreams marched to the music of hymns. It marched with its steady, slow, regular, religious tread. And monotonous as the rhythm of its march was the melody of its hymn, borne upon ever the same harp-chords.

Lucius’ procession marched with the others. He walked gravely, with Catullus by his side; Thrasyllus followed; the slaves, male and female, followed. In front of him strode his musicians, singers and dancers. And Cora’s voice rose only a little higher in the ever-repeated hymn to the god Serapis.

The temple itself produced a sense of infinity. An immense forecourt, or pronaos, soared on high with its pillars, a forest of pylons crowned by the roof, with its painted hieroglyphics. The pronaos gave admittance to the sanctuary, the holy of holies, an immeasurable empty space, without image, without altar, without anything. Nevertheless as it were a mysterious sanctity descended here, because of the height, the impressive, colossal dimensions. The “wings,” or pteres, the two sidewalls, sculptured with symbolic bas-reliefs, painted gold, azure and scarlet, approached each other with slanting lines in a mystic perspective, where a cloud of fragrance hovered like a conflagration. Behind this the holy of holies lost itself, the abode of the god, of Serapis; invisible the statue. A swarm of acolytes, zacori and neocori, were officiating on ascending stairs, in worship before close-drawn hyacinth curtains.

===Chinese===
朝圣的行列以朝圣者的步伐涌进圣道。穿过圣道，他们到达第一道塔门（propylaeum），然后是第二道、第三道、第四道。那是一系列巨大而沉重的塔门（pylon），绘着象形文字：真可谓一片塔门的森林，森然成列的塔身巍然耸起，顶着沉重的大梁，仿佛支撑着夏夜的星空本身。穿过这些无穷无尽的柱列，密集的寻梦朝圣者在颂歌的乐声中行进。他们迈着沉稳、缓慢、规整而虔诚的步子。行进的节奏如何单调，颂歌的旋律也同样单调，永远乘在同样的竖琴和弦上。

卢基乌斯的行列随众人一同行进。他庄重地走着，卡图卢斯叔父在旁；特拉叙卢斯跟着；男女奴隶们跟着。他前面走着他的乐师、歌者与舞者。在那反复吟唱的萨拉皮斯颂歌中，科拉的嗓音只是略高出些许。

神庙本身给人一种无穷无尽之感。巨大的前庭，即前殿（pronaos），带着它的柱列高耸而上，一片塔门的森林，冠以殿顶，绘满象形文字。前殿再往里便是圣所，至圣之所——一片不可度量的空阔，没有神像，没有祭坛，没有任何东西。然而一种神秘的圣洁仿佛降临于此，因为它的高度，因为它那慑人的、巨大的尺度。「两翼」，即侧墙（pteres），雕着象征性的浮雕，漆成金色、青色与绯红，以斜线在神秘的透视中彼此逼近，那里一团香雾如火燎般缭绕不散。在这后面，至圣之所渐渐隐没，那是神、萨拉皮斯的居所；神像无形可见。一大群侍祭——扎科里（zacori）与尼奥科里（neocori）——在逐级升起的阶梯上执事，在拉拢的风信子色帘幕前礼拜。

===Original===
The processions divided themselves along the wings, the sidewalls, as directed by the temple-keepers’ wands. It was as though a broad stream were dividing into two rivers. At the end of the wings, behind the holy of holies, flights of stairs widened in the open night, leading to terraces, the one ever higher than the other, so that they could not be overlooked. The golden gong-strokes solemnly rolled and thundered, echoing heavily and loudly.

Over the terraces, in a constant round, up and down, marched the chief priests, the hieropsalts, the hieroscopes, the hierogrammats, the pastophors, the sphagists and the stolists. The hieropsalts sang the hymns to the sacred harps; the hieroscopes prophesied from the entrails of victims; the hierogrammats guarded the secrets of the Hermetic wisdom; the pastophors carried the images of Anubis, with the dog’s head, in silver boats; the sphagists were the sacrificial priests; the stolists served the sacred images, adorned them, tended them with ever clean and perfumed hands. But among the hierogrammats strode the prophets. They had beheld the godhead face to face; they knew the past and the future, they knew the meaning of the sacred dreams. They were very holy; and the oldest of them were most holy. Whenever they approached, the people sank to the ground and kissed the pavement, with hands uplifted.

The sacred hour approached, the hour when Serapis would send the sacred dreams from heaven, out of the sun itself, when all the procession would have streamed in, when the gates of the dromos would have slammed with their ponderous monolithic doors, when the last gong-stroke would clatter away in the sacred night.

From the terraces the town, the canal, and the lake lay visible as in one golden shimmer of lights. But on the terraces themselves suddenly an incredible stillness reigned. Not a voice, not a rustle sounded from out of that multitude of thousands. And on the granite pavement the pilgrims were stretched one beside the other.

===Chinese===
行列顺着神庙执事的手杖指引，沿着两翼侧墙分开，仿佛一道宽阔的水流分作两条河。在两翼尽头、至圣之所的后面，一段段阶梯在露天的夜色里拓展开来，通向层层平台，一层比一层高，不容一眼看尽。金色的锣声庄严地滚动、轰鸣，回声沉重而响亮。

平台上，祭司长们循环不息地上下巡行：圣咏祭司（hieropsalt）、占卜祭司（hieroscope）、圣书祭司（hierogrammat）、抬像祭司（pastophor）、宰牲祭司（sphagist）与圣衣祭司（stolist）。圣咏祭司们和着神圣的竖琴唱颂歌；占卜祭司们凭祭牲的内脏预言；圣书祭司们守护赫尔墨斯智慧（Hermetic wisdom）的秘密；抬像祭司们抬着银船里狗首的阿努比斯（Anubis）神像；宰牲祭司们是司掌献祭的祭司；圣衣祭司们侍奉圣像，为它们妆饰，用永远洁净而芳香的手照料它们。而在圣书祭司中间，昂首走着先知们。他们曾面对面得见神；他们知晓过去与未来，知晓圣梦的含义。他们极为圣洁；其中最年长者最为圣洁。他们所到之处，民众便伏倒在地，高举双手，亲吻铺地的石板。

神圣的时刻临近了——那时萨拉皮斯将从天上、从太阳本身把圣梦降下；那时所有的行列都已涌入；那时圣道的大门将带着沉重的整块石门轰然关上；那时最后一声锣响将在神圣的夜里消散而去。

从平台上望下去，城、运河与湖尽收眼底，恍若笼在同一片金色的光晕里。而平台本身，忽然笼罩了一种难以置信的寂静。成千上万的人群之中，没有一点人声，没有一丝窸窣。花岗岩的石面上，朝圣者们一个挨着一个地躺卧下去。

===Original===
In between the rows the temple-keepers moved, the neocori. And they bent incessantly over the pilgrims and covered them with the dreaming-nets and -veils, while zacori slung the censers. A heavy, intoxicating perfume of almost stifling aromatic vapour was wafted through the air.

Suddenly, through the silence, the harps of the hieropsalts struck the sacred chord.

There was a short hymn, one single phrase, which melted away.

On the vast terraces the multitude of the thousands of pilgrims lay motionless under nets and veils, their eyes closed. Not a sound came from the illuminated city. The sacred silence reigned wide and mystic, fraught with terror, over the sea, along the starry sky, over the city and the temple. For Serapis, invisible, was rising from the underworld, to bring the dreams.

===Chinese===
行列之间，神庙执事们——尼奥科里——来回走动。他们不停地俯身在朝圣者身上，给他们罩上做梦的网与面纱，扎科里们则摇着香炉。一股浓重的、令人迷醉的香气，那几乎令人窒息的芳馨蒸汽，在空气中荡漾开来。

忽然，寂静之中，圣咏祭司的竖琴拨出了神圣的和弦。

一段短促的颂歌，仅只一句，便消融而去。

在辽阔的平台上，成千上万的朝圣者躺在网与面纱下面，一动不动，闭着眼睛。灯火通明的城没有一点声息。神圣的寂静广阔而神秘、带着慑人的恐怖，笼罩着大海，沿着星空，笼罩着城与神庙。因为萨拉皮斯，无形的萨拉皮斯，正从冥界升起，带来梦。

===Original===
He rose in a cloud of dreams, out of the sacred, subterranean Hell, where he reigns even as Osiris reigns in high Heaven. He is Osiris himself; between him and Osiris there is no difference. He is two. While Osiris is the benevolent Almighty above, he is the benevolent Almighty below. He opposes Typhon, even as Osiris combated Typhon. Victory falls to him in the end, even as it did to Osiris.

Now he rises, in the cloud of dreams. For it is his feast, the feast of his kindly waters, which he pours in summer rains from the sacred vessels wherewith the dog’s-head of Anubis, his watchman, servant and comrade, is crowned, the waters which he pours into the sacred stream, so that it may flood sacred Egypt. Now he rises in the cloud of dreams.

The earth splits and Serapis rises from the subterranean Hell. He is everything, even as Osiris is. He is feminine, Neith, the beginning, and masculine, Ammon, eternity. He is what the last will be. And he cannot be other than the benefactor. He makes the dreams hover like butterflies around the foreheads of those who believe in him. His healing power makes whole the sick. He pours the secret of that healing into the minds of the servants of sufferers who shall dream in their masters’ stead. His dreams advise what must be done or left undone to achieve prosperity, fortune, consideration, happiness and love.

And he will make Lusius dream where to find a beloved woman who has disappeared.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他升起在梦的云雾里，从神圣的地底冥府升起；在那下面他统治着，正如俄西里斯在高天之上统治。他就是俄西里斯本人；他与俄西里斯之间没有分别。他即是二者。俄西里斯是上面的仁慈全能者，他是下面的仁慈全能者。他对抗堤丰（Typhon），正如俄西里斯曾对抗堤丰。胜利终将归于他，正如当年归于俄西里斯。

如今他升起来了，在梦的云雾里。因为这是他的节日，他仁慈之水的节日；他以夏日的雨水，从阿努比斯——他的守望者、仆役与同伴——狗首所冠戴的神圣器皿中，把水倾下；他把这水注入神圣的河流，让它漫淹神圣的埃及。如今他升起在梦的云雾里。

大地裂开，萨拉皮斯从地底冥府升起。他是一切，正如俄西里斯是一切。他是女性的奈斯（Neith），太初；又是男性的阿蒙（Ammon），永恒。他是终末之所是。他不能不是那造福之人。他让梦如蝴蝶一般，盘旋在信他之人的额头周围。他的疗愈之力使病者得以痊愈。他把疗愈的秘密，倾注进病家仆从的心智，让他们代主人做梦。他的梦指点人们：要得兴旺、财富、声望、幸福与爱情，何事当为，何事当止。

而他将让卢基乌斯（Lusius）梦见，到哪里去寻那位失踪的心爱女人。……

===Original===
In the silence the young Roman lies, covered with a gold network, like a precious mummy, straight out, his arms beside his body, his eyes shut. Near him lie all his followers.

The cloud of the perfumes is wafted over their eyes reverently closed under the veils.

The sacred silence continues, hour after hour, unbroken.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
寂静中，年轻的罗马人躺着，罩着金色的网，像一具珍贵的木乃伊，直挺挺地，双臂贴着身侧，双眼阖着。他近旁躺着他的全部随从。

香雾缭绕，拂过他们在面纱下虔敬闭合的双眼。

神圣的寂静持续着，一个钟头又一个钟头，不曾间断。……

## Postscript / 后记

===Original===
Caleb of Mariaba (Saba), joint proprietor of the Great Diversorium, the Hermes House, at Alexandria, near the Canopian Gate, to the ever noble Lord Publius Lucius Sabinus, sculptor, at Cos.

“Written at Alexandria, at the second hour of the fourteenth day of the month of Pacothi, in the ninth year of the benevolent reign of our gracious Emperor Tiberius Augustus.

My ever noble Lord, greeting!

===Chinese===
马里亚巴（萨巴）的卡莱布，亚历山大里亚卡诺皮斯门（Canopian Gate）附近大客栈「赫尔墨斯之家」的合伙人，致科斯的雕塑匠、永远尊贵的普布利乌斯·卢基乌斯·萨比努斯老爷。

「书于亚历山大里亚，仁慈的提比略·奥古斯都陛下圣明御宇第九年，帕科提月（Pacothi）十四日第二个时辰。

永远尊贵的老爷，敬颂台安！

===Original===
“It is a pleasure to me, Caleb of Mariaba, to inform you by this letter, entrusted to Alexandros of Alexandria, master of the merchant trireme *Berenice*, that by chance and great good fortune I have succeeded in disposing of the valuable quadrireme, the pleasure-ship *Aphrodite*, once your property, noble lord, and in the presence of witnesses, free men, transferred to me at Cape Dire, by the Pillars of Sesostris6﻿ ﻿… selling her at Arsinoe, previous to the passage through the Nechao Canal (which would probably have been liable to legal objections), at the very profitable price (as, considering all the circumstances, it appears to me, your most obedient, humble servant, friend, guide and companion in the chase) of (after deducting my commission and all my expenses) 900,000 (say, nine hundred thousand) sesterces, a sum reckoned according to the Roman value, item that noble quadrireme *Aphrodite* and three hundred rowers (slaves), item all the necessary and most complete equipment, item costly furniture, carpets, plate and treasures of art, which would probably have produced large sums had it been possible to sell them separately, without danger of seizure in the name of our gracious Emperor Tiberius Augustus, but the separate sale of which would, in the circumstances aforesaid, have entailed innumerable drawbacks;

“Whereas I, Caleb of Mariaba, acting and appearing on my own behalf, as actual owner of the noble quadrireme *Aphrodite*, have been able to sell this magnificent pleasure-ship to the noble Lord Baabab, a Persian satrap, residing at Susa;

===Chinese===
「马里亚巴的卡莱布幸甚，谨借本函——托付予亚历山大里亚人亚历山德罗斯（Alexandros），商用三列桨船「贝雷尼基」号（Berenice）的船长——奉告老爷：我侥幸撞了大运，已将那艘珍贵的四列桨船、老爷旧日的产业——游乐船「阿佛洛狄忒」号（Aphrodite）——成功脱手；该船系在迪雷角、塞索斯特里斯石柱旁，当着见证人（自由民）的面转到我名下，6……今在阿尔西诺伊售出——在过尼科运河之前（过运河恐怕会招致法律上的异议），售价（依小人——您最恭顺卑微的仆人、朋友、向导与围猎伙伴——就各方面情形之见）极为有利，扣除我的佣金及各项花销之后，为 900,000（即九十万）塞斯特斯（sesterce），按罗马币值计；计开：尊贵的四列桨游船「阿佛洛狄忒」号连同三百名桨手（奴隶）；计开：一切必要而最完备的船具；计开：贵重的家具、地毯、银器和艺术珍藏——这些若能分开出售，本可卖得大钱，而不致有被以仁慈的提比略·奥古斯都陛下名义查抄之虞；但在前述情形下分开出售，势必滋生无数弊端；

「而小人，马里亚巴的卡莱布，以尊贵四列桨游船「阿佛洛狄忒」号实际主人之身份，以本人名义行事出面，已将这艘壮丽的游乐船售与尊贵的巴阿巴布老爷——一位驻节苏萨（Susa）的波斯总督；

===Original===
“Wishing, my noble Lord Lucius, companion of the chase and friend, to act to your ever noble advantage, I propose, in order to reduce to a minimum all risks of shipwreck and piracy and other fatal accidents (such as a jealous fate keeps ever hanging over the heads of us poor mortals), to remit to you at frequent intervals, by the intermediary of a master of merchant-vessels, one trusted by all and personally known to myself and sailing to and fro between Alexandria and the Archipelago, a small sum in gold coin or bar, with account and settlement, so that you, my noble lord, may at the earliest moment possess your whole capital in your own hands.

“And I also send you, in memory of your most beautiful ship, a few pieces of furniture and valuables (withheld and smuggled away), including two bronze bedsteads, a citron-wood table, a Babylonian carpet, some Ethiopian ostrich-feather coverings, the silver statue of Aphrodite, patroness of your ship, and a few minor trifles of taste and convenience.

===Chinese===
「区区愿为永远尊贵的卢基乌斯老爷、围猎的伙伴与朋友，谋永远的尊贵利益；为将沉船、海盗及其他致命横祸（妒忌的命运始终悬在我们可怜凡人头顶的那些祸事）的风险减至最小，我提议经由一位商船船长——众望所孚、与我素识、常年在亚历山大里亚与群岛海（Archipelago）之间往来——不时向您汇寄小额的金币或金条，随附账目与结单，以便老爷您尽早将全部本钱握在自己手中。

「为纪念您那艘最美的船，我另奉上几件家具与珍物（私自留下、偷运出来的），计有：青铜床两张、香橼木桌一张、巴比伦地毯一条、埃塞俄比亚鸵毛被数床、贵船守护神阿佛洛狄忒的银像一尊，另有若干雅致实用的小玩意儿。

===Original===
“I take advantage of the opportunity, my noble lord, dear friend and stout companion of the chase, to tell you that at Mariaba, during a temporary stay in my beloved country, Sabaea, I bought myself a most beautiful slave, a Greek like Cora and excelling in many gifts, which slave, in order to bind her with greater certainty to my affection, I set free at Mariaba, leaving her, however, as my wife in my house in that city, where I hope one day, after achieving my fortune, to enjoy a life of bliss, fearing lest my brother Ghizla might allow his rights on her to prevail (according to the manners of our country) and hoping to visit her each time that my conduct of noble lords shall bring me to the pillars of Sesostris, from which my beloved country is easily reached.

“Wishing you, my noble lord and friend and companion of the chase, the blessing of the gods upon your house, upon your new work and your household, upon your wife and your servants, the wise Thrasyllus and the faithful Tarrar, together with an always possible change of fortune, I bow low before you, in all humility and friendship, with one hand on my heart and one hand on my lips.

“Your ever most obedient, humble servant and guide and friend and companion of the chase, 

===Chinese===
「趁此机会，我尊贵的老爷、亲爱的朋友与围猎的可靠伙伴，不妨奉告：在马里亚巴——在我亲爱的祖国萨巴小住期间——我为自己买下了一名极美的女奴，一个像科拉一样的希腊女子，多才多艺；为使她更牢地系于我的情爱，我已在马里巴给她自由，但仍留她作我妻室，住在我在那座城里的宅中；我指望有朝一日发了财，在那里享尽幸福；只怕我的兄弟吉兹拉会依我们国俗，主张他对她的权利；并指望每次率领贵主们到塞索斯特里斯石柱时——从那里去我亲爱的祖国甚是便易——都能去探望她。

「祝愿我尊贵的老爷、朋友兼围猎伙伴：愿诸神降福于您的宅邸、您的新事业和您的家人、您的妻子和您的仆人们——贤明的特拉叙卢斯与忠实的塔拉尔——并愿您时来运转、否极泰来；小人谨以极度的谦卑与友谊，一手抚胸、一手抚唇，向老爷深深一躬。

「您永远最恭顺、最卑微的仆人、向导、朋友兼围猎伙伴，

===Original===
 “**Caleb of Mariaba (Saba)**, joint proprietor of the Great Diversorium, the Hermes House, at Alexandria, near the Canopian Gate.”

===Chinese===
「**马里亚巴（萨巴）的卡莱布**，亚历山大里亚卡诺皮斯门附近大客栈「赫尔墨斯之家」的合伙人。」

## V / 五

===Original===
Night had fallen over the city, a dark, starless night. To escape attention, Lucius and Caleb mounted a small, inconspicuous litter at the back of the diversorium. Caleb sat at Lucius’ feet with his legs dangling out of the litter, which was lifted by four powerful Libyans, in preparation for departing at a trot.

“Have you your dagger, my lord?” asked Caleb.

Yes, Lucius had a dagger in his girdle.

“And are you wearing your Sabaean amulets?”

Yes, Lucius had hung the amulets which he had bought round his neck, for Caleb was full of confidence in these talismans of his country: the amulets warded off all ill-luck; Caleb himself wore amulets everywhere, on his chest and round his waist and even on a narrow gold bangle round his ankle.

===Chinese===
夜幕降临在城上，一个漆黑无星的夜。为了避人耳目，卢基乌斯和卡莱布在客栈后头登上一乘不起眼的小肩舆。卡莱布坐在卢基乌斯脚边，两条腿垂在舆外；四名强壮的利比亚人抬起肩舆，准备起步小跑。

“老爷带匕首了吗？”卡莱布问。

带了，卢基乌斯腰带上插着匕首。

“还有，您戴上萨巴护身符了吗？”

戴了，卢基乌斯把买来的护身符挂在了脖子上，因为卡莱布对他祖国的这些灵符信心十足：护身符能挡住一切厄运；卡莱布自己浑身上下都戴着护身符，胸口、腰间，连脚踝上一条窄窄的金镯上也挂着。

===Original===
The bearers scurried through Bruchium and past the Gymnasium and the Museum, as though they had an enemy at their heels. They came to a square that lay higher than the Great Harbour; and Lucius looked out across the quays at the different harbours. Red and green and yellow lights and signals shone over a variegated, patched throng of ships and boats and swarming people. But the wonder to Lucius’ eyes was the lighthouse of Pharos. The nine storeys of the tall marble monument, stacked one on top of the other like so many cubes, each cube smaller than the one below, ended in a sort of cupola, where a heap of burning coal gleamed from immense mirrors and reflectors, which turned and turned continually, sending bright, broad rays from the summit of the tower upon the harbours, which they lit up each time, before stretching into the dark night. Sometimes the wide sheaves of light struck the high marble bridge of the Heptastadium, which led to the lighthouse itself and which at this hour was crowded with women and idlers.

“My lord,” whispered Caleb, “would you not like to get out … and walk … *there*? The loveliest women in Alexandria are strolling yonder … and you can take your choice.”

Lucius shook his head:

“I want to go to the sibyl,” he said.

“Your lordship is sick,” said Caleb. “Your lordship is sick with longing and useless pining. The lovely women of Alexandria would cure your lordship. They have often cured *me*, my lord, when I was sick with longing and pining.”

“Longing and pining for what, Caleb?”

===Chinese===
轿夫们一路疾奔，穿过布鲁基乌姆，经过体育馆和博学园，仿佛身后有仇家追赶。他们来到一处比大港地势高的广场；卢基乌斯隔着码头眺望那几处港口。红的绿的黄的灯火与信号，照着五色斑驳、密密匝匝、补丁似的舟船人群。但卢基乌斯眼里的奇观是法罗斯灯塔。九层高的大理石巨筑像积木一样一层摞一层，每层比底下的小，顶端收成一圈穹顶；穹顶里一堆燃烧的煤炭在巨大的镜面与反光镜中生辉——镜面不住地旋转，从塔顶把宽阔明亮的光束扫向各个港口，每照亮一处，便又伸展进黑夜。有时大团大团的光束扫上赫普塔斯塔迪翁高耸的大理石长堤——长堤通向灯塔本身，这个时辰上头正挤满了女人和闲人。

“老爷，”卡莱布低声说，“您不想下舆……走一走……去*那边*？亚历山大里亚最美的女人们正在那儿散步呢……您只管挑。”

卢基乌斯摇了摇头：

“我要去女巫那儿，”他说。

“老爷是病了，”卡莱布说。老爷害的是渴念，是无谓的憔悴。亚历山大里亚的美人能治好老爷的病。她们就常治好*我*，老爷，在我害渴念、犯憔悴的时候。

“渴念、憔悴，为了什么，卡莱布？”

===Original===
“For my country, for Saba, my lord, for Saba, the fairest and dearest country in the world, my lord, which I have had to leave … for the sake of business, my lord, for the sake of business. For we do no business in Saba.”

The four bearers trotted on. They were now trotting past the immemorial temple of Serapis, the Serapeum: sombre and grey it lay with its terraces below the Acropolis; and numbers of other shrines, also sombre, grey and mysterious, were ranged, with the needles of their obelisks, around the vast temple.

“These shrines are deserted, my lord,” said Caleb, “and no longer find worshippers. Even the Serapeum is deserted … for the temple of Serapis at Canopus. And the modern Alexandrians hold all this sacred quarter in but slight esteem since the quinquennial games were instituted at Nicopolis. All those who wish to do honour to Serapis repair to Nicopolis and Canopus. We will go there too, my lord, and you shall dream dreams full of import high up, on the roof of the temple. … Look, my lord, here we are, at Rhacotis. …”

The trotting bearers had left the aristocratic quarters. They were now hurrying through a narrower, sombre street.

“We had better get out here, my lord, and walk,” said Caleb. “We shall find our litter here when we return.”

===Chinese===
“为了我的家乡，为了萨巴，老爷，为了萨巴，全世界最美、最亲的国家，老爷，我却不得不离开它……为了生意，老爷，为了生意。因为我们萨巴是不做生意的。”

四名轿夫继续小跑。这时他们正跑过那座古老的萨拉皮斯神庙——萨拉皮雍：它带着层层台地，晦暗灰暗地卧在卫城下面；另有许多神祠，同样晦暗、灰暗而神秘，带着方尖碑的尖顶，环卫着这座大庙。

“这些神祠都荒了，老爷，”卡莱布说，“再也等不来信众了。连萨拉皮雍都荒了……萨拉皮斯的信众都去了卡诺珀斯的神庙。自打尼科波利斯设立了五年一届的赛会，如今的亚历山大里亚人就把这整片圣区看得平平。凡想敬奉萨拉皮斯的，都赶尼科波利斯和卡诺珀斯去。咱们也去那儿，老爷，让您高高地、在神庙的屋顶上做意味深长的梦。……瞧，老爷，到了，拉科提斯。……” 

小跑的轿夫们已经离开富贵街区，这时正奔进一条更窄、更暗的街。

“咱们最好就在这儿下舆步行，老爷，”卡莱布说。“回来时，肩舆就在这儿等。”

===Original===
Lucius and Caleb alighted. The sombre street was hardly lit, but was nevertheless swarming with people, including drunken sailors and fighting beldames.

“It’s very different here, my lord, from the Heptastadium and Lake Mareotis. Here the people, soldiers and sailors take their pleasure. Here a dagger is drawn as quick as thought. Here is nothing but kennels and taverns. But every traveller who wants to know Alexandria comes here. … Look, my lord, here it is,” said Caleb, “here!”

They had gone through a network of little lanes and alleys and come to a square. At one corner an old, ragged philosopher stood arguing and expounding. Around him soldiers, sailors and wenches gathered, listening attentively to what he said of true wisdom. When he put out his hand for alms, two soldiers gave him some coppers, but the others laughed and pelted him with rotten vegetables. He fled and disappeared, pursued by yelping dogs that bit him in the skirt of his torn toga.

“Will you not see the Syrian boys dance, my lord?” asked Caleb. “They dance so beautifully.”

“No, I want to go to the sibyl,” Lucius answered, impatiently.

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯和卡莱布下了舆。暗街上几乎没有什么灯火，却照旧人头攒动，有酩酊的水手，也有扭打的悍妇。

“这儿跟赫普塔斯塔迪翁和马雷奥蒂斯湖边可大不一样，老爷。这儿是百姓、大兵和水手取乐的地方。这儿拔刀比念头还快。这儿除了狗窝就是酒肆。可想见识亚历山大里亚的客人，谁都得来一趟。……瞧，老爷，到了，”卡莱布说，“到了！”

他们穿过蛛网似的小巷窄弄，来到一个广场。一角上站着个衣衫褴褛的老哲学家，正自论辩讲学。士兵、水手和粉头围着他，专心听他讲什么是真正的智慧。他伸手讨钱，两个大兵给了几个铜子儿，其余的人却哄笑着拿烂菜叶砸他。他逃走了，一转眼就不见了，身后一群狗狂吠着追咬他破托加的衣角。

“您不想看看叙利亚男孩跳舞吗，老爷？”卡莱布问。“他们跳得可美了。”

“不看，我要去女巫那儿，”卢基乌斯不耐烦地答道。

===Original===
“We are close to her dwelling, my lord,” Caleb declared.

They almost fought their way through the crowd. The men cursed them because they pushed and the women flung themselves round their necks. Caleb drew his dagger and raised it threateningly. Other knives were drawn forthwith. There was a demoniacal yelling and din. But they succeeded in avoiding bloodshed.

“I want to go to the sibyl,” Lucius repeated, panting and with his clenched fists pushing away two women who were hanging on to his arms.

Lucius and Caleb now hurried through a reek of wine past the open brothels and reeking taverns. Caleb stopped in front of a small, narrow door and knocked. It was opened by a little Greek girl, pretty and delicate as a Tanagra figurine, with very large black eyes.

“Is Herophila within?” asked Caleb. “A distinguished foreigner wishes to consult her.”

===Chinese===
“我们就快到她的住处了，老爷，”卡莱布断言。

他们几乎是打出一条路穿过人群。男人们骂他们乱挤，女人们扑上他们的脖颈。卡莱布拔出匕首，威胁地举起。别的刀子也跟着出鞘。一阵魔鬼似的喊叫喧闹。但他们总算躲过了流血。

“我要去女巫那儿，”卢基乌斯又说一遍，喘着气，用攥紧的拳头推开两个吊在他胳膊上的女人。

卢基乌斯和卡莱布这时在一股酒气里匆匆走过敞着门的妓院和酸气冲天的酒肆。卡莱布在一扇窄小门前停住，敲了敲。开门的是个小小的希腊姑娘，像塔纳格拉（Tanagra）小陶俑一样秀气娇美，一双极大的黑眼睛。

“赫罗菲拉（Herophila）在家吗？”卡莱布问。“有位显贵的外邦人要请教她。”

===Original===
“I will tell her,” said the girl.

They entered a very narrow little chamber. A woman came from behind a curtain. She was shrouded in a white veil, like a phantom; she carried an earthenware lamp; and it was not possible to see if she was young or old.

“Do you wish to know the future?” she asked, in a hollow voice.

“No,” said Lucius, “I want to know the past and the present. I want to know where a girl named Ilia is and how she vanished from my house. Here is the sandal which she left behind: the only trace of her. If she … is dead, can you make her appear before me, so that I may ask her?”

===Chinese===
“我去通报，”姑娘说。

他们走进一间极窄的小屋。一个妇人从帘后出来。她裹着白面纱，像个幽灵；手里端着一盏陶灯；看不出她是年轻还是年老。

“你想知道未来吗？”她用一种空洞的嗓音问。

“不，”卢基乌斯说，“我想知道过去和现在。我想知道一个叫伊利娅的姑娘在哪里，她怎样从我家消失。这是她留下的凉鞋：她唯一的痕迹。如果她……死了，你能让她显形在我面前吗？好让我问她？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said the sibyl, “I can. For I am descended from the witch of En-dor.”

“Who was she?” asked Lucius.

“The witch who made Samuel appear before Saul. …”

“I never heard of them,” said Lucius.

“And another of my forbears was my honoured namesake, Herophila of Erythrae.”

“Who was she?” asked Lucius.

===Chinese===
“能，”女巫说，“我能。因为我就是隐多珥（En-dor）女巫的后人。”

“她是谁？”卢基乌斯问。

“就是让撒母耳（Samuel）显形在扫罗（Saul）面前的那个女巫。……” 

“我从没听说过他们，”卢基乌斯说。

“我还有一位先祖，是我敬重的同名者，埃里特赖（Erythrae）的赫罗菲拉。”

“她是谁？”卢基乌斯问。

===Original===
“She was the custodian of the shrine of Apollo Smintheus, the divine rat-killer. She prophesied to Hecuba the calamity which would cause the death of her son Paris, whom she was bearing in her womb.”

“I never heard of her before,” Lucius repeated. “Tell me if Ilia is dead.”

The sibyl pressed the blue-leather sandal to her head; and her other hand pressed Lucius’ forehead.

“She is *not* dead!” she cried, in a voice of rapture.

“She is *not* dead?”

===Chinese===
“她是史明提乌斯·阿波罗——神圣的灭鼠神——神龛的守祠人。她向赫库芭（Hecuba）预言了那场灾祸，那灾祸将害死她怀在腹中的儿子帕里斯（Paris）。”

“我也从没听说过她，”卢基乌斯又说。“告诉我，伊利娅是不是死了。”

女巫把蓝皮凉鞋贴在自己头上；另一只手按住卢基乌斯的前额。

“她*没有*死！”她狂喜地喊道。

“她*没有*死？”

===Original===
“No, Ilia lives!”

“Where? Where is she?”

The sibyl, in a trance, muttered incomprehensible sounds:

“She appears … she appears,” she stammered, at length.

Suddenly, behind her, the curtains parted. There was nothing there but a smoking tripod. Thick fumes filled the apartment and rolled on high like a heavy curtain.

===Chinese===
“没有，伊利娅活着！”

“在哪里？她在哪里？”

女巫入了迷狂，咕哝出叫人听不懂的声音：

“她显形了……她显形了，”她终于结结巴巴地说。

突然，她身后的帘幕分开了。后面什么也没有，只有一座冒着烟的三脚炉。浓烟灌满小屋，像一幅沉重的帷幕高高卷起。

===Original===
“She appears … she appears,” the sibyl went on stammering.

Lucius stared breathlessly.

Suddenly, in the fumes, a figure was vaguely outlined as of a dainty woman, flimsy and thin, a shade that moved to and fro.

“I see her!” cried Lucius. “Ilia, Ilia! Speak one word to me! Come back to me! I cannot live without you!”

The vision had vanished. The smoke clouded away. The curtains closed again.

===Chinese===
“她显形了……她显形了，”女巫继续结结巴巴地说。

卢基乌斯屏住呼吸，凝目而望。

突然，烟雾里隐约显出一个身形，像是个纤巧的女人，轻薄缥缈，一个来回移动的影。

“我看见她了！”卢基乌斯喊道。“伊利娅，伊利娅！对我说一句话！回到我身边来！没有你我活不下去！”

幻影消失了。烟雾散去。帘幕又合上了。

===Original===
“It is difficult,” said the sibyl, faintly, “to hold the astral bodies of living persons for more than a single moment. I can summon the dead for you for a longer time. But Ilia is not dead.”

“Then where is she?” cried Lucius.

The sibyl now pressed the sandal to her forehead and her other hand lay on Lucius’ head:

“I see her,” said the sibyl. “She is lying in a boat, swooning. … The sea is raging. … Now rough, bearded men are hurrying her away. …”

“She is kidnapped!” cried Lucius. “By pirates?”

===Chinese===
“很难，”女巫有气无力地说，“很难让活人的星魂停留一瞬以上。我能为你把死者召来，留得久些。可伊利娅没有死。”

“那她在哪里？”卢基乌斯喊道。

女巫这时把凉鞋贴上额头，另一只手搭在卢基乌斯头上：

“我看见她了，”女巫说。“她躺在一条船上，昏晕着。……海在发怒。……这会儿，满脸胡子的粗鲁男人正匆匆把她带走。……” 

“她是被劫走的！”卢基乌斯喊道。“被海盗？”

===Original===
“Yes!” cried the sibyl and fell into a faint.

The pretty Greek girl appeared and said:

“The fee is half a ptolemy, in gold. …”

Caleb paid.

Lucius looked down in despair upon the swooning sibyl.

===Chinese===
“是！”女巫喊了一声，晕了过去。

美丽的希腊姑娘现身出来说：

“谢金是半个托勒密（ptolemy），要金的。……” 

卡莱布付了钱。

卢基乌斯绝望地低头望着晕厥的女巫。

===Original===
“Tomorrow night, my lord,” said the Greek girl, in a singsong voice, “Herophila will be able to tell you more … where Ilia was taken by the pirates.”

But Lucius clenched his fists; he foamed at the mouth with sudden anger and roared:

“She has merely read my own thoughts! No more! No more!”

He glared round him like a madman, drew his dagger and made as though to fling himself upon the sibyl’s swooning body.

“My lord! My lord!” shouted Caleb, holding him back and gripping him in his strong arms.

The Greek girl, standing in front of the fainting woman, spread wide her arms and cried:

“Do not murder a holy woman, my lord! Do not murder a poor, holy woman!”

And, as she stood thus, Lucius saw that she was like the shade of Ilia … and he burst into sobs.

===Chinese===
“明晚，老爷，”希腊姑娘拉着调子说，“赫罗菲拉就能告诉您更多……伊利娅被海盗带到了哪里。”

可卢基乌斯攥紧了拳头；他口喷白沫，勃然大怒，吼道：

“她不过是读了我自己的心事！不要再说了！不要再说了！”

他像疯子一样环视四周，拔出匕首，做出要扑向女巫晕厥之身的样子。

“老爷！老爷！”卡莱布喊着，拦住他，用强壮的胳膊死死抱住他。

希腊姑娘挡在昏倒的女人前面，张开双臂喊道：

“不要杀害圣洁的女人，老爷！不要杀害一个可怜的、圣洁的女人！”

她这样站着，卢基乌斯忽然看见，她像极了伊利娅的影子……他放声痛哭起来。

## VI / 六

===Original===
Those were sad days. Lucius would lie on his bed, sobbing like a child, then rise suddenly, in transports of rage, tear his clothes or take up a stool and hurl it at a marble statue, which fell down in dust and fragments. He showed Thrasyllus the door; and Uncle Catullus kept out of the way. Lucius had ended by banging Tarrar against a table; and the little slave had a deep wound in his forehead. Caleb, who was a good hand at doctoring, had himself bandaged Tarrar’s head.

Anxiously, in the palm-garden, the travelling merchants whispered about the wealthy Roman, who was sick with sorrow, and Uncle Catullus whispered in their company. Thrasyllus consoled himself by visiting the libraries of the Museum and the Serapeum. Lucius refused to hear any music. He did not leave his bed. He did not eat. He did not sleep. He looked unshaven, lean-cheeked and hollow-eyed, as one who was desperately sick.

They were sad days. The first charm of Alexandria was past; and Lucius cursed his journey, his whole life and everybody. In his impotent pain he groaned, sobbed and raved. Master Ghizla ordained silence and quiet around his rooms. Not a sandal creaked, not a voice sounded.

Lucius listened to this stillness. It was after luncheon, which Uncle Catullus had taken alone with Thrasyllus. And in the burning sunny stillness of that glowing June Lucius suddenly heard a child sobbing.

===Chinese===
那是些悲惨的日子。卢基乌斯时而躺在床上，像个孩子似的抽泣，时而又霍然起身，一阵狂怒，撕破衣裳，或抓起一张凳子朝大理石雕像掷去，雕像颓然碎作尘埃瓦砾。他给特拉叙卢斯吃过闭门羹；卡图卢斯叔父躲得远远的。到后来，卢基乌斯把塔拉尔往桌子上撞了一下；小奴隶额头上磕开一道深口子。卡莱布颇通医道，亲手给塔拉尔包扎了头。

棕榈园里，行商们忧心忡忡地低声议论着这位害着悲伤之病的阔罗马人；卡图卢斯叔父夹在他们中间窃窃私语。特拉叙卢斯则去博学园和萨拉皮雍的图书馆里自遣。卢基乌斯拒绝听任何音乐。他不下床。他不吃饭。他不睡觉。他满面胡茬，两颊凹陷，眼窝深陷，像个病得没了指望的人。

那些日子真悲惨。亚历山大里亚最初的新鲜劲儿过去了；卢基乌斯咒骂他的旅行、他的一生和所有人。他在无可奈何的疼痛中呻吟、抽泣、胡言乱语。吉兹拉老板下令在他房间四周保持肃静安稳。一声草鞋响也不闻，一声人语也不闻。

卢基乌斯听着这份寂静。那是午饭之后，卡图卢斯叔父单独同特拉叙卢斯一起用的午饭。在那个烈日灼灼的六月，一片灼热的寂静中，卢基乌斯忽然听见一个孩子在抽泣。

===Original===
He rose from his couch. The sobs came from the back-garden; and Lucius raised the curtain and looked out. There, listening for his master’s gong, sat Tarrar, huddled, like a little monkey, in a gaudy coat. He wore a napkin round his head as a bandage. And he was weeping, with little sobs, as if he were in great sorrow.

“Tarrar!” cried Lucius.

The little slave started up:

“My lord!” he answered.

And he rose and approached with comical reverence and sobbed.

“Tarrar,” said Lucius, “why are you weeping? Are you in pain?”

“No, my lord,” said Tarrar. “I beg pardon, my lord, for weeping. I must not weep in your august presence. I humbly beg your pardon, my lord. But I am weeping because … because … because I am so unhappy.”

===Chinese===
他从卧榻上起身。哭声从后园传来；卢基乌斯掀起帘子往外看。塔拉尔正蹲在那儿，候着听主人的锣声，蜷成一团，像只穿着艳丽短衣的小猴子。他头上蒙着一块布巾当绷带。他在哭，小声地抽噎，像是伤心极了。

“塔拉尔！”卢基乌斯喊道。

小奴隶一惊跳起：

“老爷！”他应道。

他站起来，滑稽地恭恭敬敬走上前，一面走一面抽噎。

“塔拉尔，”卢基乌斯说，“你哭什么？疼吗？”

“不疼，老爷，”塔拉尔说。“求您饶恕，老爷，我不该哭。我不该在您尊贵的驾前哭。小人叩请老爷饶恕。可我哭，是因为……因为……因为我太伤心了。”

===Original===
“And why are you unhappy? Because I struck you? Because you are in pain? Because you fell and made a hole in your head?”

“No, my lord,” said Tarrar, trying to control himself, “not because you struck me. I am your little slave, my lord, and you have the right to strike me. And also not because I am in pain: there is only a little burning pain now, for Caleb bandaged my head this morning with cool ointment. The hole is not so very deep either; and, when it is healed, the scar will remind me that I belong to you, my lord, and that I am your little slave.”

“But then why are you weeping, Tarrar, and why are you unhappy?”

“I am weeping, my lord,” Tarrar began, “because. …”

And then he could restrain himself no longer, comical, respectful little monkey that he was, and sobbed aloud.

===Chinese===
“那你为什么伤心？因为我打了你？因为你疼？因为你摔了一跤，头上磕出个窟窿？”

“不是，老爷，”塔拉尔竭力忍着说，“不是因为您打了我。我是您的小奴隶，老爷，您有权打我。也不是因为我疼：现在只有一点点火烧似的疼，因为卡莱布今天早上用凉药膏给我包扎了头。窟窿也不很深；等它长好了，疤会提醒我，我属于您，老爷，我是您的小奴隶。”

“那你到底为什么哭呢，塔拉尔，为什么伤心？”

“我哭，老爷，”塔拉尔开口道，“是因为。……” 

于是他再也忍不住了——他就是那只滑稽而恭顺的小猴子——放声抽噎起来。

===Original===
Lucius laid his hand on the boy’s curly head:

“Why are you weeping, child?”

“Because the snakes wouldn’t dance any more!” sobbed Tarrar, in despair. “Because one of them is dead and the other gone, for it crept out of its skin and left its skin behind! Because, whatever pains I took to pipe the magic tune on the flute—in the garden behind the house, so as not to make a noise or disturb you—the snakes would *not* dance any more … as they did when the merchant piped to them! And because now … one of the snakes is dead, my lord, and the other crept away out of its skin!”

And Tarrar, overcome with misery, sobbed aloud and showed his master the snake and the ebony casket, from which a skin hung, with a square piece of glass gummed to the head.

Lucius gave a melancholy smile. Was he not himself miserable, like Tarrar, because he too had been robbed of his plaything? And he said:

“Come with me, Tarrar.”

And he took the little slave by the hand and led him to his room.

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯把手放在男孩鬈发的头上：

“你哭什么，孩子？”

“因为蛇不肯再跳舞了！”塔拉尔绝望地抽噎着。“因为一条死了，另一条跑了，它从自己的皮里爬出来，把皮留下了！因为不管我多费劲地在笛子上吹那支魔曲——在后园里吹，为了不出声、不打扰您——蛇就是*不肯*再跳舞了……不像商人吹的时候那样！还因为现在……一条蛇死了，老爷，另一条从自己的皮里爬走了！” 

塔拉尔伤心透顶，放声抽噎，把蛇和乌木匣拿给主人看；匣子上搭着一张蜕下的蛇皮，皮头上还粘着一块方玻璃。

卢基乌斯露出一个忧郁的微笑。他自己不也同塔拉尔一样可怜吗，因为他的玩物也被夺走了？于是他说：

“跟我来，塔拉尔。”

他牵起小奴隶的手，把他领进自己的房间。

===Original===
He sat down, with Tarrar standing in front of him. Then he said:

“Tarrar, I am sorry for hurting you so badly. Forgive me, Tarrar.”

But Tarrar shook his head:

“It is not for me to forgive you, my lord,” he said, earnestly, with great, dim eyes. “You are the master.”

“Tarrar,” Lucius continued, “when we are back in Rome, you shall be free. I will set you free. And you shall no longer be a little slave. But you shall go to school, to the freedmen’s college. And learn all sorts of things. And become very clever, like Thrasyllus. And I will give you money. And you will be able to do whatever you please.”

Tarrar was a little taken aback:

“You are very kind, my lord,” he said. “But, if I go to school, who will fold your clothes? And listen for your gong? You are not driving me away, my lord, because I was so unhappy? I would rather stay with you, my lord, I would rather remain your little slave … and I will never again be so disrespectful as to weep. … I would rather remain your stupid little slave.”

===Chinese===
他坐下，塔拉尔站在他面前。随后他说：

“塔拉尔，把你伤得那么重，我很过意不去。原谅我，塔拉尔。”

塔拉尔却摇了摇头：

“轮不到我来原谅您，老爷，”他一本正经地说，一双朦胧的大眼睛望着人。“您是主人。”

“塔拉尔，”卢基乌斯接着说，“等我们回到罗马，你就自由了。我要放你自由。你就不再是小奴隶了。你要去上学，上获释奴的学校。学各种各样的东西。变得非常聪明，像特拉叙卢斯那样。我还要给你钱。你想做什么就能做什么。”

塔拉尔有点儿发懵：

“您太好了，老爷，”他说。“可要是我去上学，谁替您叠衣裳？谁听您的锣声？您不是要赶我走吧，老爷，因为我太伤心了？我宁愿留在您身边，老爷，我宁愿还做您的小奴隶……我再也不敢放肆地哭了。……我宁愿还做您那只笨笨的小奴隶。”

===Original===
“You shall be free, Tarrar. But you will be allowed to serve me all the same.”

“I don’t want to be free, my lord. What use is freedom to me? I am your little slave. I should always be your little slave just as before.”

“Then ask me something else, Tarrar, something that you would like very much.”

Tarrar grinned with his white teeth through his tears:

“May I tell you, my lord?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“你会自由的，塔拉尔。但你照样可以侍候我。”

“我不想自由，老爷。自由对我有什么用？我是您的小奴隶。我还是要跟从前一样，永远做您的小奴隶。”

“那你就向我要点别的吧，塔拉尔，要一件你特别喜欢的东西。”

塔拉尔含着泪咧出满口白牙：

“我能说吗，老爷？”

“说吧。”

===Original===
Tarrar hesitated. Then he said, shyly:

“Two other little dancing snakes, my lord.”

Lucius laughed softly:

“Child!” he said. “I will give you two other little snakes! But I fear that those also will refuse to dance as only the Indian merchant can make them dance.”

“I fear so too,” said Tarrar, reflecting. “The snake that is still alive has crept back to the merchant, I expect, out of the skin which it left behind it. I also fear that the new snakes would refuse to dance. … Then I would rather have nothing, my lord. I don’t want anything. If only I may serve you.”

“Then get everything ready to shave me … and tell the slaves to prepare the bath.”

“Yes, my lord,” said Tarrar, with alacrity.

===Chinese===
塔拉尔迟疑了一下。然后他羞答答地说：

“再要两条会跳舞的小蛇，老爷。”

卢基乌斯轻轻地笑了：

“孩子！”他说。“我再给你两条小蛇！不过我怕它们也不肯跳，只有那个印度商人才逗得它们跳。”

“我也怕是这样，”塔拉尔若有所思地说。“那条还活着的蛇，我猜是顺着它留下的皮，爬回商人那儿去了。我也怕新蛇不肯跳。……那我宁愿什么都不要，老爷。我什么都不要。只要让我侍候您。”

“那你去把替我剃须的东西都备好……再告诉奴隶们准备浴水。”

“是，老爷！”塔拉尔爽快利落地应道。

## VII / 七

===Original===
> “Mother of Eros, hear thy slave!
> “Child of the foam, great goddess of love,

 Aphrodite, look down from above!

 Thou, who dost madden the gods with desire,

 Thou, who fulfillest men’s hearts with thy fire,

 All but the heart of my lord that I crave,

 Hark to thy slave!

> “Spill this hot blood that courses in vain for him,

 Darken these eyes that are heavy with pain for him,

 Smite the parched lips that he sees but to spurn them,

 The hands stretched in love … take them, break them and burn them!

> “Then, in the place where lately he strode,

 Mingle mine ash with the dust of the road;

 Thus, though I win not a glance from his eye,

 Thus, though as ever he pass me by

 Careless, unseeing, at least my lord’s heel

 Cannot but touch me, at least I shall feel

 The embrace of his foot; and his sandall’d sole

 Shall kiss my dust and make me whole.

> “Then let the heart that he has press’d,

 The ashen lips by him caressed

 Sink low in the lowly dust of the road

 Lest another tread where late he trod.

> “Mother of Eros, hear thy slave!

 “Child of the foam, great goddess of love,

 Aphrodite, look down from above!

 Thou, who dost madden the gods with desire,

 Thou, who fulfillest men’s hearts with thy fire,

 All but the heart of my lord that I crave,

 Hark to thy slave!”

===Chinese===
> “厄洛斯之母，垂听你的女奴！
> “泡沫之女，伟大的爱之女神，

 阿佛洛狄忒，请自高处俯瞰！

 你以欲望使众神癫狂，

 你以烈火填满世人的心房，

 唯独我渴念的夫君之心不得，

 垂听你的女奴！

> “放尽这腔为他徒然奔涌的热血，

 黯淡这双为他痛得沉重的眼睛，

 击打这双他一见便弃绝的焦唇，

 这双因爱伸出的手……拿去，折断它们，把它们烧尽！

> “然后，在他新近阔步的地方，

 把我的骨灰拌进路的尘土；

 这样，纵然我赢不得他一瞥，

 纵然他照旧从我身旁走过，

 漠然不顾，至少我夫君的脚跟

 不能不触到我，至少我还能感到

 他一足的拥抱；他穿凉鞋的脚底

 将亲吻我的尘土，使我完满。

> “那就让被他紧贴过的心，

 让被他爱抚过的灰白之唇，

 低低沉入路旁卑微的尘土，

 免得旁人踏上他新踏过的地方。

> “厄洛斯之母，垂听你的女奴！

 “泡沫之女，伟大的爱之女神，

 阿佛洛狄忒，请自高处俯瞰！

 你以欲望使众神癫狂，

 你以烈火填满世人的心房，

 唯独我渴念的夫君之心不得，

 垂听你的女奴！”

===Original===
Cora’s song rang through the falling night. Her clear voice, tinkling as though with little golden bells, at first soft and hushed, rose throbbing in passion and then broke like a crystal ray and melted in mournfulness and plaintive prayer.

The shadows lay heaped under the palm-trees. Outside the doors of their apartments, in the galleries of the diversorium, sat the travelling merchants, squatting or lying on mat or rug, listening. Uncle Catullus lay in a hammock and Thrasyllus sat beside him and looked up at the stars, which were beginning to show like silver daisies in wide, blue meadows.

“You have sung beautifully, Cora,” said Uncle Catullus to the slave, who was sitting on the ground with the four-stringed harp before her.

“Thank you, my lord,” said the slave.

“Why not call me uncle?” said Catullus, good-naturedly.

===Chinese===
科拉的歌声响彻渐沉的夜。她清亮的嗓音，像缀着一串小金铃，起初轻柔低抑，随即激情涌动地高扬，末了如一道水晶光折断，消融在哀伤与哀恳的祈祷里。

树影一堆堆卧在棕榈树下。行商们坐在客栈游廊里各自的房门外，蹲着，或躺在席子上毯子上，听着。卡图卢斯叔父躺在一张吊床里，特拉叙卢斯坐在他身旁，仰望星空——星星正开始显露，像辽阔蓝色草甸上的银色雏菊。

“你唱得美极了，科拉，”卡图卢斯叔父对那坐在地上、四弦竖琴摆在身前的女奴说。

“谢谢老爷，”女奴说。

“干吗不叫我叔父？”卡图卢斯和气地说。

===Original===
“I should not dare,” said Cora, smiling.

“Ilia used to call me uncle.”

“I am not Ilia, my lord.”

Tarrar appeared in the pillared portico.

But his appearance was a surprise. For Tarrar, no longer bandaged, looked like a little savage: he wore his Libyan festive garment; a girdle of feathers hung round his waist; he was crowned with a headdress of feathers. And he stood grinning.

===Chinese===
“我不敢，”科拉微笑着说。

“伊利娅从前就叫我叔父。”

“我不是伊利娅，老爷。”

塔拉尔出现在柱廊里。

可他这一露面叫人吃了一惊。塔拉尔不再包绷带了，看着竟像个小野人：他穿着利比亚的节日衣裳；腰间围一圈羽毛腰带；头上还顶着羽毛头饰。他咧着嘴站着笑。

===Original===
“Great gods, Tarrar!” cried Uncle Catullus, with a start. “What have you done to yourself? You look like a little cannibal! You frighten me! What’s happening?”

“We are going to Canopus, my lord, tonight!” cried Tarrar, jubilantly. “My Lord Lucius lets you know that we are all going to Canopus this very night! Here is his lordship himself!”

And Tarrar pointed triumphantly to Lucius, who appeared upon the threshold. Cora had risen and now curtseyed low to the ground, with outstretched arms.

Lucius looked like a young Egyptian god. He wore an Egyptian robe of striped byssus, with a border of hieroglyphics worked in heavy embroidery and precious stones; his legs were encased in hose of gold tissue; about his head was an Egyptian coif, like that of a sphinx, with broad, projecting, striped bands, which fell to his shoulders; he glittered with strange jewels and was wrapped from head to foot in fine gold net like a transparent cloak, like an immaterial shroud. And he approached with a smile, brilliantly, superhumanly beautiful.

===Chinese===
“伟大的众神啊，塔拉尔！”卡图卢斯叔父吓了一跳，喊道。“你把自己弄成什么样子了？你看着像个小食人番！吓我一跳！出什么事了？”

“我们今夜去卡诺珀斯，老爷！”塔拉尔欢天喜地地喊。“卢基乌斯老爷让我告诉您，我们今夜全体去卡诺珀斯！老爷他本人到啦！”

塔拉尔得意地指向卢基乌斯，卢基乌斯正跨进门来。科拉已经起身，此刻伸出双臂，深深地行了个及地屈膝礼。

卢基乌斯看着像一位年轻的埃及神。他穿着条纹细麻绢的埃及长袍，宽边上用粗重的刺绣和宝石绣出象形文字；双腿裹着金线织的胫衣；头上是一顶埃及头巾，像斯芬克斯戴的那种，宽大前伸的条纹带头层层叠叠，垂到肩头；他浑身闪着奇异的珠宝，从头到脚罩着一层细金网，像一件透明的斗篷，像一袭无形的殓衣。他含笑走上前来，光华灿烂，美得超凡入圣。

===Original===
“Great sacred gods! Great sacred gods!” exclaimed Uncle Catullus.

He rose; Thrasyllus rose too; and the merchants gathered round and, in salaam upon salaam, showed their admiration for the dazzling stranger.

“Lucius, what possesses you? What is happening? Have you turned into Serapis himself?”

“No, uncle,” smiled Lucius, “I am merely clad in ceremonial raiment because I want to go to Canopus and dream on the roof of the temple of Serapis. It is the great feast; and Caleb”—he pointed to Caleb stepping forward—“has persuaded me to go this night in state to Canopus. You are coming too, uncle; you also, Thrasyllus; we shall all go, all my freedmen and slaves. Caleb will see about a boat.”

A violent and feverish excitement followed. Slaves, male and female, streamed from every side of the diversorium, rejoicing and clasping their hands in amazement.

===Chinese===
“伟大的圣神啊！伟大的圣神啊！”卡图卢斯叔父惊呼。

他站起身；特拉叙卢斯也站起身；商人们围拢过来，一躬接一躬，向这位光华夺目的陌生人表达仰慕。

“卢基乌斯，你着了什么魔？这是怎么回事？你莫非变成萨拉皮斯本人了？”

“没有，叔父，”卢基乌斯微笑道，“我不过是穿上了礼服，因为我想到卡诺珀斯去，在萨拉皮斯神庙的屋顶上做梦。今天是盛大的节日；卡莱布”——他指了指走上前来的卡莱布——“劝我今夜摆开全副仪仗去卡诺珀斯。你也一起去，叔父；你也去，特拉叙卢斯；我们全体都去，我所有的获释奴和奴隶。卡莱布去安排船。”

接着是一阵狂热而亢奋的忙乱。男女奴隶从客栈四面八方涌来，又惊又喜，拍着手。

===Original===
“When any princely noble, such as his lordship,” Caleb explained, “goes to Canopus, to the feast of Serapis thrice holy, he goes in the greatest state, with all his household to accompany him.”

“So I am going too, as I belong to the household?” exclaimed Uncle Catullus. “Only … am I to rig myself out like that? And where shall I find such a sumptuous raiment?”

“My lord,” said Caleb, “you will find everything ready in your chamber. You too, Master Thrasyllus.”

Uncle Catullus hurried away, clasping his fat stomach in his two hands. You never knew where you were with that Lucius! For days and days he had been mourning and sobbing and lamenting; he had remained invisible and had eaten nothing … and there, there he appeared, decked out like a young god, and wanted to go to Canopus, to dream on the roof of the temple!

“And I had just been reckoning on a quiet evening, because I feel that I’ve overloaded my stomach!” moaned Uncle Catullus. “Egypt will be the death of me!”

===Chinese===
“凡王公贵人，像老爷这样的，”卡莱布解释道，“去卡诺珀斯赴三倍神圣的萨拉皮斯节，都要摆出最盛大的仪仗，带着全副家眷随行。”

“这么说我也去，算我是家眷的一份子？”卡图卢斯叔父喊道。“只是……我也得那样一身披挂吗？这样一身华服，我上哪儿找去？”

“老爷，”卡莱布说，“您屋里一切都备好了。您也是，特拉叙卢斯先生。”

卡图卢斯叔父双手捧着肥肚子，匆匆走了。跟这个卢基乌斯打交道，你永远摸不清状况！一连多少天，他哀恸、抽泣、悲叹；他谁也不见，滴水不进……可忽然，忽然他就这么打扮成一个年轻的神出现了，还要去卡诺珀斯，去神庙屋顶上做梦！

“我还正指望过一个安生晚上呢，只觉得我这肚子已经超载了！”卡图卢斯叔父哀叹道。“埃及早晚要我的命！”

===Original===
Lights everywhere, links and torches; fever and gaiety everywhere, because one and all were going to Canopus that night. What a surprise! Their lord was no longer sick! It was the great feast! It was the feast of Serapis! The feast of dreams! The water-festival and the boat-festival! It was the summer festival of Canopus!

Vettius and Rufus, the two stewards, gave orders here, there and everywhere. One and all, they said, were to deck themselves in festive garb. Ione, the old female slave, who had charge of the harpists and dancers, was given leave to buy from the merchants whatever she needed, veils and ornaments.

“We are going to Canopus, we are going to Canopus!” cried the women, in joyful chorus. “Quick, Ione, hand me the poppy-rouge! Here, a stick of antimony! I want a blue veil, Ione, and blue lotus-flowers for my hair! Quick, quick, Ione! The master is ready!”

===Chinese===
到处灯火，火把与火炬；到处是狂热与欢闹，因为这一夜人人都要去卡诺珀斯。多大的惊喜！他们的老爷病好了！这是盛大的节日！这是萨拉皮斯节！这是梦之节！水节与船节！这是卡诺珀斯的夏节！

两位管家维提乌斯和鲁弗斯四处发号施令。他们说，一律都要穿上节日盛装。伊俄涅（Ione），那管着竖琴女和舞女的老女奴，获准去向商人们买她需要的一切：面纱和饰物。

“我们去卡诺珀斯喽，我们去卡诺珀斯喽！”女人们欢欢喜喜地齐声喊。“快，伊俄涅，把罂粟红递给我！这儿，一根锑墨笔！我要蓝面纱，伊俄涅，还要蓝莲花插头发！快，快，伊俄涅！主人已经好了！”

===Original===
“We are going to Canopus, we are going to Canopus!” Cora cried, joyfully, with the rest. “My lord was like a young god, he looked like Serapis himself! Ione, I must have a net of gold thread and a dreaming-veil of gold thread and pink water-lilies for my hair! I want a wreath of pink water-lilies!”

Lucius from afar beheld this stir, in the reflection of the lamps and torches in the night. Slaves were running to and fro; litters were prepared. He thought only of Ilia. He wanted to wrap himself in the dreaming-veil and to lie on the temple-roof and dream where Ilia was, where she had been carried … by the pirates. And he stood like a priest, gazing solemnly before him.

===Chinese===
“我们去卡诺珀斯喽，我们去卡诺珀斯喽！”科拉也同大家一起欢喜地喊。“老爷像个年轻的神，他看着就是萨拉皮斯本人！伊俄涅，我一定要一张金线网，要一条金线的梦纱，要粉色睡莲戴头发！我要一顶粉色睡莲花冠！”

卢基乌斯远远望着这片欢腾，映着灯与火炬在夜里的光。奴隶们跑来跑去；肩舆在备办。他心里只有伊利娅。他想裹进那条梦纱，躺在神庙顶上做梦，梦见伊利娅在哪里，她被带到了哪里……被海盗。他像一位祭司那样站着，庄严地凝望着前方。

## VIII / 八

===Original===
During those evenings of the summer festival, Alexandria was lighted more brilliantly than Rome itself. The town glittered with hundreds of lights, lamps, lanterns, torches and links; it glittered in its harbours, where the blinding sheaves of light floated from the dome of the lighthouse; it glittered in its two main streets, which intersected each other; it glittered in the colonnades of the Museum and the Gymnasium, colonnades and stadia themselves restlessly teeming, up to where the multitude were making merry for the festival.

But above all it glittered over Lake Mareotis and the Canopus Canal. The splendid villas on the lake were bright with many-coloured lanterns and balls of fire; the temple of Aphrodite, on the eyot, was silhouetted in flaring lines; and over the golden waters of the lake itself the illuminated boats pressed and crowded, filled with song, filled with dance, full of colour, gladness and joy; streamers flapped and rugs trailed over the sides of the boats down to the water.

Through the lighted streets the bearers hurried and thronged with the litters towards Lake Mareotis. They hurried from the diversorium, with the harpists and the dancing-girls and a great procession of slaves in festive raiment. An army of freedmen followed on horses and mules; and the passersby pointed to the imposing procession, evidently the household of a very wealthy Roman who was going to Canopus to dream.

The procession reached a landing-stage on the lake. Here a great barge lay moored, a thalamegus which Caleb had succeeded in hiring at the last moment for a vast sum of money. The thalamegus was painted blue and gilded, with blue-and-gilt oars, which stuck out like so many swan’s-legs. Caleb had had her covered with tapestry and adorned with wreaths of flowers and festoons of leaves. The silver statue of Aphrodite stood on the prow, with incense burning before it. The troop of slaves, male and female, and freedmen, with Vettius and Rufus, hastened on board to await the master’s coming.

===Chinese===
夏令节庆的那些夜晚，亚历山大里亚（Alexandria）的灯火比罗马城还要璀璨。满城闪烁着成百上千的光亮——油灯、灯笼、火把与松明；港湾里流光溢彩，灯塔的穹顶泻下一束束炫目的光华，浮动在水面之上；纵横相交的两条通衢大道上流光溢彩；博学园（Museum）与体育馆（Gymnasium）的柱廊间流光溢彩，柱廊和竞技场里人潮涌动不歇，一直涌到民众欢庆佳节的地方。

而最流光溢彩的，莫过于马雷奥蒂斯湖（Lake Mareotis）与卡诺珀斯运河（Canopus Canal）之上。湖畔华美的别墅点亮了五彩的灯笼与团团火球；小岛上的阿佛洛狄忒（Aphrodite）神庙，剪影勾出在跳动的光线里；金色的湖面上，张灯的游船挤挤挨挨，满载歌声，满载舞影，满载色彩、欢欣与喜悦；彩旗飘扬，船舷边垂挂的绒毯一直拖进水中。

抬夫们抬着肩舆，穿过灯火通明的街道，匆匆涌向马雷奥蒂斯湖。他们从客栈（diversorium）里出来，带着竖琴师、舞女，还有一长列节庆盛装的奴隶。一大队被释奴（freedmen）骑着马和骡子跟在后面；路人指指点点地看着这气派的行列——显然是某位豪富罗马人的家眷随从，正要到卡诺珀斯（Canopus）去做梦。

行列到了湖边的一座码头。一艘大游船泊在那里——一条泰拉美戈斯（thalamegus）豪华楼船，是卡莱布（Caleb）在最后一刻花了巨款才租到的。楼船漆成蓝色，饰以鎏金，船桨也是蓝底金饰，一支支出挑在船外，宛如一只只天鹅的腿。卡莱布让人给船身铺满织锦挂毯，缀上鲜花花环与枝叶彩绸。船头立着阿佛洛狄忒的银像，像前燃着香。男女奴隶与被释奴，连同维提乌斯（Vettius）和鲁弗斯（Rufus），匆匆上船，等候主人驾临。

===Original===
A dense multitude pressed round to look on greedily. Now a Roman litter approached, recognizable by its square shape; yet another and the master alighted, with the aid of his slaves, male and female. He was accompanied by an old, corpulent kinsman and a grave tutor.

“He’s going dreaming! He’s going dreaming!” cried the populace. “See, he has his dreaming-veil on! He looks like Serapis himself!”

Beggars crowded round the travellers:

“Divine lord and exalted prince! Image of Horus, the son of Osiris! May Serapis send you good dreams! May Serapis load you with blessings! May he keep bad dreams locked far from you, in the shadowy underworld!”

The stewards distributed money among the beggars. Lucius had gone on board. The slave-girls scattered flowers before his feet as he walked.

===Chinese===
稠密的人群围拢过来，贪婪地观看。一乘罗马式的肩舆过来了，四四方方的形制一望即知；又是一乘，主人在男女奴隶的搀扶下下了轿。随行的是一位年老肥胖的亲戚，还有一位神情庄重的导师。

「他去做梦了！他去做梦了！」百姓们喊道。「看哪，他蒙着做梦的面纱！他简直就像萨拉皮斯（Serapis）本人！」

乞丐们围住了这些游客：

「神圣的老爷，尊贵的王子！俄西里斯（Osiris）之子荷鲁斯（Horus）的化身！愿萨拉皮斯赐您好梦！愿萨拉皮斯降福于您！愿他把噩梦紧锁在幽冥地府，远离您身旁！」

管家们向乞丐们散发钱币。卢基乌斯（Lucius）已经上了船。他行走时，女奴们在他脚前撒下鲜花。

===Original===
The song of the rowers was heard from the body of the boat. The creaking ropes were cast off; the barge glided towards the middle of the lake. She gleamed with blue, green and yellow lights and left a trail of brightness in her wake; the water was bright around her. On the banks the villas and palaces of light stood in gardens of light.

Hundreds of other barges were gliding slowly in the same direction. Above the monotonous drone of the rowers’ song rang ballads and hymns. The music of citharas was heard in descending chords; the harps rang out; the notes of double flutes quavered through the evening air with a magic intoxication of melody.

The waters of the lake stood high. It was the month when the kindly Nile stepped outside its banks with a moist foot and overflowed the Delta. The golden waters of the lake lapped higher than the marble steps of the villas down which the brilliant hetairae descended, holding the lappets of their veils, to take their seats on the cushions of their barges.

===Chinese===
船腹里传来桨手的歌声。吱呀作响的缆绳解开了；游船向湖心滑去。船上蓝、绿、黄各色灯火熠熠，身后曳出一道明亮的水痕；四周的水面波光粼粼。岸上，灯火筑成的别墅与宫殿，矗立在灯火的花园里。

成百艘别的游船也朝同一个方向缓缓滑行。桨手歌单调的低吟之上，升起了谣曲与颂歌。基萨拉琴（cithara）的和弦层层下行；竖琴铮铮鸣响；双管笛的音符颤巍巍地穿过晚风，带着一种令人心醉神迷的旋律。

湖水高涨。正是这样的月份：仁慈的尼罗河（Nile）迈着湿润的脚踏出河岸，漫过三角洲。金色的湖水拍得比别墅的大理石台阶还高，华艳的名妓（hetairae）们提着面纱的垂带，沿台阶款款而下，到游船的软垫上落座。

===Original===
Flowers fell on the water, in unison with the notes of hymn and song. All the craft, hundreds and hundreds, large and small, barges and coracles, square rafts and canoes, pressed gently forward towards the entrance of the Canopian Canal. On the banks were thousands of idlers and spectators, all the people of Alexandria.

The vessels glided to the harmony of the twanged strings into the broad canal. It was very full of water; the banks were flooded. Reeds tall as a man, biblos and cyamos, rose like pillars, blossoming during this month with thousands of waving plumes: the leaves of the biblos were long and bending over, as though each were languidly broken; those of the cyamos were round as scales and goblet-deep, stacked one above the other along the stems, like cups.1

Here lay the Canopian harbour, here the suburb of Eleusis; and the canal split into two branches. The narrower channel led to Schedia, on the Nile; the broader led past Nicopolis to Canopus.

Beyond stretched the sea, wide and blue. Only a narrow strip of land separated it from the canal; and it lay boundless under a thousand twinkling stars.

===Chinese===
花朵落在水面上，应和着颂歌与谣曲的音节。大大小小的船只，成百上千——游船、圆舟、方筏、独木舟——轻轻地挤向卡诺珀斯运河的入口。岸上聚着成千上万闲逛看热闹的人，亚历山大里亚倾城而出。

船只在拨响琴弦的和声里滑进了宽阔的运河。运河水涨得满满的，两岸都被淹了。一人高的芦苇——纸莎草（biblos）与白睡莲（cyamos）——如立柱般拔起，在这个月里绽出千万支摇曳的羽穗：纸莎草的叶子修长低垂，仿佛每一片都慵懒地折了腰；白睡莲的叶子圆如鳞片、深如杯盏，沿着茎秆层层相叠，宛如一只只酒杯。1

这里是卡诺珀斯港，这里是埃莱夫西斯（Eleusis）郊镇；运河分成两汊。窄的一汊通向尼罗河上的斯凯迪亚（Schedia），宽的一汊经尼科波利斯（Nicopolis）通往卡诺珀斯。

再往外便是大海，宽阔湛蓝。只有一带窄窄的陆地把它与运河隔开；它在千百颗闪烁的星辰下无边无际地铺展。

===Original===
“Lucius,” said Thrasyllus, sitting spellbound at the feet of the young Roman, who sat on a raised throne and gazed in front of him like a priest, full of longing for his dream of that night, “Lucius, my Lord Catullus, look! We have passed Nicopolis, with its amphitheatre and stadium; and yonder lies Taposiris, with Cape Zephyrium; and on a height I can see the temple of Aphrodite Arsinoe.”

“I see,” said Lucius, turning his eyes towards the temple, which was lit with lines of fire and rose above the water like a mansion in Olympus.

“I see,” echoed Uncle Catullus, seated by Lucius’ side.

“I was reading,” Thrasyllus explained, “that at the same place where that temple now stands there once stood the city of Thonis, named after the king who hospitably entreated Menelaus and Helen. Homer mentions it and speaks of the secret herbs and precious balsams which Helen received from Queen Polydamna, Thonis’ spouse.”

===Chinese===
「卢基乌斯，」特拉叙卢斯（Thrasyllus）说。他出神地坐在年轻罗马人的脚边，而后者坐在高起的宝座上，像祭司一样凝望着前方，满心渴望着今夜之梦。「卢基乌斯，卡图卢斯（Catullus）大人，看哪！我们已经过了尼科波利斯，它的圆剧场和竞技场都过去了；那边是塔波西里斯（Taposiris）和泽菲里乌姆角（Cape Zephyrium）；在高岗上，我看得见阿尔西诺伊·阿佛洛狄忒（Aphrodite Arsinoe）神庙。」

「我看见了。」卢基乌斯说着，把目光转向那座神庙。神庙燃着一线线火光，像奥林波斯（Olympus）山上的一座宫殿，耸出水面。

「我也看见了。」卡图卢斯叔父（Uncle Catullus）应声道，他坐在卢基乌斯身旁。

「我读到过，」特拉叙卢斯解释道，「在那座神庙如今矗立的地方，从前有座托尼斯（Thonis）城，城名得自那位殷勤款待过墨涅拉俄斯（Menelaus）与海伦（Helen）的国王。荷马（Homer）提到过它，还讲到海伦从托尼斯的王后波吕丹娜（Polydamna）那里得到的秘药香草与珍贵的香膏。」

===Original===
“You know everything, Thrasyllus,” said Uncle Catullus, warmly, “and it is a joy to travel with you.”

“Tell the slave from Cos to sing the Hymn to Aphrodite as we row past the goddess’ temple,” said Lucius.

Thrasyllus went to Cora and communicated the master’s order. Forthwith a group of singers and dancers rose to their feet. Cora herself struck the resounding chords. And she sang:

> “Mother of Eros, hear thy slave!
> “Child of the foam, great goddess of love,
> Aphrodite, look down from above!
> Thou, who dost madden the gods with desire,
> Thou, who fulfillest men’s hearts with thy fire,
> All but the heart of my lord that I crave,
> Hark to thy slave!”

===Chinese===
「你真是无所不知啊，特拉叙卢斯，」卡图卢斯叔父热切地说，「与你同行实在是件乐事。」

「吩咐那个科斯（Cos）岛来的女奴，等我们摇过女神的神庙时，唱《阿佛洛狄忒赞歌》（Hymn to Aphrodite）。」卢基乌斯说。

特拉叙卢斯走到科拉（Cora）面前，传达了主人的吩咐。随即一队歌者与舞女站起身来。科拉亲手拨响了铮铮的琴弦。她唱道：

> 「厄洛斯（Eros）之母，垂听你的女奴！
> 「泡沫之女，伟大的爱之女神，
> 阿佛洛狄忒，请自高天俯望！
> 你，使诸神为爱欲而癫狂；
> 你，以烈火盈满世人之心，
> 唯独我渴念的主君之心未得盈满，
> 垂听你的女奴！」

===Original===
She stood as one inspired while she sang, with her fingers on the chords, facing the temple. Around her the girls danced to the song. The movements of their lithe bodies, light as the ripple of a silken scarf in the breeze, met and dissolved in picture after picture with each word of the song. The singer’s voice swelled crystal-clear. From the bank of the canal, from the open houses, on the temple-steps the people listened to her song. In the tall reeds lay smaller boats, wherein a man and woman embraced in love. Their hands thrust aside the yielding stems; and their smiles glanced at Cora.

===Chinese===
她唱的时候如受了神启一般站着，手指按在琴弦上，面朝神庙。姑娘们绕着她随歌起舞。她们柔韧的肢体轻盈摆动，如微风中丝巾的涟漪，随着歌中的每一句聚拢又消散，化出一幅又一幅画面。歌者的嗓音清越渐扬。运河岸边、敞开的宅子里、神庙的台阶上，人们聆听着她的歌。高高的芦苇丛中泊着几条小船，船上一对男女拥抱着相爱。他们的手拨开柔顺的茎秆；他们的笑意望向科拉。

===Original===
> “All but the heart of my lord that I crave,
> Hark to thy slave!”

the other singers now sang after her.

“She sings well,” said Lucius.

Cora heard him. She blushed crimson between the great rose-coloured flowers at her temples. But she behaved as though she had heard nothing. And she sat down quietly among her companions, at the foot of the silver statue of Aphrodite.

===Chinese===
> 「唯独我渴念的主君之心未得盈满，
> 垂听你的女奴！」

其他歌者这时跟着她唱了起来。

「她唱得很好。」卢基乌斯说。

科拉听见了。她两鬓大朵的玫瑰色花朵之间，泛起了绯红。但她装作什么也没听见，安安静静地坐回同伴中间，坐在阿佛洛狄忒银像的脚下。

===Original===
The barge glided on slowly with the others. From all of them, in turns, came music. The water of the flooding canal was like a broad golden mirror. On the bank, between the stalks of the tall reeds, the open taverns and brothels rose, wreathed in flowers, as from an enchanted lake. The women in them beckoned and waved with long lotus-stems.

But the barges glided on, towards Canopus. They were all going to the temple of Serapis. Not until after the dreams would the brothels and taverns be visited. The orgy was to come after the dream.

===Chinese===
游船同其他船一起缓缓前行。各条船上轮番传来乐声。涨水的运河水面，像一面宽阔的金镜。岸上高高的芦苇秆之间，敞开的酒馆与妓院缀满花环拔地而起，宛如从一座魔湖中升起。里面的女人们挥着长长的莲茎，向人招呼致意。

但游船继续向前，驶向卡诺珀斯。它们都是去萨拉皮斯神庙的。要到做完梦之后，才会去光顾那些妓院酒馆。狂欢纵欲，排在梦之后。

## X / 十

===Original===
Had Lucius slept? Had he dreamed? Had the fragrant cloud drugged his senses? Had a strange mystic power spread over him? Had Serapis descended upon him? Had the dreams surrounded him?

It seemed to him that a golden thunder roused him from his heavy, motionless lethargy. The gong-strokes rolled through the temple and far away into the starry night. Harp-chords sounded, a hymn was intoned. He felt his veil wet with thick-rising dew.﻿ ﻿…

Round and round the terraces, singing, moved the long procession of the priests. It was still night. Everywhere around Lucius the dreamers arose, drunk with sleep and dreaming. In the reflections of the lamps and torches their faces were ghostly, spiritualized as after a long prayer, after protracted adoration and ecstasy, wherein their thoughts, desires and souls had been refined.

On the topmost terrace, round which the whole city shimmered visibly with light﻿—on the one side the nocturnal blue of the sea, on the other the silvery forking of the Nile’s mouths through the Delta﻿—the learned hierogrammats, the keepers of the sacred writings, sat each on his throne. In their hands they held unrolled the sacred scrolls, whose hieroglyphics gave answer to all things. Temple-slaves behind them lifted high the coloured lanterns. In front of them the multitudinous dreamers thronged.

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯睡着了吗？他做梦了吗？是芳馨的云雾迷醉了他的感官？是一种奇异的神秘力量笼罩了他？是萨拉皮斯降临到了他身上？是梦把他围住了？

他仿佛觉得，是一声金色的雷鸣把他从沉重、僵滞的昏沉中唤醒。锣声滚过神庙，远远滚进星夜之中。竖琴的和弦响了，一首颂歌被吟诵起来。他觉得面纱被浓重升腾的露水浸湿了。……

祭司们的长列唱着颂歌，绕着平台巡回。夜还未尽。卢基乌斯四周，做梦的人们纷纷起身，还沉醉于睡眠与梦。灯与火把的光影里，他们的脸苍白如幽灵，如经灵化，仿佛经过长久祈祷、经过绵延的崇拜与迷狂之后，他们的思绪、欲望与灵魂都得了提炼。

在最高的平台上——环着它，全城的灯火明晃晃地闪烁：一边是夜海之蓝，另一边是尼罗河各汊口穿过三角洲的银色分叉——博学的圣书祭司们、圣典的守护者，各自坐在自己的宝座上。他们手中展开着圣卷，卷上的象形文字解答万事。他们身后的神庙奴隶高举着彩灯。他们面前，无数的做梦者蜂拥着。

===Original===
Great was the thronging. The dreamers wanted to know the interpretation of their dreams. But those who had dreamed were so many that the priests did not answer save with a few words full of dark meaning.

Many, disappointed, went down the terraces. Orgy awaited them in the taverns and brothels along the canal.﻿ ﻿…

Lucius had risen, in the midst of all his followers. He stood stiff, motionless, veiled in the gold net, like a god entranced.

“Lucius,” Thrasyllus asked, “my dear child and master, tell me: have you dreamed?”

===Chinese===
拥挤极了。做梦的人们想知道自己梦的解析。但做过梦的人实在太多，祭司们只以寥寥数语作答，语意晦暗。

许多人失望了，走下平台。运河边的酒馆妓院里，纵酒狂欢正等着他们。……

卢基乌斯已经起身，站在随从们中间。他僵直地、一动不动地立着，罩在金网里，像一位出神入定的神。

「卢基乌斯，」特拉叙卢斯问道，「我亲爱的孩子、我的主人，告诉我：你做梦了吗？」

===Original===
“Yes,” replied Lucius, in a trance.

“I too,” said Uncle Catullus. “It was a nightmare, *most* unpleasant! I had dined too heavily. My stomach was overloaded. And I am now shivering with this chilly dew. Egypt is *most* interesting, but Egypt will positively be the death of me!”

Caleb had approached:

“My gracious lord,” said Caleb, “your Sabaean amulets have no doubt inspired you with a favourable dream. You must have your dream expounded. But not by the hierogrammats.﻿ ﻿… Look, the dreamers are crowding in front of them. There is no reaching them. You must have your dream expounded by a most holy prophet, by Amphris, the centenarian.﻿ ﻿… Come with me, let me lead you to him.﻿ ﻿…” He took Lucius by the hand. “It costs half a talent, no less,” said Caleb. “Thirty minae, my lord. But then Amphris will expound your dreams for you, Amphris, the holy Amphris. The hierogrammats charge ten or twenty drachmae. But they can never tell it as the holy Amphris, the prophet does. This is where he sits enthroned, my lord.”

===Chinese===
「做了。」卢基乌斯答道，恍恍惚惚。

「我也做了，」卡图卢斯叔父说。「是个噩梦，*最*不痛快！我晚饭吃得太饱，胃里撑得慌。这会儿又被冰凉的露水冻得直哆嗦。埃及*最*有意思，可埃及非把我这条老命送掉不可！」

卡莱布走上前来：

「仁慈的主人，」卡莱布说，「您的萨巴人（Sabaean）护符想必已启发您做了个吉梦。您得请人解析这个梦。但不能找圣书祭司。……瞧，做梦的人把他们都围满了，挤不过去。您得请最圣洁的先知来解析，请那位百岁人瑞安弗里斯（Amphris）。……跟我来，我领您去见他。……」他拉住卢基乌斯的手。「要花半个塔兰同（talent），少一分都不行，」卡莱布说。「三十弥那（mina），大人。不过那样，安弗里斯就会亲自为您解梦了——安弗里斯，圣洁的安弗里斯。圣书祭司们只收十个二十个德拉克马（drachma）。可他们绝讲不出圣洁的先知安弗里斯那样的道理。他端坐宝座的地方就是这儿，大人。」

===Original===
They were standing in front of a small pyramid, on one of the upper terraces. Two sphinxes beside the narrow door lay like mysterious stone sentinels. Temple-keepers guarded the gate.

“The most holy Amphris?” Caleb asked.

“Forty minae,” said one of the priests.

“Why not a talent right away?” grumbled Caleb.

===Chinese===
他们站在一座小金字塔前，在上层的一处平台上。窄门两旁卧着两尊斯芬克斯，像神秘的石头卫兵。神庙执事守着大门。

「至圣的安弗里斯？」卡莱布问。

「四十弥那。」一位祭司说。

「怎么不干脆要一个塔兰同？」卡莱布咕哝道。

===Original===
“Forty minae,” repeated the priest.

Caleb took the gold coins from the long purse at his girdle and slipped them into the priest’s hand:

“Enter, my lord,” he said, pointing to the open door.

Lucius entered. Seated on a throne was an old man who looked like a god of age and wisdom. Lucius himself was as beautiful as a young god. A strange light, as of soft moons, shone from blue globes. Lucius bowed to the ground, fell upon his knees and kissed the floor. He remained in this position.

===Chinese===
「四十弥那。」祭司重复道。

卡莱布从腰间的长钱袋里取出金币，塞进祭司手里：

「请进，大人。」祭司指着敞开的门说道。

卢基乌斯走了进去。宝座上坐着一位老人，形貌宛如高龄与智慧之神。卢基乌斯自己则俊美如一位年轻的神。一团奇异的光，如柔和的月光，从几颗蓝色的球体里照出。卢基乌斯拜倒在地，双膝跪下，亲吻了地面。他保持着这个姿势。

===Original===
“Did Serapis pass over you, my son?”

“Yes, holy father.”

“What did he make you see, in your dreams?”

“The woman whom I love.﻿ ﻿…”

The prophet had laid his long, thin, transparent hand on the dreamer’s head:

“But who did not love you,” he said, gently and quietly.

===Chinese===
「萨拉皮斯从你身上经过了吗，我的孩子？」

「是的，圣洁的父。」

「他在梦里让你看见了什么？」

「我爱着的那位女人。……

先知把他那瘦长透明的手放在做梦者的头上：

「可她并不爱你。」他轻柔而平静地说。

===Original===
“How do you know, holy father?﻿ ﻿… I saw the pirates who kidnapped her.﻿ ﻿…”

“But by whom she was not kidnapped.﻿ ﻿…”

“How do you know, holy father?”

“And by whom she was not sold as a slave.”

“Where is she then, O father?”

“What did Serapis make you see in the dream?”

===Chinese===
「您怎么知道，圣洁的父？……我看见了绑架她的海盗。……

「可她并不是被他们绑架的。……

「您怎么知道，圣洁的父？」

「她也并不是被他们卖为奴隶的。」

「那她在哪儿，哦，父啊？」

「萨拉皮斯在梦里让你看见了什么？」

===Original===
Lucius sobbed:

“I do not know, father.﻿ ﻿… I saw her and﻿ ﻿… those who kidnapped her.”

“How many were they?”

“Many.”

“Old and young?”

“No, they resembled one another like brothers, like doubles.”

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯呜咽道：

「我不知道，父啊。……我看见了她，还有……那些绑架她的人。」

「他们有多少人？」

「很多。」

「有老有少？」

「不，他们彼此相像，像兄弟，像一模一样的双影。」

===Original===
“Because they were not many.”

“Not many?”

“No.”

“How many were they, father?”

“They were﻿ ﻿… *one*.”

“Not more?”

===Chinese===
「因为他们并非很多人。」

「不是很多人？」

「不是。」

「他们有几个人，父啊？」

「他们只有……*一个*。」

「没有更多了？」

===Original===
“They were *one*,” repeated the prophet. “My son, your soul is sick. It is sick with sorrow and love. Love is strong, but wisdom is stronger. Gather wisdom, my son. My child, I can see into your soul. I see it lying tortured and trembling.”

“There is no comfort if I do not find her!”

“There is comfort. Isis seeking for Osiris recovered all the pieces of his body except that piece which fructified her. And yet she found comfort, in the end.”

“Give me comfort, holy father.”

“I am wisdom, child, and you are young. Serve wisdom, but honour love.”

===Chinese===
「他们只有*一个*。」先知重复道。「我的孩子，你的灵魂病了。它因悲伤与爱而病。爱是强大的，但智慧更强大。积聚智慧吧，我的孩子。孩子，我能看进你的灵魂。我看见它躺在那里，受着折磨，瑟瑟发抖。」

「找不到她，就没有安慰！」

「有安慰的。伊西斯（Isis）寻访俄西里斯，寻回了他身体的全部碎块，只除了使她受孕的那一块。可她最终仍然找到了安慰。」

「给我安慰吧，圣洁的父。」

「我是智慧，孩子，而你还年轻。侍奉智慧，但要敬重爱。」

===Original===
“Father, why did the pirates resemble one another?”

“Because they were one.”

“One pirate?”

“One pirate.”

“Where is Ilia, father?”

“My son, even my wisdom does not tell me that whereof you have not dreamed. You dreamed of many pirates, who resembled one another like doubles. There was one pirate, my child.”

===Chinese===
「父啊，为什么那些海盗彼此相像？」

「因为他们本是一个人。」

「一个海盗？」

「一个海盗。」

「伊利娅（Ilia）在哪儿，父啊？」

「我的孩子，就连我的智慧，也无法告诉我你梦中未见的事。你梦见了许多海盗，他们像双影一般彼此相像。其实只有一个海盗，我的孩子。」

===Original===
“Who was he?”

“Did Serapis conjure up his image before you?”

“I no longer see it.”

“Then go in peace. And let love and wisdom comfort you.”

===Chinese===
「他是谁？」

「萨拉皮斯可曾把他的形象召到你面前？」

「我现在看不见了。」

「那就平心而去吧。让爱与智慧来安慰你。」

===Original===
Lucius went. On the threshold of the pyramid he met an hetaira. She glittered like an idol in her ceremonial garb, sewn with jewels, and looked at him with painted eyes.

“It’s Tamyris, my lord,” said Caleb. “She is going to consult Amphris. She has paid a talent! Has Amphris interpreted your dream? The doorkeeper, who also is wise, has interpreted mine for me! And for only five drachmae.”

“One pirate! One pirate!” murmured Lucius.

And he clenched his fists, impotently.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯走了出去。在金字塔门口，他遇见一位名妓。她穿着缀满珠宝的礼服，像神像一样闪闪发光，用画过的眼睛望着他。

「那是塔米里斯（Tamyris），大人。」卡莱布说。「她要去求问安弗里斯。她付了一个塔兰同呢！安弗里斯给您解梦了吗？守门的也很有智慧，他替我解了梦！只收五个德拉克马。」

「一个海盗！一个海盗！」卢基乌斯喃喃道。

他攥紧双拳，却无能为力。……

===Original===
The multitude streamed away along the terraces. The barges glided back on the canal, in the night.

And constantly, near the pleasure-houses and taverns, the vessels stopped and the dreamers alighted.

Here mead flowed and foaming golden beer and heavy Mareotis wines and the intoxicating liqueurs of Napata. Here the naked women, who beckoned with lotus-stalks, twisted in the dance.

“Back!” cried Lucius. “Back to Alexandria!”

The barge stopped at no pleasure-houses, at no taverns. The master sobbed, his head wrapped in his golden dreaming-veil. There was no music. Only the plaintive song of the rowers made itself heard from below.

Behind, in the east, the dawn paled in one long, rosy line, above the sea﻿ ﻿… while the festal lamps flickered out and died.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
人群沿着平台流散。游船在夜里沿运河滑回。

一路上，行乐之所和酒馆旁边，船不停地停下，做梦的人纷纷登岸。

这里流淌着蜂蜜酒、泛泡的金色啤酒、浓烈的马雷奥蒂斯酒，还有纳帕塔（Napata）令人迷醉的甜酒。这里，挥着莲茎招呼客人的裸女们扭动起舞。

「回去！」卢基乌斯喊道。「回亚历山大里亚去！」

游船在任何行乐之所、任何酒馆都不停泊。主人把头裹在金色的做梦面纱里，呜咽着。没有音乐。只有桨手们哀婉的歌声从下面传来。

身后，东方，曙光在海上泛起一线长长的玫瑰色……而节庆的灯火闪烁着，渐渐熄灭。……

## XI / 十一

===Original===
Serapis had opened the floodgates of the sky.

The first spring rains had already descended in heavy torrents; the water-gods had already poured the kindly streams from their urns into the swelling Nile; the river-surveyors, who had consulted the Nilometers2 at every place, declared that the sacred stream was steadily rising and that the maximum gauge would be reached that summer.

The rains clattered down in white curtains of pouring waters.

The palm-garden of the diversorium was inundated. Master Ghizla made his slaves dig little canals to carry the water to cisterns.

There was much joy and gladness at all this water. The air was fresh; though midsummer was approaching, an equable coolness tempered the atmosphere around Alexandria; no river-mist spread seeds of disease; and the great dampness brought relief even to this ground, which had dried up during the winter, and to the parched air.

===Chinese===
萨拉皮斯打开了天上的水闸。

最初的春雨已经滂沱而降；水神们已经从瓮中把仁慈的水流倾进上涨的尼罗河；在各地察看过尼罗水位计（Nilometer）2的河道测量师们宣告：圣河正在稳步上涨，那年夏天水位将涨到最高刻度。

雨水哗哗地倾泻而下，像一道道白色的水幕。

客栈的棕榈园被淹了。吉兹拉老板（Master Ghizla）让他的奴隶们挖小水渠，把水引进蓄水池。

有了这些水，人人欢喜。空气清新；虽然仲夏将临，一种匀和的凉爽调和着亚历山大里亚四周的大气；没有河雾播撒疫病的种子；这充分的湿润，甚至给干了一冬的土地和燥渴的空气都带来了舒解。

===Original===
The travellers remained indoors. After the night of dreams at Canopus, Lucius had come home in one of his impotent fits of fury, locking himself in his room in despair and refusing to see anybody whatever.

Uncle Catullus abandoned himself to long siestas; Thrasyllus studied books, maps and globes.

In the porch of the slaves’ quarters sat Cora. As she was forbidden to sing or play, she sat crouched with her arms around her knees, gazing mournfully at the rains. Their lord’s sickness spread melancholy among all his household.

Caleb squatted beside Cora. Like her he sat with his arms around his knees and he smiled with his flashing eyes and teeth and said:

===Chinese===
旅人们待在屋里不出门。卡诺珀斯那场梦之夜后，卢基乌斯带着一阵无能为力的狂怒回了家，绝望地把自己锁在房间里，什么人也不肯见。

卡图卢斯叔父沉溺于长长的午睡；特拉叙卢斯钻研书籍、地图和地球仪。

科拉坐在奴隶住处的门廊里。她被禁止唱歌弹琴，便抱着膝盖蜷坐着，哀愁地望着雨水。主人的病恹把忧郁传给了全家上下。

卡莱布蹲在科拉身旁。他像她一样抱着膝盖坐着，闪亮的眼睛和牙齿含着笑，说道：

===Original===
“Cora, I love you very much.”

Cora did not move; she merely answered, very gently:

“I am not free; I belong to the master.”

“I should like to buy you, Cora; and then you would be free.”

Cora did not answer; the rain poured down in an endless grey sheet; and in the palm-garden, under an umbrella, Master Ghizla drilled his dripping slaves.

===Chinese===
「科拉，我非常爱你。」

科拉一动不动，只是轻声答道：

「我不是自由身；我属于主人。」

「我想把你买下来，科拉；那样你就是自由的了。」

科拉没有回答；雨无尽地倾泻着灰白的一片；棕榈园里，吉兹拉老板撑着伞，正在操练淋得湿透的奴隶们。

===Original===
“You would be free,” Caleb repeated. “You would not be my slave, but my wife. I am rich: we are rich, Ghizla and I. We do a very good business. Our diversorium is the finest in Alexandria. We make a great deal of money, because all princely nobles alight at our establishment. Cora, you would be its mistress. You would have slaves, male and female. I would pay your master whatever he asked; it would be deducted from his bill. For business is business, you know. But I could pay for you, if necessary, in ready money. And then, Cora, when we have grown very rich﻿ ﻿… then we would go back to Saba, to my native land. It is the sweetest and most beautiful country in the world﻿ ﻿… to live in, you know. But there’s no business to be done there. You have to be rich there; then it’s delightful. When we are rich, we will go back there. Cora, shall I tell you about Saba, about my country, even if it were only, Cora, to divert you, now that it’s raining and you mustn’t sing?”

“I am listening, Caleb.”

===Chinese===
「你会自由的，」卡莱布重复道。「你不会做我的奴隶，而是做我的妻子。我有钱：我们有钱，吉兹拉和我。我们生意做得很红火。我们的客栈是亚历山大里亚最好的。我们赚大钱，因为王公贵胄都落脚在我们店里。科拉，你将做客栈的老板娘。你会有男女奴隶。你主人要多少，我就付多少；从他的账上扣好了。生意归生意嘛，你知道的。不过必要时，我也可以付现钱。到那时，科拉，等我们发了大财……我们就回萨巴（Saba）去，回我的故乡。那是世界上最甜美、最美丽的国家……住起来真是，你知道的。可是在那儿没生意可做。在那儿你得先有钱；有钱就美了。等我们富了，我们就回去。科拉，要不要我给你讲讲萨巴，讲讲我的国家——哪怕就当是，科拉，逗你散散心，眼下下着雨，你又不许唱歌？」

「我听着呢，卡莱布。」

===Original===
“Saba, dear Cora, is the mightiest kingdom of Arabia; Saba is Arabia Felix, Cora.
