Saba is the sweet land where the balsam-trees grow and the precious spices are gathered: myrrh and frankincense and cinnamon. All the herbs and flowers, Cora, are scented in Saba; there is no herb and no flower that is not scented. Under the sky, which is transparent as empty blue space, the clouds of perfume waft up and rise to the feet of the gods, who always glance down smilingly upon my country, upon my happy country. The palm-tree is scented there and the calamus-reed is scented there; the scented papyrus blossoms there. Nowhere are the flowers so big and of so many kinds, or the trees so densely-leaved or so green. Nowhere are the nights so mild and the days so blissful. The nights are for feasting and the days for resting. We climb up long ladders into the tall trees and sleep in leafy nests, like birds. Mariaba is my town, the golden capital of my sweet land. Have you ever seen a fairy-city in your dreams, Cora? That is Mariaba. There are temples of chrysolite with domes of blue crystal, which imitate the firmament. The streets are strewn with golden sand. Mariaba is situated on a hill, like the palace of a god. The king, Cora, is a descendant of Balkis, our great queen, who brought Solomon the treasures of Ophir; the king lives at Mariaba in a palace walled with gold. The walls of his apartments are like blue mirrors and he treads on carpets that are woven of flowers and hourly renewed. He does not eat, but lives on perfumes. He is sacred, but he may not leave his palace; for an oracle has commanded his people to stone him the moment that he comes out. Everything in his palace and in the town is luxury and delight. There is no commerce, there is no business. The Sabaeans surrender the trade in the precious products of their country to the men of Syria and Mesopotamia. They themselves, Cora, are rich and as gods.﻿ ﻿… When we are rich and you are my wife﻿ ﻿… we shall be as gods in Mariaba and you shall see the king, behind a transparent hanging of gold glass, while he feeds on those perfumes. We shall live in a house of alabaster, which is transparent, but only to those inside. We shall have a barge of blue leather with red-silk tassels and little golden, tinkling bells.﻿ ﻿… When the evening wind is fresh, we shall warm our hands at glowing cinnamon. I shall anoint your body with fluid larimnum, which is the most costly of aromatics and is not exported, not even to Caesar. We shall have no plate except of gold and an ivory couch inlaid with jasper, or perhaps with sard. And you will go about on an elephant with silvered hoofs, many gold bands round his trunk and, at night, two little lanterns on his tusks, Cora. And we shall be happier than you can imagine or than I can tell.”3

===Chinese===
「萨巴，亲爱的科拉，是阿拉伯最强大的王国；萨巴就是幸福阿拉伯（Arabia Felix），科拉。萨巴是那片甜美的土地，香脂树在那里生长，珍贵的香料在那里采撷：没药、乳香和肉桂。所有的香草与花朵，科拉，在萨巴都带着芬芳；没有一株草、一朵花是不香的。在明澈如湛蓝虚空的天空下，香气的云霭升腾而起，直上诸神的脚边；诸神总是含笑俯瞰我的国家，俯瞰我这幸福的国家。那里的棕榈树是香的，菖蒲苇是香的，芬芳的纸莎草在那里开花。没有哪里的花朵有这样大、这样多，没有哪里的树木有这样浓密苍翠。没有哪里的夜这样温良，没有哪里的昼这样安乐。夜里宴饮，白日安歇。我们攀着长长的梯子爬上高树，睡在枝叶编成的巢里，像鸟儿一样。马里亚巴（Mariaba）是我的城，是我那甜美国土的金色都城。你在梦里见过仙人的城吗，科拉？那就是马里亚巴。那里有橄榄石筑的神庙，覆着蓝水晶的穹顶，仿造天穹。街道上铺撒着金沙。马里亚巴坐落在一座山丘上，像神的一座宫殿。国王，科拉，是巴尔基斯（Balkis）的后裔——我们伟大的王后，她曾把俄斐（Ophir）的财宝献给所罗门（Solomon）；国王住在马里亚巴一座金墙环围的宫殿里。他居所的墙壁如蓝色的镜子，他脚踩的花毯用鲜花织成，每个钟头都换新。他不进饮食，以香气为生。他是神圣的，但不得离开宫殿；因为有神谕命令他的子民，他一露面就用石头砸他。他宫里和城里的一切都是奢华与享乐。没有交易，没有买卖。萨巴人把本国珍货的贸易都让给叙利亚和美索不达米亚人去做。他们自己，科拉，富得如神一般。……等我们富了，你做了我的妻子……我们在马里亚巴就会像神一样，你还能见到国王，隔着金玻璃的透明垂帘，看他以香气为食。我们将住在一座雪花石膏的房子里，房子是透明的，但只对屋里的人透明。我们将有一艘蓝皮游船，挂着红丝缨和叮当作响的小金铃。……晚风清凉的时候，我们就着烧红的肉桂暖手。我要用流动的拉里姆努姆香膏（larimnum）涂抹你的身体——那是最贵重的香料，从不出口，连恺撒（Caesar）那里也不出口。我们将只用金制的餐具，睡一张镶碧玉、也许还镶红玉髓的象牙卧榻。你将骑一头蹄子镀银的大象出行，象鼻上缠着许多道金箍，夜里，两根象牙上各挂一盏小灯，科拉。我们将幸福得超出你的想象，也超出我嘴上说的。」3

===Original===
“What you are describing, Caleb, is indeed like fairyland. But I have heard say that, because of all that fragrance in their country, the Sabaeans one and all suffer from headache.”

“When we suffer from headache, Cora, we burn asphalt and the hairs of a goat’s beard. There is no remedy to compare with that for headache. Or else we wear the sacred amulets. Wear one, Cora: wear this amulet, which I have always worn.”

“No, Caleb.”

“Are you afraid that I shall bewitch you?”

“Yes. I fear the Sabaean amulets. It is perhaps because of one of them that the master dreamed the bad dream which has made him ill and sad.”

===Chinese===
「你说的这些，卡莱布，真像仙境。可我听人说，正因为那满国的香气，萨巴人个个都害头痛病。」

「我们头痛的时候，科拉，就烧沥青和山羊胡子的毛。治头痛没有比这更灵的药了。再不然，我们就戴上神圣的护符。戴一个吧，科拉：戴上这枚护符，我一直戴着的。」

「不，卡莱布。」

「你是怕我对你施巫术？」

「是的。我怕萨巴护符。主人做的那场害他病恹恹、愁惨惨的噩梦，说不定就是因为其中一枚。」

===Original===
“Cora, I love you so much.﻿ ﻿… Will you permit me to buy you from your master?”

“If you bought me, O Caleb, I should be a faithful slave and sing and play the harp to you. But I should be unhappy, even if I were your wife and free﻿ ﻿… because I should be so far from my master.﻿ ﻿…”

“Whom you love.”

Cora hesitated. Then she said:

“Whom I love, Caleb﻿ ﻿… but as the flower loves the sun, as the moth loves the star﻿ ﻿… from afar and from the depths﻿ ﻿… without hope.”

===Chinese===
「科拉，我这么爱你。……你肯不肯让我把你从你主人手里买下来？」

「你若买下我，哦，卡莱布，我会做个忠实的奴隶，为你唱歌弹琴。可我不会幸福的，哪怕做你的妻子、得了自由……因为我离主人就那么远了。……」

「你爱着的主人。」

科拉迟疑了一下。然后她说：

「我爱的主人，卡莱布……可是像花爱太阳，像飞蛾爱星星……隔着遥远，隔着深渊……没有指望。」

===Original===
The rain poured down in an endless grey sheet. In the garden, Master Ghizla was swearing at the slaves and wading, with tucked-up tunic and lean, hairy legs, through the puddles.

Caleb rose. He said nothing and went away, his head sunk in melancholy. Then he came back and resumed:

“You would go hunting with me, Cora, and you would sit in front of me on a Sabaean stallion, which would be swift as the wind, and we should catch lion-whelps in nets and tame them with palm-wine and they would follow you about like big cats.”

Cora only smiled and said nothing.

===Chinese===
雨无尽地倾泻着灰白的一片。园子里，吉兹拉老板正呵斥着奴隶，掖起长衣，露着瘦削多毛的腿，蹚过一个又一个水洼。

卡莱布站起身。他一言不发地走开了，头颓然低着，满怀忧郁。随后他又回来，接着说：

「你会跟我一起去打猎，科拉，你坐在我的前面，骑一匹萨巴骏马，它快得像风；我们会用网捉小狮子，拿棕榈酒驯服它们，它们会像大猫一样跟着你到处走。」

科拉只是笑，什么也没说。

===Original===
“I *know*, Cora, why you will not be my wife. It is *not* because you love your master. For, even if your master loved you, you would be a slave. *My* wife would be a free woman and reign as queen in my house. But you will not be my wife because perhaps you know the Sabaean law which prescribes that a married woman is also the wife of all her husband’s brothers. But Ghizla, dear Cora, would not dare to touch even the hem of your garment.”

“I did not know that law,” said Cora.

“There was a king’s daughter in our country, Cora. She was dazzlingly beautiful and was the wife of fifteen brothers, who were princes. All the fifteen of them glowed with love for her. When one of the brothers wished to tarry in her chamber, he set his stick outside the door, as a sign. Then the others passed their way.﻿ ﻿… When she wearied of their eagerness to love her, she devised a stratagem. She had sticks made for her, like the brothers’. When one of the princes left her, she placed one of these sticks outside her door. In this way she enjoyed peace.﻿ ﻿… But one day all the brothers happened to be in the square of the town at the same time. One of them went to visit her﻿ ﻿… and found outside the door the stick of a brother﻿ ﻿… whom he had just left in the town-square! Then he thought that his wife, the wife of the fifteen brothers, was unfaithful to them﻿ ﻿… with a sixteenth, a stranger. And he sought his father and told him of his suspicions. But it appeared that the wife was innocent. And not only the father but the fifteen brothers and their spouse laughed at the stratagem and were happy.﻿ ﻿… But you, Cora, would never need to put a stick like mine outside your door. For I have only one brother, Ghizla, and he would not dare to touch so much as the hem of your garment.”

===Chinese===
「我*知道*，科拉，你为什么不肯做我的妻子。*不是*因为你爱你的主人。因为，就算你的主人爱你，你也还是个奴隶。*我的*妻子将是自由的女人，在我家里像王后一样当家。你不肯做我的妻子，是因为你也许知道萨巴的法律：已婚女子同时是她丈夫所有兄弟的妻子。可是吉兹拉，亲爱的科拉，连你的衣角都不敢碰一下。」

「我不知道这条法律。」科拉说。

「我们国内从前有位公主，科拉。她美得耀眼，是十五个兄弟的妻子，那十五位都是王子。十五个人全为她燃着爱火。哪个兄弟想在她的房里留宿，就把自己的手杖立在门外作记号，别的兄弟便走开。……等她倦于他们争先恐后的求爱，便想出一条妙计。她叫人给自己做了些手杖，跟兄弟们的一样。哪位王子离开她，她就在门外放上一根。这样她就得了安宁。……可有一天，十五个兄弟碰巧同时都在城里的广场上。其中一个去看她……却在门外发现一根兄弟的手杖……而那位兄弟，他刚刚才在广场上同他分手！于是他想，他的妻子，这十五兄弟的妻子，对他们不忠……有了第十六个，一个外人。他去找父亲，说了自己的疑心。结果查明妻子是清白的。不止父亲，连十五个兄弟和他们的妻子都笑了那妙计，皆大欢喜。……可是你，科拉，永远不必在我门外立我这样的手杖。因为我只有一个兄弟，吉兹拉，他连你的衣角都不敢碰一下呢。」

===Original===
Cora laughed and Caleb laughed and his eyes and teeth flashed and glittered.

“In that case, I’ll think it over, Caleb!” laughed Cora. “In that case, I’ll think it over!”

“Do think it over, Cora,” laughed Caleb. “If you are willing, I’ll buy you from your master. And we shall have a pleasure-boat of cedarwood, but with sails like a bird’s wings, so that we can either sail about on the sea or soar high into the clouds. And then on some nights we could visit the moon, where all the people are transparent, like shades.﻿ ﻿… This is not a fairytale, Cora; it’s as I tell you. We *have* those magic ships in our seas, in our skies.﻿ ﻿… Think it over, Cora! Do think it over!”

And, while Cora was still laughing incredulously, Caleb girdled his tunic high and waded barefoot through the puddles of the palm-garden, looking round and laughing as he went. For Ghizla had called to him to see the canals which the slaves were digging to carry off the rainwater to the cisterns.

===Chinese===
科拉笑了，卡莱布也笑了，眼睛和牙齿闪闪发亮。

「那样的话，我就考虑考虑，卡莱布！」科拉笑着。「那样的话，我就考虑考虑！」

「好好考虑吧，科拉，」卡莱布笑着说。「你若愿意，我就把你从你主人手里买下来。我们还会有一条雪松造的游船，帆像鸟翼一样，这样我们既能在海上航行，也能高高飞上云端。那么，有些夜晚我们还能去月亮上做客，那里的人个个透明，像影子。……这不是童话，科拉；真是这样的。我们的海上、我们的天上，*就是有*那种魔船。……考虑考虑吧，科拉！好好考虑考虑！」

科拉还在将信将疑地笑着，卡莱布已经高高掖起长衣，赤脚蹚过棕榈园的一个个水洼，一边走一边回头笑着。原来是吉兹拉喊他去看奴隶们挖的水渠，好把雨水引进蓄水池。

## XII / 十二

===Original===
But Libyan bearers carried a litter into the garden.

The litter was close-curtained with blue canvas, against the rain.

And a veiled woman peeped through a slit in the curtains and beckoned to Caleb:

“Is he at home?” she asked.

Caleb recognized her, but he answered with an air of innocence and asked:

“Who, gracious lady?”

===Chinese===
这时，利比亚（Libyan）抬夫们抬着一乘肩舆进了园子。

肩舆围着蓝布帘，遮得严严实实，挡雨。

一个蒙着面纱的女人从帘缝里张望，向卡莱布招手：

「他在家吗？」她问。

卡莱布认出了她，却装出一副无辜的神情答道，并问：

「谁，仁慈的夫人？」

===Original===
“He,” repeated the woman. “The young Roman, Publius Lucius Sabinus.”

“He is at home, gracious lady,” said Caleb. “But he is unwell. He will not see anyone.”

“If he is at home, I want to see him,” said the woman.

And she alighted on the stone steps of the portico. She was closely wrapped in her veils, but Caleb had recognized her. And she offered Caleb a gold coin, which Caleb did not refuse, because business was business and a well-invested stater brought him still a little nearer to his native land, for which he was longing.

“I do not know whether I can let you in,” said Caleb, hesitatingly.

The woman produced a second piece of gold. It disappeared in Caleb’s girdle as though by witchcraft.

===Chinese===
「他，」女人重复道。「那位年轻的罗马人，普布利乌斯·卢基乌斯·萨比努斯（Publius Lucius Sabinus）。」

「他在家，仁慈的夫人，」卡莱布说。「可他身子不适。他谁也不见。」

「他既然在家，我就要见他。」女人说。

她在门廊的石阶上下了轿。她裹得严严实实，可卡莱布已经认出了她。她递给卡莱布一枚金币，卡莱布没有推辞——生意归生意嘛，一枚投资得当的斯塔特（stater），能让他离魂牵梦萦的故乡又近一点。

「我不知道能不能放您进去。」卡莱布迟疑着说。

女人又掏出第二枚金币。它像被巫术摄走一般，消失在卡莱布的腰袋里。

===Original===
“Where is he staying?” she asked.

“In the princes’ building, of course,” said Caleb, proudly. “Where his little black slave is squatting.”

The veiled woman went up to Tarrar, squatting on a mat outside a door:

“I want to see him,” said the woman. “I want to speak to him. Take me to him.”

“The master is asleep,” said Tarrar.

“Wake him.”

===Chinese===
「他住在哪儿？」她问。

「当然是王公楼，」卡莱布骄傲地说。「他的小黑奴就蹲在那儿呢。」

蒙纱的女人走到塔拉尔（Tarrar）跟前，他正蹲在一扇门外的席子上：

「我要见他，」女人说。「我有话要对他说。带我去见他。」

「主人睡着了。」塔拉尔说。

「叫醒他。」

===Original===
“The master is sick,” said Tarrar.

“Tell him that I can cure him.”

“I dare not,” said Tarrar. “He would be angry. It would be against his orders. He is accustomed to have us obey him.”

“Announce me.”

“No,” said Tarrar.

“You’re a little monkey,” said the woman.

===Chinese===
「主人病着呢。」塔拉尔说。

「告诉他我能治好他的病。」

「我不敢，」塔拉尔说。「他会生气的。那就违了他的命令了。他惯了要我们事事听他的。」

「通报我来了。」

「不。」塔拉尔说。

「你这只小猴子。」女人说。

===Original===
And she opened the door and lifted a curtain.

Tarrar and Caleb, dismayed, tried to stop her:

“She’s inside!” said Caleb.

“The master will beat me!” said Tarrar, shivering. “That impudent wench!”

But Caleb, with his finger to his mouth, told him to be silent﻿ ﻿… and listened at the door.

===Chinese===
说着她推开门，掀起一道帘子。

塔拉尔和卡莱布惊慌失措，想拦住她：

「她进去了！」卡莱布说。

「主人会打我的！」塔拉尔哆嗦着说。「那个不要脸的丫头！」

可卡莱布把手指竖在唇边，叫他别作声……自己贴着门听了进去。

===Original===
The veiled woman stood in Lucius’ room. Lucius lay on a couch in mournful meditation. He opened his eyes wide with amazement.

“I am Tamyris,” said the woman. “Lucius, I am Tamyris. I am famed for my beauty; and I have kept kings waiting on the threshold of my villa on Lake Mareotis merely out of caprice. I once kissed a negro slave while the King of Pontus was waiting; and, when my black lover held me in his arms, I called the king in﻿ ﻿… and then showed him the door and drove him away.”

“That’s not true,” said Lucius.

===Chinese===
蒙纱的女人站在卢基乌斯的房里。卢基乌斯躺在卧榻上，郁郁地出着神。他惊讶地睁大了眼睛。

「我是塔米里斯，」女人说。「卢基乌斯，我是塔米里斯。我以美貌闻名；我曾纯然出于任性，让国王们在我马雷奥蒂斯湖畔别墅的门槛上久等。有一次，本都（Pontus）国王在外面等着，我却亲吻一个黑人奴隶；等我的黑人情人把我搂在怀里，我把国王叫了进来……然后给他指门，把他赶走了。」

「这不是真的。」卢基乌斯说。

===Original===
Tamyris opened her veils and laughed:

“No, it’s not true,” she said. “But what *is* true is this, that I have been burning with love for you since the day when I saw you, beautiful as a god, on the threshold of Amphris’ pyramid. Lucius, I want to be your slave. I want to serve and love you. I will cure you and make you laugh. I shall make you forget all your sorrow. Lucius, I have served the sacred goddess Aphrodite since I was a child of six. She has taught me, through oracles and dreams, the utter secret of her science, the secret of her highest voluptuousness, which she herself did not know until she loved Adonis. Lucius, if you will love me, I shall be your slave and reveal the secret of Adonis to you.”

“Go away,” said Lucius.

“Lucius,” said Tamyris, “I have never asked a man to love me. But my days, since I looked into the mournful depths of your eyes, have been like withered gardens and my nights like scorched sands. I suffer and I am ill. I have an everlasting thirst here, in my throat, despite draughts cooled with snow and fruit steeped in silphium. See, my hands shake as though I were in a fever. See, Lucius, how my hands shake. They want to fondle you, to fondle your limbs and.﻿ ﻿…”

“Go away,” said Lucius.

===Chinese===
塔米里斯敞开面纱，笑了：

「不，这不是真的，」她说。「可*真的*是这个：自从那天在安弗里斯的金字塔门口见到你——俊美如神——我就为你燃着爱火。卢基乌斯，我想做你的奴隶。我想侍奉你、爱你。我会治好你，逗你笑。我会让你忘掉全部的忧伤。卢基乌斯，我从六岁起就侍奉神圣的女神阿佛洛狄忒。她借着神谕和梦，把她的秘术、她至高欢愉的秘密传授给我——那秘密连她自己也是直到爱上阿多尼斯（Adonis）才知晓的。卢基乌斯，你若肯爱我，我就做你的奴隶，把阿多尼斯的秘密透露给你。」

「走开。」卢基乌斯说。

「卢基乌斯，」塔米里斯说，「我从不曾求过一个男人爱我。可自从望进你眼睛忧伤的深处，我的白昼便如枯萎的花园，我的黑夜如烧焦的沙地。我受着煎熬，我病了。我喉咙里这里，有一股永不止息的渴，喝了雪镇的饮品、吃了罗盘草（silphium）浸渍的果子也不解。瞧，我的手抖着，像在发烧。瞧，卢基乌斯，我的手抖成什么样。它们想爱抚你，爱抚你的肢体，还有。……」

「走开。」卢基乌斯说。

===Original===
“Lucius, I long to be your slave. I, Tamyris, the famous hetaira, who possess treasures, as you do, and the largest beryl discovered in Ethiopia, I long to be your slave and I long to shake your pillows high and soft and to lave your feet in nard and to dry them with my kisses, kiss after kiss until they are dry.”

Lucius struck a hard blow on the gong. Caleb and Tarrar appeared.

“Call the guards,” Lucius commanded. “And drag this woman away if she does not go.”

“I am going,” said Tamyris. “But, when I am dead, O Lucius, burnt out with love, I shall haunt you and my ghost will twine around you, without your being able to prevent it, and I shall suck your soul from your lips﻿ ﻿… until I have you inside me﻿ ﻿… inside me.”

===Chinese===
「卢基乌斯，我渴望做你的奴隶。我，塔米里斯，著名的名妓，拥有和你一样的珍宝，还有埃塞俄比亚（Ethiopia）发现的最大一颗绿柱石；我渴望做你的奴隶，渴望把你的枕头拍得又高又软，用甘松香汤（nard）洗你的脚，再用我的亲吻把它们吻干，一个吻接一个吻，直到吻干为止。」

卢基乌斯重重敲了一记锣。卡莱布和塔拉尔出现了。

「叫卫兵来，」卢基乌斯命令道。「她要是不走，就把这女人拖出去。」

「我这就走，」塔米里斯说。「可是，等我死了，哦卢基乌斯，被爱火烧尽之后，我会来纠缠你，我的魂魄会缠绕着你，你拦也拦不住，我要从你的唇上吸走你的灵魂……直到把你吸进我的身体里……我的身体里。」

===Original===
“Gracious lady,” said Caleb, obsequiously, “the rain has ceased and your litter waits.”

“I am going,” said Tamyris. “The Prince of Numidia expects me. He has come with twenty swimming elephants, over the sea and straight across the lake, to love me. I am giving an orgy tonight, just to amuse him. Lucius, if you call on me tonight, we will tie up the Prince of Numidia and tickle the soles of his feet till he dies of laughing. Will you come?”

“You lie,” said Lucius. “There is no prince come to see you and there are no swimming elephants. You weary me. Go away, or I shall have you scourged from my presence with long whips.”

“I am going,” said Tamyris. “But, at a moment when you are not thinking of it, I shall bewitch you. Then you, without knowing it, will drink a philtre which I have prepared for you; and you will come to me and I shall embrace you. And in my embrace *you shall know what otherwise would have always remained a secret to you*. I am going.”

===Chinese===
「仁慈的夫人，」卡莱布殷勤地说，「雨已经停了，您的肩舆在等着呢。」

「我这就走，」塔米里斯说。「努米底亚（Numidia）王子在等我。他带着二十头会游泳的大象，渡过大海、径直蹚过湖面，来爱我。今晚我要办一场狂欢会，专为给他取乐。卢基乌斯，你今夜要是来找我，咱们就把努米底亚王子捆起来，挠他的脚心，挠到他笑死为止。你来不来？」

「你撒谎，」卢基乌斯说。「没有什么王子来看你，也没有什么会游泳的大象。你让我厌烦。走开，不然我叫人用长鞭把你从我面前抽出去。」

「我这就走，」塔米里斯说。「可会在你想不到的时候，我对你施巫。到那时，你会不知不觉喝下我为你备好的春药；你会来找我，我会拥抱你。在我的怀抱里，*你将知晓那原本会对你永远保密的东西*。我走了。」

===Original===
---

That night Lucius went to Tamyris.

But he returned, the next morning, disillusionized and disappointed.

===Chinese===
---

当夜，卢基乌斯去了塔米里斯那里。

可第二天早晨他回来了，幻灭而失望。

## XIII / 十三

===Original===
“My son,” said old Thrasyllus, sitting beside his couch, “do you intend always to cherish your illness and longing, like a serpent that devours you, bone and flesh? The sibyl of Rhacotis merely guessed your own thoughts. The holy Amphris could explain nothing more than that many, who resemble one another, mean only one in the dream. After that, what could your credulity imagine that a crafty hetaira would make you guess in her embrace? The name of that one man? The name of the pirate? The place where he is hiding Ilia?﻿ ﻿… One pirate?﻿ ﻿… Who could have stolen her?”

“I don’t know,” said Lucius, wearily.

“My poor, sick boy,” said the tutor, “no one knows and no one will ever know. She has disappeared. If she has not been kidnapped by pirates, she is drowned. Did you not visit the slave-markets in Rome on purpose to find her? Have you not done the same thing here, in Alexandria? She is not to be found. Forget her, my son. Try to get better. If no other woman can cure you, let some other power than love cure you. Amphris mentioned wisdom. There is wisdom. Seek it here, in the land of wisdom. This city, my son, is a sinful city, though it is fair to look upon. This city is as Tamyris herself: it is a wanton among cities. There is no more wisdom in this city, notwithstanding the Museum, notwithstanding the Serapeum, notwithstanding the dreams of Canopus, which die away in orgies. In this city I have met none save merchants, usurers and venal women. This magnificent city is a venal city. Even the philosophers here are avaricious and venal. Even the prophets demand a talent for their divinations. The power of money holds sway here and no longer wisdom. Let us go farther. There is wisdom left in Egypt. And in the wisdom which we shall find you will be cured. Listen, my son: there is the sacred word of the Kabbala, which Moses himself received from the godhead on Mount Sinai. That word has never been graven on tables of stone, but Moses whispered it to his sons and those sons to theirs. It is the key to happiness. He who utters it has the power to avoid suffering and to know all that can be known on earth. I have sought for it, in the Museum, in the Serapeum, here and at Canopus. While you lay sorrowing on your couch, my son, I have held converse with priests and with philosophers, with prophets. I am persuaded that I shall not find the word in Alexandria.”

===Chinese===
「我的孩子，」老特拉叙卢斯坐在他的卧榻旁说，「你打算永远抱着你的病与渴望不放吗？它像一条蛇，连骨带肉地吞噬着你。拉科提斯（Rhacotis）的女巫不过是猜中了你自己心里想的事。圣洁的安弗里斯至多解释出：梦中许多彼此相像的人，只意味着一个。此后，还能让你的轻信再想象些什么，让一个狡黠的名妓在她的怀抱里让你猜出些什么？那一个人的名字？那海盗的名字？他藏匿伊利娅的地方？……一个海盗？……谁能把她劫走呢？」

「我不知道。」卢基乌斯疲惫地说。

「我可怜的、病着的孩子，」导师说，「没有人知道，也永远不会有人知道。她失踪了。她若不是被海盗劫走，就是溺死了。你不是特意寻遍了罗马的奴隶市场去找她吗？在这里，在亚历山大里亚，你不是又做了同样的事吗？找不到的。忘了她吧，我的孩子。想法子好起来吧。若没有别的女人能治好你，那就让爱之外的别的力量来治你。安弗里斯提到过智慧。智慧是有的。到智慧之邦来寻它吧。这座城，我的孩子，是一座罪孽之城，尽管它外表美丽。这座城就像塔米里斯本人：它是众城中的一个荡妇。这座城里再没有智慧，尽管有博学园，尽管有萨拉皮雍（Serapeum），尽管有卡诺珀斯的那些梦——而梦都在狂欢里消散。在这座城里，我遇见的无非商人、放贷者和拜金的女人。这座浮华的城是一座拜金的城。连这里的哲学家都又贪又俗。连先知卜一卦都索要一个塔兰同。金钱的权势在此称雄，智慧已不再。我们往远处去吧。埃及还有智慧留存。在我们将寻得的智慧里，你会痊愈的。听着，我的孩子：有卡巴拉（Kabbala）的神圣之言，是摩西（Moses）本人在西奈山（Mount Sinai）上从神那里领受的。那言辞从未刻上石版，摩西只把它悄悄传给儿子们，儿子们又传给他们的儿子。它是幸福的钥匙。诵出它的人便有能力避开苦难，知晓世上可知的一切。我寻找过它，在博学园，在萨拉皮雍，在这里，在卡诺珀斯。当你卧在榻上哀伤的时候，我的孩子，我曾与祭司和哲学家们、与先知们交谈。我确信，在亚历山大里亚我找不到那言辞。」

===Original===
“But where will you find it, Thrasyllus?”

The tutor stared before him:

“Perhaps farther on,” he said. “Perhaps at Memphis. Let us go to Memphis. If I do not find the word at Memphis, I shall look for it farther still. Let us sail up the Nile, to Thebes, to Ethiopia. Let us go to the pillars of Sesostris. Something tells me that we shall find it﻿ ﻿… and that you will be cured, my son. But let us go.”

Lucius approved and the departure was decided. Thereupon Master Ghizla and Caleb had a long talk on “business,” after which Caleb asked for an interview with Lucius, which was granted and at which Uncle Catullus and Thrasyllus were present.

===Chinese===
「那您到哪里去找呢，特拉叙卢斯？」

导师凝望着前方：

「也许在更远处，」他说。「也许在孟斐斯（Memphis）。我们到孟斐斯去吧。若在孟斐斯找不到那言辞，我就再往远处找。我们溯尼罗河而上，去底比斯（Thebes），去埃塞俄比亚。我们到塞索斯特里斯（Sesostris）石柱去吧。有什么在告诉我，我们会找到它……而你会痊愈的，我的孩子。走吧。」

卢基乌斯同意了，启程之事就这么定了。于是吉兹拉老板和卡莱布就「生意」长谈了一番；随后卡莱布求见卢基乌斯，蒙了准许，卡图卢斯叔父和特拉叙卢斯也在座。

===Original===
“Noble lords,” Caleb began, “I should like to speak to you in your own interest. The question, noble lords, is this: I understand from the most learned Master Thrasyllus that there is a plan on foot to leave Alexandria and to travel over Memphis to Ethiopia, as far as the pillars of Sesostris. That will certainly be a fine journey; and all great lords take that road. But permit me, your servant, to give you a piece of advice, in your own interest, noble lords, in your own interest. My advice is this: hire from me and my brother Ghizla a comfortable and spacious Nile barge, a thalamegus, not only to ascend the Nile in, but also to live in, so far as possible, because﻿—spoken without slander, noble lords, spoken without slander!﻿—the diversoria which you will find at Hermopolis, at Leontopolis, ay, even at Memphis and Thebes are﻿ ﻿… *bad*, are all *bad*, not to be compared with our far-famed Hermes House, O my honoured benefactors! No, they are unclean hovels, standing on the edges of marshes, without any modern conveniences; and, though you have your own cook, you would not even find any unpolluted wells there, not to speak of wine, and would never have a good meal again, O my Lord Catullus! Therefore, O my patrons, hire our Nile thalamegus, in which you can live with a small following, with a few slaves; leave the other slaves here, with the greater part of your splendid equipment; and allow me﻿—if you have been satisfied, O my Lord Lucius, with my conduct at Alexandria and Canopus﻿—to be your guide, at the head of your own escort, and to remove all difficulties from your path. I know the whole of Egypt! I have already conducted numbers of noble lords, ay, to the sources of the Nile, to those most mysterious sources! We will take tents with us and hire camels, when necessary, but take my advice﻿ ﻿… and never alight at any other Egyptian diversoria, except our Hermes House, for they are all *bad, bad, bad*﻿ ﻿… indescribably bad, O my noble lords!”

===Chinese===
「诸位大人，」卡莱布开口道，「我想为诸位的利益进一言。事情是这样的，诸位大人：我从最博学的特拉叙卢斯大师那里得知，诸位有意离开亚历山大里亚，取道孟斐斯前往埃塞俄比亚，远至塞索斯特里斯石柱。那必定是一次壮游；凡是贵人大人都走那条路。但请容许我——您的仆人——给诸位提个忠告，为诸位自己的利益着想，诸位大人，为您自己的利益。我的忠告是：向我和我兄弟吉兹拉租一条舒适宽敞的尼罗游船，一条泰拉美戈斯，不光用来溯尼罗河而上，还尽量住在船上，因为——这话不带诽谤，诸位大人，不带诽谤！——赫尔摩波利斯（Hermopolis）、莱昂托波利斯（Leontopolis），乃至孟斐斯和底比斯的那些客栈，都……*糟*，全都*糟*，没法跟我们远近闻名的赫尔墨斯之家（Hermes House）相比，哦我尊敬的恩主们！不，那是些不干净的窝棚，搭在沼泽边上，毫无时新的便利；就算自带厨子，诸位在那里连一口没被污染的井都找不着，更别提酒了，从此再也吃不上一顿好饭，哦我的卡图卢斯大人！所以，哦我的主顾们，租下我们的尼罗泰拉美戈斯吧，诸位可以带着少量随从、几个奴隶住在船上；其余的奴隶留在此地，连同诸位大部头的华美行装；再请容许我——哦我的卢基乌斯大人，若您对我在亚历山大里亚和卡诺珀斯的当差还算满意——做诸位的向导，统领诸位自己的护卫，为诸位扫清路上的一切阻碍。整个埃及我都熟！我已经引导过许多位贵人大人，是啊，直到尼罗河的源头，那些最神秘的源头！我们会带上帐篷，必要时雇骆驼，但请听我的忠告……千万别在任何别的埃及客栈落脚，除了我们赫尔墨斯之家，因为它们全都*糟、糟、糟*……糟得难以言表，哦我尊贵的大人们！」

===Original===
“Caleb,” said Lucius, “I was just about to propose to you what you are proposing to me, that you should be our guide to the pillars of Sesostris and hire me a barge to sail up the Nile.”

“O my lords!” cried Caleb, overjoyed and obviously relieved. “How glad I am of that! For now I am convinced that you will be comfortable and travel pleasantly and that you, O my Lord Catullus, will dine as you have been wont to do here﻿ ﻿… especially as we shall not forget to take our own wines on board, the purple Mareotis wine, thick as ink, and the topaz-yellow liqueur of Napata.”

“But is the last really necessary, Caleb?” asked Uncle Catullus, mischievously. “After all, we are going to Ethiopia!”

“And on the way, my lord? Before we reach Ethiopia? And above all let me also explain that the Ethiopian liqueurs﻿ ﻿… must first descend the Nile, to acquire the perfume and the rich flavour which they do *not* possess in Ethiopia itself.”

===Chinese===
「卡莱布，」卢基乌斯说，「我正要向你提你向我提的事：请你做我们去塞索斯特里斯石柱的向导，替我雇一条溯尼罗河而上的游船。」

「哦，大人们！」卡莱布喊道，喜出望外，分明如释重负。「我太高兴了！这下我确信诸位一路会舒舒服服、快快活活，而您，哦我的卡图卢斯大人，会吃得同在这里一样好……何况我们不会忘了把我们自己的酒搬上船：紫红的马雷奥蒂斯酒，稠得像墨汁，还有黄玉色的纳帕塔甜酒。」

「可后面那种当真必要吗，卡莱布？」卡图卢斯叔父促狭地问。「别忘了，我们是去埃塞俄比亚呀！」

「那路上呢，大人？还没到埃塞俄比亚的路上呢？况且容我说明：埃塞俄比亚的甜酒……必得先顺尼罗河而下走一遭，才得着那香气和醇味——在埃塞俄比亚本地，它们可*没有*这等味道。」

===Original===
“If only they don’t lose that perfume, Caleb, when they ascend the Nile again!” said Uncle Catullus, jestingly.

“I shall see to *that*, my lord,” said Caleb, who saw through Uncle Catullus quite as plainly as Uncle Catullus saw through Caleb. “I’ll see to *that*. You just leave it to *me*.”

“We are leaving everything to you, Caleb. Get the barge ready for tomorrow,” said Lucius.

“Then we shall go up the Nile next day, my lord,” said Caleb, happy and delighted.

And he retired with salaam upon salaam.

===Chinese===
「但愿它们再溯尼罗河而上时别把那香气丢了就好，卡莱布！」卡图卢斯叔父打趣道。

「这个*我*会留神的，大人，」卡莱布说——他把卡图卢斯叔父看得透亮，正如卡图卢斯叔父把他看得透亮。「*我*会留神的。您只管交给*我*。」

「一切都交给你了，卡莱布。明天就把船备好。」卢基乌斯说。

「那么第二天我们就溯尼罗河而上了，大人。」卡莱布说，欢天喜地。

他行了一礼又一礼，退了下去。

===Original===
And Master Ghizla, in the palm-garden, pretending to be busy with the little canal, but in reality full of eagerness to know the result of Caleb’s advice, whispered:

“I say!﻿ ﻿… Brother!﻿ ﻿…”

“Yes?”

“Well, Caleb, well?” asked Ghizla, anxiously and looking a little pale.

“They’re hiring the thalamegus﻿ ﻿… they’re alighting at no other diversorium﻿ ﻿… they’re sleeping in *our* tents, they’ll travel with *our* camels and.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
棕榈园里，吉兹拉老板假装忙着照看小水渠，其实一心急着想打听卡莱布进言的结果，悄声问：

「喂！……兄弟！……」

「嗯？」

「怎么样，卡莱布，怎么样？」吉兹拉追问，急得脸色微微发白。

「他们租泰拉美戈斯了……他们不进别家客栈……他们睡*我们的*帐篷，他们用*我们的*骆驼，还有……」

===Original===
“Well, Caleb, and what else?” asked Ghizla, rubbing his hands.

“They’re drinking *our* wines﻿ ﻿… all the way to Napata!”

“Where *you’ll* pretend to lay in a fresh stock of liqueurs?”

“You leave that to me, Brother Ghizla, you just leave it to me!”

“May the gods bless you, Brother Caleb; may Thoth, Hermes and Serapis bless you! Quick, let us look in the cellars if we have enough in store!”

There came a sudden shower, as though poured from an urn in the sky by an invisible water-god; and the two brothers, with their garments girdled up, rushed bare-legged through the puddles of their palm-garden to their wine-cellars, which lay warm as stone cupolas in the sun, or else were kept cool with double walls filled with snow.

===Chinese===
「还有呢，卡莱布，还有什么？」吉兹拉搓着手问。

「他们喝*我们的*酒……一路喝到纳帕塔！」

「到了那边，*你*再假装新进一批甜酒？」

「这个你交给我，吉兹拉兄弟，你只管交给我！」

「愿诸神赐福于你，卡莱布兄弟；愿托特（Thoth）、赫尔墨斯（Hermes）和萨拉皮斯赐福于你！快，去酒窖里瞧瞧咱们的存货够不够！」

忽然来了一阵骤雨，像一位看不见的水神从天上的瓮中倾下；兄弟俩掖起衣袍，赤着腿蹚过棕榈园的一个个水洼，奔向他们的酒窖——那些酒窖在日头下暖如石砌的穹顶，另一些则夹墙填雪，保着阴凉。

## XIV / 十四

===Original===
In the still and silent night, the Delta lay flooded by the kindly waters of the sacred river. From the Canopic to the Sebennytic mouth, from the Phatmetic along the Mendesian to the Pelusiac mouth, the Delta lay flooded: one still and silent sea in the night, a wide, silver sheet of water without a ripple, stretching farther than the eye could reach in the soft-falling sheen of the full moon. Between the river-mouths the canals lay in streaks of silver light, full of water to their edges. Past the blossoming reeds, past the blossoming lotuses and water-lilies, the great barge glided up the stream as in a vision.

There was not a sound amid the silence but the dripping from the oars.

The night was muffled, wide and immense. It was as though the moon, up above, had inundated the sky, even as the flood the sacred land below. It was as though the flood of moonshine were drenching the sacred sky also with a calm, unrippled sea, but a sea of light. The night was like a noiseless, silvery day; the night was like a shadow of the day. In that inundation of the light of heaven the stars paled, innumerous, like a silvery powder sprinkled by the moonshine. There lay the lake of Butos, wide and mystic and gleaming. Island emerged after island. Palms stood in clusters, stately, motionless and delicate. A shrine appeared and vanished as the dream-barge glided down a bend of the canal. Country-mansions stood peacefully linked together. There were taller dykes and patches of golden, shadowy wheat. Sheaves of corn looked like the images of gods, reverence-compelling, ranged in order beside one another, against the wall of a barn. A peculiar scent was wafted by, a fresh aroma as of always moist flowers.

===Chinese===
静静的、无声的夜里，三角洲浸在圣河仁慈的水中。从卡诺珀斯河口到塞本尼提克（Sebennytic）河口，从法特米提克（Phatmetic）河口、沿门德西安（Mendesian）河口直到佩卢西亚克（Pelusiac）河口，三角洲一片汪洋：夜里一片静默的海，宽阔的银色水面不起一丝涟漪，在满月柔泻的光华里，伸展到目力不及的远方。各河口之间，运河泛着一道道银光，水满齐岸。大游船掠过开花的芦苇，掠过盛放的莲花与睡莲，溯流而上，恍如在梦境里。

寂静中只有桨上滴落的水声。

夜是沉静的、宽阔而无边的。仿佛天上的明月淹没了天空，一如洪水淹没了下面的圣土。仿佛月光之洪也把神圣的天空浸在一片平静无澜的海里——那是光的海。夜像无声的银色白昼；夜像白昼的影子。在那天光的漫淹之中，星辰黯淡了，数不胜数，像月光撒下的一层银粉。布托斯湖（Butos）横卧在那里，宽阔、神秘而闪光。岛屿一座接一座浮现。棕榈成丛而立，端庄、静穆、纤秀。一座神祠随着梦之舟滑过运河的弯道而隐现。乡村的宅邸宁静地彼此相连。还有更高的堤坝和一片片金黄而朦胧的麦田。一捆捆麦束像一尊尊神像，令人肃然，整整齐齐地挨靠着谷仓的墙。一阵奇异的香气飘过，一种清新的芬芳，像常润不谢的花。

===Original===
The outline of a village came into view. And hamlet joined itself to hamlet, with shrines and mansions in between. Suddenly, farther up, in the sea of glory, in the sea of light, huge needles rose on high from the ground, with quivering lines, and became lost in the midst of light.

Thrasyllus standing by Cora on the forecastle pointed:

“The obelisks of Sais.”

She turned, with a start, and was silent. The barge that afternoon had left Naucratis along the canals which seam the Saitic nome, or province. They were now nearing the capital, Sais, the capital of all Lower Egypt. They already saw the Anubis Avenue. And suddenly, at a bend, between very tall reeds blossoming with tassels and bowing before the barge, Thrasyllus pointed:

“The temple of Isis-Neith.”

===Chinese===
一座村庄的轮廓映入眼帘。村落连着村落，中间点缀着神祠与宅邸。忽然，在更上游的地方，在光华的海、光的海里，巨大的针从地面高高拔起，线条颤动，隐没进光的深处。

特拉叙卢斯站在船头的科拉身旁，抬手一指：

「塞易斯（Sais）的方尖碑。」

她一惊，转过身去，沉默了。那天下午，游船离开诺克拉提斯（Naucratis），沿着纵横交织塞易斯诺姆（Saitic nome）的运河行来。此刻他们正接近都城塞易斯——整个下埃及的都城。他们已经望见阿努比斯大道。忽然，在一处河湾，在开着穗花、向游船俯身的高高的芦苇之间，特拉叙卢斯抬手一指：

「伊西斯-奈斯神庙。」

===Original===
There were sphinxes: they seemed to lift their basalt heads in prayer to the moon and the sky. Lamps and lights twinkled like stars. The thalamegus hove to; orders rang out; the sailors moored the vessel.

“The temple of Isis-Neith,” Thrasyllus repeated to Lucius, who approached with Catullus and Caleb.

They were all arrayed in long, white-linen robes. Cora also was similarly clad, in a long, white, close fitting linen robe. She wore a wreath of wheatears and lotus-flowers at her temples. For it was the Night of the Glowing Lights, the Feast of the Burning Lamps.

===Chinese===
那里立着斯芬克斯：它们仿佛举起玄武岩的头，向月亮和天空祈祷。灯火如星辰闪烁。泰拉美戈斯停了船；号令声响起；水手们把船系缆泊好。

「伊西斯-奈斯神庙。」特拉叙卢斯对走近来的卢基乌斯重复道；同来的还有卡图卢斯和卡莱布。

众人都穿着白色的亚麻长袍。科拉也穿着同样的衣服：一件白色紧身的长亚麻袍。她鬓间戴着麦穗与莲花编的花环。因为这是光辉之夜，燃灯节（Feast of the Burning Lamps）。

===Original===
“Nemu-Pha is waiting for me in the temple,” said Thrasyllus. “I wrote to him and he has consented to receive me. He is the high-priest of Isis; and tonight he receives those who come to consult him. I thought, Lucius, of going alone. Nemu-Pha is one of the holiest prophets in Egypt. One word from him can perhaps enable me to guess much. But, if you accompany me, with only a single thought in your sick brain, you would break the mystic thread which might be woven between the high-priest’s spirit and mine. Let me go alone. I have no other care than your happiness﻿ ﻿… even though we are not agreed on the form which it should take.

“Go, Thrasyllus,” said Lucius.

“I don’t think that I shall go on shore,” said Uncle Catullus. “The Night of the Glowing Lights and the Feast of the Burning Lamps leave me cold. It is colourless and cheerless; it will be a spectral orgy. I am too old and fat, Lucius, for spectral orgies. Go on shore alone and amuse yourself as you may.”

===Chinese===
「内穆-法（Nemu-Pha）在神庙里等我，」特拉叙卢斯说。「我写信给他，他答应接见我。他是伊西斯的祭司长；今夜他接见前来求问的人。我原想，卢基乌斯，独自去。内穆-法是埃及最圣洁的先知之一。他的一句话，也许能让我猜出许多。可你若陪我去，你病中的头脑里哪怕只有一个念头，也会扯断或许会在祭司长的精神与我的精神之间织起的那根神秘之线。让我一个人去吧。我一心所系的只有你的幸福……尽管对幸福该是什么样子，我们意见并不一致。」

「去吧，特拉叙卢斯。」卢基乌斯说。

「我看我就不上岸了，」卡图卢斯叔父说。「什么光辉之夜、燃灯节，引不起我的兴致。素淡没味，阴气森森；那将是一场鬼影般的狂欢。我太老太胖了，卢基乌斯，经不起鬼影般的狂欢。你自己上岸去，怎么开心怎么玩吧。」

===Original===
Lucius assembled his slaves, male and female. They were all in long, white robes, the women wreathed with ears of wheat and lotus-flowers.

“You are all free tonight,” said Lucius. “You have a night of liberty. Until sunrise you belong to yourselves. Go your ways and do whatever you please.”

Rufus handed each a small sum of money. The slaves bowed low and disappeared, between the palms, in the direction of the moonlit, twinkling city.

Only a guard of sailors kept watch on the barge. Uncle Catullus retired to his cabin. Tarrar also did not wish to go on shore and remained to sleep at his master’s threshold. The Feast of Isis made many shudder who were not accustomed from their youth to its shivery mysticism.

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯把男女奴隶们召集起来。他们都穿着白色长袍，女奴们戴着麦穗与莲花的花环。

「你们今夜全都是自由之身，」卢基乌斯说。「你们有一夜的自由。日出之前，你们属于自己。各走各的路，愿意做什么就做什么。」

鲁弗斯发给每人一小笔钱。奴隶们深深鞠躬，随即消失在棕榈树间，朝月光下灯火闪烁的城去了。

只有一队水手留在船上守望。卡图卢斯叔父回舱房去了。塔拉尔也不愿上岸，留在主人门槛边睡觉。伊西斯的节日让许多人不由得发抖——那些自幼不曾习惯它阴森神秘气氛的人。

===Original===
Thrasyllus had gone. Lucius also went on shore. He saw Cora hesitating under the palm-trees while the other women slaves had already gone gaily to enjoy their night of liberty:

“Why don’t you join your companions, Cora?” asked Lucius.

“My lord,” Cora replied, “if you permit me, I would rather stay here.”

“You are free tonight.”

“What should I do with liberty, my lord?”

“You can do what you please, go to the temple and see the veiled Isis﻿ ﻿… and enjoy yourself as and with whom you choose.”

===Chinese===
特拉叙卢斯走了。卢基乌斯也上了岸。他看见科拉在棕榈树下犹豫，别的女奴早已欢欢喜喜享受她们的自由之夜去了：

「你怎么不和同伴们一起去，科拉？」卢基乌斯问。

「大人，」科拉答道，「若您容许，我宁愿留在这里。」

「你今夜是自由的。」

「我要自由做什么呢，大人？」

「你愿意做什么就做什么，去神庙看看蒙着面纱的伊西斯……跟你自己挑中的人，怎么开心怎么来。」

===Original===
She cast down her eyes and blushed.

“There is a general holiday tonight,” continued Lucius, “for slaves male and female.”

She folded her hands as though in prayer:

“My lord,” she begged, “suffer me to remain here, near the barge. I am afraid of liberty and of the big city.”

“Do as you please,” said Lucius.

===Chinese===
她垂下眼睛，红了脸。

「今夜是普天同庆的节日，」卢基乌斯接着说，「男女奴隶一律有份。」

她双手合拢，像在祈祷：

「大人，」她恳求道，「容我留在这里吧，就在船近旁。我怕自由，也怕大城市。」

「随你的便吧。」卢基乌斯说。

===Original===
He went on alone. Loneliness sent a shiver through him because of this strange night which was like day. A white melancholy emanated from his soul. He felt aimless. He would have preferred to accompany Thrasyllus. He would not have minded going to bed. He had almost invited Cora to accompany him to Sais, but did not think it suited to his dignity.

He went alone, in his white raiment, in the bountiful moonlight. How strange the night was, all white and trembling. He approached the town. There was nothing but the monotonous rattling of the sistra carried by the long-robed pilgrims who walked in procession to the temple. All the houses along the road were lit with the lamps burning at the doors and windows, vessels full of oil with burning wicks. It was a strange pale-yellow twinkling in the moonshine. It was like a funeral ceremony. For it commemorated the night on which Isis had collected the scattered limbs of her brother and husband Osiris, murdered and quartered by Typhon and scattered all over Egypt.

The procession streamed to the temple. Along the road, the hierodules, the priestesses, danced to a monotonous chant, hand in hand, in a long row. They threw a laugh to the numberless strangers who had come to Sais, for that night. The strangers laughed back and picked out the priestesses; and they withdrew together, first to the temple, then farther away.

===Chinese===
他独自往前走。这白昼般的奇异之夜，让孤独感穿透他，使他一阵战栗。一种苍白的忧郁从他灵魂里渗出。他漫无目标。他本想陪特拉叙卢斯同去。他也不妨上床睡觉。他差点开口邀科拉陪他去塞易斯，又觉得那不合自己的身份。

他独自走着，一身白袍，浴在丰盈的月光里。这夜多么奇异，一片苍白而颤动。他走近城去。一路只有长袍朝圣者手中叉铃（sistrum）单调的嘚嘚声，他们列队走向神庙。沿路的房屋全都亮着，门边窗上燃着灯——盛满油的器皿，芯子在燃烧。月光里是一种奇异的淡黄闪烁。像一场葬仪。因为这纪念的，正是伊西斯收殓她兄长兼丈夫俄西里斯散落肢体的那一夜——他被堤丰杀害、肢解，抛散在全埃及。

人流涌向神庙。一路上，神妓（hierodule）们——那些女祭司——手拉手排成长列，和着单调的吟唱起舞。她们向那夜涌进塞易斯的无数外乡人抛去笑靥。外乡人报以笑，挑中哪个神妓；她们便双双退去，先到神庙，再往更远处。

===Original===
Three hierodules laughed to Lucius. They danced round him. He did not wish to seem uncivil; also he felt very forlorn. He just laughed back, wearily and kindly.

“Shall we come with you?” asked one of the hierodules.

“As you please,” said Lucius. “Are you going to the temple?”

“If you wish.”

They walked in front of him and beside him. They wore white, close-fitting robes, with lotus-flowers and ears of wheat in their hair. They were gentle and civil and obliging and young, like three young children.

===Chinese===
三个神妓向卢基乌斯笑。她们绕着他舞。他不愿显得失礼；何况他也实在孤单。他便也回以笑，疲惫而和善。

「我们陪你好吗？」一个神妓问。

「随你们，」卢基乌斯说。「你们去神庙吗？」

「你愿意的话。」

她们走在他前面和身旁。她们穿着白色紧身袍，发间簪着莲花与麦穗。她们温柔、斯文、殷勤而年轻，像三个小孩子。

===Original===
The white multitude streamed along the streets. The obelisks of the dromos came into view. The temple rose gigantic and mysterious, with numbers of square buildings and terraces stacked one above the other. There were rows of gigantic pylons, which lost themselves in the moonlit night. The monotonous melody of the sistra rattled on every side; on every side the lamps twinkled. Lucius felt within him an immeasurable melancholy, because of life and because of death, because of people and because of himself.

The hierodules led the way. They were kind and courteous, glad at meeting this amiable stranger, to whom they would be obliging, as their duty prescribed that night.

They entered the pronaos and secos. In the immensity of the pillared spaces the countless sistra rattled eerily, producing a vibration which was no longer music: it was as though the pylons and pillars themselves were rattling, as though the very earth were rattling.

===Chinese===
白色的人流沿街涌动。圣道上的方尖碑映入眼帘。神庙巨大而神秘地耸起，无数的方形殿宇与平台层层相叠。一排排巨大的塔门，隐没进月光之夜。叉铃单调的旋律四面嘚嘚作响；四面灯火闪烁。卢基乌斯心里涌起一种无边的忧郁，为了生，为了死，为了众人，也为了他自己。

神妓们在前面引路。她们善良而有礼，庆幸遇见这位和气的陌生人，愿意为他殷勤效劳——那是她们当夜的职分。

他们走进前殿和内殿（secos）。在柱厅的宏阔里，数不清的叉铃嘚嘚作响，森然可怖，激起一种已不再是音乐的震颤：仿佛是塔门和石柱自己在震响，仿佛是大地本身在震响。

===Original===
Suddenly Lucius felt a cold shiver pass through him. In the holy of holies rose the veiled Isis. It was an immense statue, five fathoms high and surrounded entirely with a silvery film, seamed with hieroglyphics. Above the image, on the architrave, was written:

> I Am Who Have Been,
> Who Am
> And Who Shall Be;
> And No One Has Lifted My Veil.

Around the image shone thousands of burning vessels, of glowing lamps. There was a mist of light and a smoke of incense. And round about the image there was the incessant dance of the hierodules and the worship of the sacrificing priests, all the night through. And ever, like an obsession, there was the rattle of the sistra, as though the whole immense temple were rattling.

===Chinese===
忽然，卢基乌斯觉得一阵寒颤穿过全身。至圣之所里耸立着蒙纱的伊西斯。那是一尊巨大的神像，高五噚，周身罩着一层银色的薄膜，缀满纵横的象形文字。神像上方的大梁上镌着：

> 我是昔在者，
> 今在者，
> 亦是将来的永在者；
> 从未有人揭开我的面纱。

神像四周，成千上万只燃烧的灯盏辉映着。有光的雾，有香的烟。神像周围，神妓们不停地舞蹈，献祭的祭司们不停地礼拜，彻夜不休。而叉铃之声如魔缠身，嘚嘚不息，仿佛整座巨大的神庙都在震响。

===Original===
Lucius, led by the three women, offered his sacrifice at one of the numberless altars. The priest pronounced the sacred words and Lucius poured forth the libation and paid his gold coin.

He felt desperately unhappy.

“Sir,” asked one of the women, “do you wish us all three to accompany you to one of the temple-chambers? Or would you have two of us go away?”

He laughed softly at their polite manners, like those of young and well-brought-up children. He gave a melancholy glance:

“I am unwell, I am very unwell,” he said. “I think I will go home alone.”

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯由三位女子引着，在无数祭坛中的一座前献了祭。祭司诵念神圣的言辞，卢基乌斯行了奠酒，付了金币。

他觉得绝望地不快活。

「先生，」其中一个女子问，「您要我们三个都陪您到神庙的房里去吗？还是要我们中的两个走开？」

她们那份教养良好的小孩子似的礼貌，逗得他轻轻一笑。他投去忧郁的一瞥：

「我不舒服，我很不舒服，」他说。「我想我还是独自回去。」

===Original===
“Your eyes are full of pain, sir,” said one of the hierodules.

And one of the others said:

“Cannot we comfort you and cure you?”

Lucius shook his head.

“Then let us lead you home,” said the third.

===Chinese===
「您的眼睛里满是痛苦，先生。」一个神妓说。

另一个说：

「我们不能安慰您、治好您吗？」

卢基乌斯摇了摇头。

「那让我们送您回家吧。」第三个说。

===Original===
They left the temple.

“I live on the river,” said Lucius. “I came in a thalamegus.”

They walked beside him, like shades. When they reached the barge, Lucius said:

“I am at home here. Let me thank you and pay you. May holy Isis protect you!”

“May holy Isis cure you, sir!” said the hierodules.

===Chinese===
他们离开了神庙。

「我住在河上，」卢基乌斯说。「我乘泰拉美戈斯来的。」

她们走在他身旁，像三道影子。到了船边，卢基乌斯说：

「我到家了。让我谢谢你们，付你们酬劳。愿神圣的伊西斯保佑你们！」

「愿神圣的伊西斯治好您，先生！」神妓们说。

===Original===
He gave them a gold coin apiece. They disappeared in the night, like shades. But under the palm-trees was another shade. It was Cora.

“I am not well,” said Lucius. “I came back.”

“Do you wish to go to bed, my lord?” asked Cora.

“No, I should not be able to sleep,” replied Lucius. “This night is strange and unreal. I will lie here under the trees.”

===Chinese===
他给了她们每人一枚金币。她们消失在夜色里，像影子。可棕榈树下还有一道影子。那是科拉。

「我不舒服，」卢基乌斯说。「我回来了。」

「您想上床歇息吗，大人？」科拉问。

「不，我睡不着，」卢基乌斯答道。「这夜太奇异、太不真实了。我就躺在这树下。」

===Original===
“I will leave you, my lord.”

“No, stay,” he said. “I am ill and I feel lonely. Stay.”

“Suffer me to fetch you a cloak and a pillow, my lord.”

“I thank you.”

She disappeared into the barge and returned with the pillow and cloak. She covered him up and pushed the pillow under his head.

===Chinese===
「那我就退下了，大人。」

「不，留下，」他说。「我病着，我觉得孤单。留下吧。」

「容我去给您取一件斗篷和一个枕头，大人。」

「谢谢你。」

她走进船舱，抱着枕头和斗篷回来。她给他盖好，把枕头垫在他头下。

===Original===
“The night is strange,” he repeated, “and unreal. It is like a white day. There is no dew falling. I shall remain here till Thrasyllus comes. But do you stay. I feel ill and lonely.”

“What can I do, my lord? I may not sing: only the sistrum may sound tonight.”

“Dance to me; move in the moonlight. Can you dance without accompaniment?”

“Yes, my lord,” said Cora.

===Chinese===
「这夜真奇异，」他重复道，「不真实。像白色的白昼。露水都没有落。我就在这儿等特拉叙卢斯回来。你也留下吧。我觉得病弱，孤单。」

「我能做什么呢，大人？我不许唱歌：今夜只许叉铃作响。」

「给我跳舞吧；在月光里舞动。没有伴奏你也能跳吗？」

「能，大人。」科拉说。

===Original===
He lay under the palms. Cora danced in the open moonlight, near the tall river-reeds. She twisted and turned like a white water-nymph that had risen from the stream. She stood still, in attitudes of rapture. She adored Isis, her hands uplifted to the moon. She was very lithe and slender, very white, with white flowers and ears of wheat around her temples.

He lay without moving, watching her. And he thought his only thought: where could Ilia be? For there had not been more than one pirate.﻿ ﻿…

When, late in the night, Thrasyllus returned, he found Lucius asleep under the palms with Cora keeping vigil beside him.

===Chinese===
他躺在棕榈树下。科拉在朗朗的月光里起舞，在高高的河芦苇旁。她旋来转去，像从河里升起的白色水仙。她伫立不动，摆出狂喜的姿态。她崇奉伊西斯，双手向月亮高举。她非常柔韧纤细，非常洁白，鬓边环着白色的花与麦穗。

他一动不动地躺着，望着她。他想着他唯一的心事：伊利娅会在哪里？因为那海盗从来就只有一个。……

深夜，特拉叙卢斯回来时，发现卢基乌斯已在棕榈树下睡着，科拉守在他身旁。

===Original===
“My lord is asleep,” said Cora. And she asked, “Tell me, Thrasyllus: what did Nemu-Pha say?”

The old tutor looked gloomy. And he said:

“The wise ages have been drowned in the night of time. Egypt is Egypt no longer. Sais is Sais no longer. If wisdom still tarries here and is still to be found, I shall find it not by the sea, not in the Delta. This is the granary and the emporium of the world﻿ ﻿… but nothing more. Great Isis hides behind her veil the worthlessness and venality of her priests, whose last remaining pride is to sell in great secrecy the word, ‘Be a god unto yourself.’﻿ ﻿… That word does not satisfy me. But there is Memphis, there is Thebes. I still have hope, Cora﻿ ﻿… that I shall find the divine word which will cure him.”

===Chinese===
「大人睡着了。」科拉说。接着她问：「告诉我，特拉叙卢斯：内穆-法说了什么？」

老导师神色阴郁。他说：

「智慧的诸时代，早已溺毙在时间之夜。埃及已不复为埃及。塞易斯已不复为塞易斯。智慧若还滞留于此、还能寻到，我也不会在海边寻到，不会在三角洲。这里是世界的粮仓与商埠……仅此而已。伟大的伊西斯用面纱遮着她祭司们的卑劣与贪贿，他们剩下的最后一点骄傲，就是极秘密地出卖那句言辞：『做自己的神。』……那句言辞不能让我满足。还有孟斐斯，还有底比斯。我还有希望，科拉……希望我能找到治好他的神圣之言。」

===Original===
The old man stepped on board the barge. The night waned; yonder, in Sais, the twinkling of the Burning Lamps died away.

In the east, the light broke through, as through a bursting sluice. Long, rosy islands seemed to drift in an ocean of molten gold. A long flight of cranes, black against the golden sky, swept down to meet the dawn. Cocks crowed; and on the waters of Lake Butos the first lotus-blooms opened their white chalices. As it were crimson flowed and lay, here and there, over the silent, silver streaks of the canals, in pools of purple red.

===Chinese===
老人上了船。夜将尽；那边，塞易斯城里，燃灯的闪烁渐渐熄灭。

东方，光冲破而出，像冲破决口的闸门。一道道长长的玫瑰色岛屿，仿佛漂流在熔金的大洋里。一长列仙鹤，衬着金色的天空成了黑色，掠下去迎接黎明。鸡鸣了；布托斯湖水面上，第一批莲花张开了白色的杯盏。恍如殷红在流淌、在停驻，这里那里，漫过运河静默的银色水痕，积成一汪汪紫红。

## XIX / 十九

===Original===
It was very early one morning and Lucius was walking alone on the opposite bank of the river. In the tender dawn the vast grey lines of Memphis became visible in rose-red silhouette.

Lucius was wandering alone. Solitude had become dear to him, like rest after a severe illness, especially because he doubted his cure. He doubted; he doubted the certainty.

Did he know the truth? He was doubting now, after a sleepless night, and asking himself, did he know the truth? And, if he knew the truth, was he really cured, cured in his sick soul, cured of his suffering?

He did not know; he no longer knew anything. He wandered beside the Nile, alone, without knowing, without knowing. A dullness filled his brain, like a mist. Life was awaking on the farms with cheerful rural activity. The grain burst under the millstones; and the women on their knees rubbed with powerful palms the dough which the men beside them had already kneaded with the vigorous dance of their feet. Lucius stopped to look on; and they laughed; and he laughed back. The men danced and the women rubbed; and they laughed and were happy. A jealousy of their happiness rose hotly in the young Roman.

===Chinese===
一天清晨，天色极早，卢基乌斯独自在河对岸散步。柔和的晨曦里，孟斐斯城灰蒙蒙的庞大轮廓渐渐显出玫瑰红的剪影。

卢基乌斯独自徜徉。孤寂于他已变得亲切，如同大病之后的休养，尤其因为他怀疑自己是否痊愈。他怀疑；他怀疑这份笃定。

他知道真相吗？彻夜无眠之后，他此刻正在怀疑，扪心自问：他知道真相吗？倘若知道了真相，他又真的痊愈了吗——他那害病的灵魂痊愈了吗，他从苦难中解脱了吗？

他不知道；他什么都不知道了。他独自在尼罗河畔徜徉，一无所知，一无所知。一种混沌充塞着他的头脑，如同迷雾。田庄上的生活正随着欢快的农家劳作苏醒。谷粒在磨盘下迸裂；妇人们跪在地上，用有力的手掌揉搓着面团，身旁的男人们方才踏着欢腾的舞步将它们踩匀。卢基乌斯驻足观看；他们笑了；他也报以笑容。男人们踏着舞步，妇人们揉着面团；他们笑着，快活着。一股对这些人之快乐的妒意，在这个年轻的罗马人胸中灼灼升起。

===Original===
“Will you give me some milk?” he asked a girl who was milking a splendid, snow-white cow.

The girl handed the stranger the milk in the hollow leaf of a cyamus-plant. Lucius did not know whether to give her any money. He drank and handed back the reed goblet:

“Thank you,” he said; and she laughed and went on milking.

He gave her no money and went on. How beautiful the world was and the morning! How rosy this first light over the silvering stream! How grey and colossal the past, yonder, of that dying, sinking city! How beautiful and impressive were every form and tint! How lovely was the world! Even the people down there, those labourers, those shepherdesses, those men and women baking, had a calm rustic, idyllic beauty in their simplicity and naturalness. How good the world was and how happy people could be, if the gods did not pour grief into their hearts!

===Chinese===
“能给我一点牛奶吗？”他问一个正给一头出色的雪白母牛挤奶的姑娘。

姑娘用一片窝成盏形的睡莲叶，把牛奶递给这个外乡人。卢基乌斯不知道该不该给她钱。他喝完，把那芦苇杯似的莲盏递还给她：

“谢谢你，”他说；她笑了，继续挤奶。

他没有给她钱，继续前行。世界多美，这清晨多美！银光渐亮的河面上，这第一线晨曦多红润！那边那座正在死去、正在沉没的城池，它的往昔多灰暗、多庞大！一切形态、一切色泽都多美、多动人！世界多可爱！就连下方那些人——那些劳作者、那些牧羊女、那些烘烤食物的男女——也在质朴与自然之中透着一种恬静的田园之美、牧歌之美。世界多好，倘若诸神不把悲哀倾进人们心里，人们本可以多么幸福！

===Original===
Grief! Did he feel grief? Or had the mere thought that Ilia had proved unworthy of his great love already cured him of the disease that was grief? But was he cured and did he know?

He was approaching the hamlet of Troia. And he remembered reading in Thrasyllus’ notes that Menelaus had come past here with his band of Trojan captives and generously permitted them to settle here. They had founded their colony. Behind Troia stood a rocky mountain-range; and behold, there was the ancient quarry from which, years ago, the blocks of stone were hewn to build the pyramids, block upon block, without cement! And Lucius’ feet rattled through the curious fossils which strewed the ground like pebbles with the shapes of long lentils and pea-pods and which were thought to be the petrified remnants of the meals served to the many thousand slaves who had worked at the pyramids.

===Chinese===
悲哀！他感到悲哀吗？还是说，仅仅一个念头——伊利娅已证明配不上他伟大的爱——就已经治好了他这名为悲哀的病？可他痊愈了吗？他知道吗？

他走近特洛亚村。他记得在特拉叙卢斯的笔记里读到过，墨涅拉俄斯（Menelaus）曾带着一队特洛亚俘虏经过此地，并宽宏地允许他们在此定居。他们建立了自己的聚落。特洛亚村背后耸立着一道多石的山岭；看哪，那里便是古老的采石场，多年以前，建造金字塔的石块就是从那里凿出的，一块块垒起来，不用灰浆！卢基乌斯的脚踏得遍地奇异的化石咔啦作响，它们像鹅卵石一样散落一地，形似细长的扁豆和豌豆荚，人们说，那是当年服苦役的成千上万金字塔奴隶所食饭食的石化残渣。

===Original===
Suddenly he saw a woman. She was resting, sitting against the rocks and gazing at the rosy sky. He recognized his slave, the one with the beautiful voice, the singer, Cora.

She started when she saw him and rose and bowed low, with outstretched hands:

“Forgive me, my lord,” she stammered, “for straying so far from the barge.”

He reassured her: he was a master who did not grudge his slaves a liberty. And he asked her, in a kind voice:

“Why did you stray so far?”

“I strayed without intending it, my lord. My thoughts carried me along!”

===Chinese===
忽然，他看见一个女人。她正在歇息，倚着岩石坐在那里，凝望玫瑰色的天空。他认出了自己的女奴——那个嗓音美妙的歌女，科拉。

她见到他，吃了一惊，起身深深一躬，双手前伸：

“恕罪，主人，”她讷讷地说，“我离船走得太远了。”

他安抚她：他这个做主人的，并不吝惜给奴隶们一点自由。于是他和颜悦色地问她：

“你怎么走得这么远？”

“我不是有意走远的，主人。是我的思绪带着我走！”

===Original===
“What were you thinking of?”

“I was thinking of Cos, my dear birthplace, and whether I should ever see it again.”

“It is the birthplace of Apelles the painter and of Epicharmus the poet-philosopher and inventor of comedy. It is a place of beauty and art, is it not, Cora?”

“It is like a most charming garden, my lord. It contains the temples of aesculapius and Aphrodite. I was born there in the slave-school. I had a delightful childhood. There was a big garden in which I used to play.﻿ ﻿… Forgive me, my lord.﻿ ﻿…”

“Go on.”

“I was trained there and tended. I was bathed and carefully anointed and rubbed. This was done by the negresses. I learned to dance when I was very young. That is why I am lithe, my lord; and I hope that I dance well. But I also loved music; I sang. We had masters, who taught us to sing and play the harp, and mistresses, who taught us to dance. Dryope, who was in charge of the slave-school, was stern, but she was not unkind. My parents also were her slaves. My father was a runner and my mother was a dancer too. There were wagers when my father ran in a race; and he but seldom failed to win the prize for our mistress. She would have him flogged when he did not win the prize, but not hard, for she did not want to injure his precious body. Dryope was a good mistress to us, for my mother stopped dancing after she had once sprained her foot and Dryope nevertheless remained kind and gentle to her slave. But, when I was able to sing and dance, my lord, Dryope sold me for a big sum to a slave-dealer who was going to Rome with a number of slaves, male and female. I embraced Dryope and my parents and went with the dealer. He also was not harsh to me, because I was a valuable slave, my lord; he was not harsh to his slaves; he was careful of them as of precious merchandise. Thrasyllus bought me for you, my lord, on the slave-market in Rome; and I was proud when he paid a big sum for me after hearing my trial song and seeing my trial dance. And now﻿ ﻿… now I am happy, my lord, to belong to a master like yourself. But still my thoughts often wander to Cos, to the slaves’ quarters, to my parents, to my fellow-slaves there and to Dryope. Forgive me, my lord.”

===Chinese===
“你在想什么？”

“我在想科斯，我亲爱的出生地，想我是否还能再见它一面。”

“那是画家阿佩莱斯（Apelles）的诞生地，也是诗人哲学家、喜剧创始者埃庇卡摩斯（Epicharmus）的诞生地。那是个美与艺术之地，不是吗，科拉？”

“它就像一座最迷人的园子，主人。岛上有埃斯库拉庇乌斯（Aesculapius）和阿佛洛狄忒的神庙。我就出生在那里的奴隶学校。我有过一个快乐的童年。有一座大园子，我常在里面玩耍。……恕罪，主人。……”

“说下去。”

“我在那里受训，被人照料。有人为我沐浴，细心地为我涂膏、按摩。这些都是黑人女奴做的。我很小便学跳舞。所以我身段柔软，主人；我也希望自己跳得好。可我还爱音乐；我唱歌。我们有男师傅教我们唱歌、弹竖琴，有女师傅教我们跳舞。德律俄佩（Dryope）主管那所奴隶学校，她严厉，却不刻薄。我的父母也是她的奴隶。我父亲是个竞走的，我母亲也是个跳舞的。我父亲出赛时，人们纷纷下注；他很少不为我们的女主人赢得彩头。偶尔失手，她便叫人鞭打他，但打得不重，因为她不愿伤了他那宝贵的身体。德律俄佩待我们很好，因为我母亲有一次扭了脚，从此不再跳舞，德律俄佩却依旧待她的奴隶和气温柔。可是，等我能够唱歌跳舞了，主人，德律俄佩便把我卖了个大价钱，卖给一个正要带着一批男女奴隶去罗马的奴隶贩子。我拥抱了德律俄佩和我的父母，跟着贩子走了。他待我也不苛刻，因为我是值钱的奴隶，主人；他待自己的奴隶不苛刻；他小心看护他们，如同看护贵重的货物。特拉叙卢斯在罗马的奴隶市场上替您把我买下，主人；他听了我的试唱、看了我的试舞之后，为我付了一大笔钱，我很是自豪。如今……如今我很幸福，主人，能属于您这样一位主人。可我的心绪还是常常飘回科斯，飘回奴隶的住处，飘回我的父母、那里的同伴和德律俄佩身边。恕罪，主人。”

===Original===
“And would you like to go back to Cos, Cora?”

“My lord, our native land remains dear to us. But I belong to you; and where you are there I will be.”

“And shall you be happy there too, Cora, so far from Cos?”

“I shall be happy where you are happy, my lord, and unhappy where you are unhappy.”

===Chinese===
“那么，你愿意回科斯去吗，科拉？”

“主人，故土于我们总是亲切的。可我属于您；您在哪里，我就在哪里。”

“在那里，离科斯这么远，你也会幸福吗，科拉？”

“您幸福的地方我就幸福，主人；您不幸福的地方我就不幸福。”

===Original===
Lucius looked at her. He did not take her words to be more than the politeness of a courteous slave, who came from a famous slave-school and for whom he had paid a high price, because of her delicate beauty and her accomplishments. But still the sound of Cora’s voice was pleasant to his ear; and he said, graciously and with a gentle smile:

“You know how to speak the word that sounds well, even as you sing true and play true.”

She made no further answer and bowed her head, feeling that he did not count her words as more than a well-sounding speech:

“Have I your permission, my lord, to go back to the barge?” she asked.

“Yes,” he said, “go.”

She made a gesture of graceful reverence and moved away. He followed her at a distance. She walked along by the tall reeds of the river. She was very pretty and dainty, like the soft-tinted statuettes that came from Tanagra. Her flowered muslin peplos hung limply pleated around her shapely body in a succession of thin folds, which blew open and shut. Her bare arms were very slender. Her blue-black hair was fine and caught golden gleams. Now, while she stopped to pluck a flowering reed, she stood among the stems like a nymph.

And Lucius smiled because she was so very pretty, so tenderly winsome, because she sang and played the harp so very beautifully and because she said such civil words and had spoken so charmingly of her native island, Cos, where she was born in Dryope’s slave-school.

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯望着她。他只把这些话当作一个女奴的客气——她出身上好的奴隶学校，因姿容秀丽、才艺出众而被他出了高价。但科拉的声音听在他耳中依然悦耳；他雍容地、带着温柔的微笑说：

“你懂得怎样说出悦耳的话，正如你唱得准、弹得准。”

她没有再回答，垂下头去，明白他把她的话只当作了一番中听的言辞：

“主人允许，我可以回船上去吗？”她问。

“好，”他说，“去吧。”

她优雅地打了个致敬的手势，转身走开。他远远地跟在后面。她沿着河边高高的芦苇走着。她秀丽而娇巧，宛如出自塔纳格拉的那些色泽柔美的小陶俑。她那件印花细麻纱的佩普罗斯（peplos）长裙，一圈圈细褶松松地垂裹着她窈窕的身体，随风开开合合。她赤裸的双臂十分纤细。她蓝黑的头发细柔，映着金色的微光。此刻她停下来摘一枝开花的芦苇，亭亭立于苇丛之间，恍若一位宁芙。

卢基乌斯微笑了，因为她那么美丽，那么温婉动人；因为她唱歌、弹竖琴弹得那么美妙；因为她说话那么彬彬有礼，把她出生的故岛科斯——她在德律俄佩的奴隶学校里出生的地方——讲得那么动人。

## XV / 十五

===Original===
The travellers had left Sais, after visiting the temple of Athene and the tomb of Psammetichus, son of Necho, founder of the twenty-sixth dynasty, one of the twelve kings of the Dodecarchy, who divided Egypt among themselves after the death of Sethos in BC 671. Psammetichus, in obedience to the oracles, defeated and expelled his eleven fellow-kings and reigned alone at Memphis and afterwards at Sais. Here was his tomb; it was sacred; there was an oracle attached to it; and Lucius had consulted that oracle.

After that, Lucius had consulted the manteum, or oracle, of Latona at Butos, on an island in the lake. He had next visited Xois, Hermopolis, Lycopolis, Mendes and all the Sebennytic nome, which contained numberless oracles and shrines. At Mendes the god Pan was worshipped; and there was an oracle which spoke by means of the god’s pipes. Here the goat was held sacred and received public worship at the hands of priestesses in Dionysiac frenzy. The travellers next visited Diospolis and Leontopolis, Busiris and Cunopolis and all the Busiritic nome.

===Chinese===
旅人们离开了塞易斯。此前他们参观了雅典娜（Athene）神庙和普萨美提克（Psammetichus）的陵墓——尼科（Necho）之子、第二十六王朝的创建者、十二分治（Dodecarchy）诸王之一；公元前 671 年塞托斯（Sethos）死后，十二王把埃及瓜分。普萨美提克遵从神谕，击败并驱逐了十一位同僚王，先在孟斐斯、后在塞易斯独揽王权。他的陵墓就在这里；那是圣地，附有一处神谕所；卢基乌斯求问了那神谕。

此后，卢基乌斯又求问了布托斯湖中岛上的勒托（Latona）神谕所（manteum）。接着他走访了克索伊斯（Xois）、赫尔摩波利斯、吕科波利斯（Lycopolis）、门德斯（Mendes）和整个塞本尼提克诺姆——那里有数不清的神谕所与神祠。在门德斯，人们崇奉潘神（Pan）；那里有一处神谕所，借神的排箫发声。山羊在这里被视为神圣，由陷入酒神秘狂的女祭司们公开礼拜。旅人们随后又走访了狄奥波利斯（Diospolis）与莱昂托波利斯、布西里斯（Busiris）与库诺波利斯（Cunopolis）以及整个布西里特诺姆。

===Original===
All these towns, with numbers of villages in between, covered the islands of the flooded Delta, densely peopled and luxuriantly cultivated. The great farmsteads and country-mansions stood linked along the canals, which were filled high to their banks with the flowing waters. The ears of corn swelled with ripeness along the shores; and the cattle gleamed and glanced, grazing in the rich meadows. The fat fields were fragrant, in these last days of the summer month of Epiphi, with a strange, moist scent as of nameless flowers ever drenched in dew. The sun was warm, but not burning, as though the moisture of so many waters tempered all the heat; the fierce rays did not burn, as though they were ever drinking the excessive damp. And from the marshes, which the Nile had turned into lakes, rose no mist, but the scent of the water-flowers: lotus, nymphea and nenuphar.

The rains seemed to have ended. The maximum gauge in the Nilometers appeared to have been reached; only the morning dew was often heavy, like rain. But the days glided past in an immaculate glory of sunshine tempered by moisture, while the rich, fragrant country lay stretched under smooth skies, which changed cloudlessly from morning rose to midday blue and evening gold, in a gradual fusing of tints. There was hardly a breeze in the evening; the atmosphere retained an ideal perfection of heavenly, temperate warmth; this summer warmth was fresh and cool.

===Chinese===
所有这些城镇，连同其间的无数村落，布满了洪水漫淹的三角洲诸岛，人烟稠密，耕作丰饶。大农庄和乡间宅邸沿着运河连绵而立，运河里流水涨得齐岸。沿岸的麦穗灌浆饱满，趋于成熟；牛群在丰美的草场上放牧，毛色油亮。时值埃庇菲月（Epiphi）这个夏季月份的最后几天，肥沃的田野散发着一种奇异的湿润芬芳，像不知名的花恒浸露水中的气息。阳光温暖而不灼人，仿佛众多水泽的湿气调和了一切炎热；烈焰般的光线并不炙烤，仿佛它们时时在啜饮那过盛的潮气。尼罗河化成湖泊的沼泽上，升起的不是雾，而是水生花卉的香气：莲花、睡莲与萍蓬莲。

雨似乎已经下完了。尼罗水位计的最高刻度想必已经涨到；只是清晨的露水常常重得像雨。日子在无瑕的、被湿润调和的阳光荣光里滑过，丰饶芬芳的国土平展在晴滑的天空下，天空无云，从清晨的玫瑰色变成正午的蓝色，再变成傍晚的金色，色调缓缓交融。晚间几乎无风；大气保持着一种天堂般温凉暖意的至善至美；这夏日的温暖清新而凉爽。

===Original===
The thalamegus glided up the Nile. The river was as wide as a sea; everywhere, in the noonday sun, the pools of the waters glittered in among the farmsteads, mansions and shrines. On the horizon, the outlines of the towns, with the needles of the obelisks, shimmered in the damp haze. At every moment, dense palm-clusters or sycamores raised their regular canopies along the river, forming an avenue, or else tamarisks luxuriated and their branches threw fine shadows, like blue stripes upon gold.

There lay the Athribitic nome and the Prosopitic nome, whose capital is Aphroditopolis. Lucius went on shore with a great retinue. The town, consecrated to Aphrodite, was peopled by none but hierodules, priests and priestesses of the goddess. Lucius consulted the oracle.

Next morning, after the orgy, he was lying under the triple awning of the barge which was gliding still higher up the river. Around him were screens of plaited, transparent reeds, interwoven with flowers. Thrasyllus sat by his side:

===Chinese===
泰拉美戈斯溯尼罗河而上。河面宽如海洋；正午的阳光下，农庄、宅邸与神祠之间处处水光闪烁。地平线上，城镇的轮廓连同方尖碑的尖针，在潮湿的霭气里微微发亮。沿岸时时有浓密的棕榈丛或西克莫无花果树举起齐整的树冠，连成一条林荫道；再不然是柽柳繁生，枝条投下细细的阴影，像金色上面的蓝色条纹。

那边是阿特里比诺姆（Athribitic nome）和普罗索皮诺姆（Prosopitic nome），后者的首府是阿佛洛狄忒城（Aphroditopolis）。卢基乌斯带着大批随从上了岸。这座献给阿佛洛狄忒的城，居民无非神妓和这位女神的男女祭司。卢基乌斯求问了神谕。

第二天早晨，狂欢之后，他躺在船的三层天篷下，船正往更上游滑行。他四周围着编织的透明苇帘，帘间织着花朵。特拉叙卢斯坐在他身旁：

===Original===
“Nemu-Pha told me,” said Thrasyllus, “that both Plato and Pythagoras spent years and years on the steps of the temples of Isis before they were deemed worthy of learning one word of the Hermetic wisdom. Well, I never imagined that Nemu-Pha would unlock the Hermetic wisdom to me. But I did hope perhaps to learn a single word with which, continuing to meditate my own thoughts, I could have unlocked the secret, Lucius, of your happiness. But Nemu-Pha did not speak that word to me. And yet, my son, I had to pay him a high price to be admitted to his sanctuary. I am sorry for wasting your money.”

Lucius smiled:

“Nevertheless, Thrasyllus, the oracles, even though they never satisfy the questioner wholly, say very strange and impressive things. Shall I make you a confession? I certainly hope that I shall one day know who robbed me of Ilia. And, when I know, I shall not rest until I have tortured him and made him die a thousand deaths.”

“It was the pirates, Lucius,” said Thrasyllus, evasively, “unless Ilia was drowned.”

“It was one pirate, Thrasyllus,” said Lucius. “All the oracles now never speak except of one pirate. And it is for me﻿ ﻿… as though I saw him before me! The dog!”

===Chinese===
「内穆-法告诉我，」特拉叙卢斯说，「柏拉图（Plato）和毕达哥拉斯（Pythagoras）当年都在伊西斯神庙的台阶上熬了一年又一年，才被认为有资格领受赫尔墨斯智慧的一个字眼。唉，我从未幻想内穆-法会向我开启赫尔墨斯智慧。但我确实指望，或许能学到单独一个字眼，让我接着冥想自己的思路，就能解开你幸福的秘密，卢基乌斯。可内穆-法没有对我说出那个字眼。然而，我的孩子，为了进他的圣所，我还付了高价。浪费了你的钱，我很抱歉。」

卢基乌斯微微一笑：

「不过，特拉叙卢斯，神谕虽然从不让求问的人全然满足，说出的东西却离奇而撼人。要我向你坦白一件事吗？我确实希望有朝一日知道是谁夺走了我的伊利娅。等我知道了，我非把他拷打得死去活来一千次，绝不罢休。」

「是那些海盗，卢基乌斯，」特拉叙卢斯闪烁其词，「除非伊利娅是溺死了。」

「是一个海盗，特拉叙卢斯，」卢基乌斯说。「如今所有的神谕，说的无一不是单独一个海盗。而且对我来说……就像他就立在我眼前！那条狗！」

===Original===
The barge was gliding past Latopolis, on the right; on the left, standing farther back from the river, Heliopolis showed faintly. They were nearing Babylon, but the travellers were to go through to Memphis.

“Look!” said Thrasyllus, starting up in rapture. “The Pyramids!”

Lucius turned, with real interest. There on the horizon, like an enormous, mystic geometry, the triangles of the Pyramids, which announced Memphis, rose against the pink morning sky. They were like eternal lines drawn by the gods from earth to heaven.

“The Pyramids!” echoed Lucius, as though overcome by a mystic impression.

===Chinese===
游船正掠过右岸的拉托波利斯（Latopolis）；左边，离河更远处，赫利奥波利斯（Heliopolis）隐约显现。他们快到巴比伦要塞了，但旅人们要径直前往孟斐斯。

「看哪！」特拉叙卢斯狂喜地跳起来。「金字塔！」

卢基乌斯带着真切的关注转过身去。地平线上，金字塔的三角——孟斐斯的报信者——映着粉红的晨空升起，像一幅巨大而神秘的几何图形。它们像诸神从大地画向天穹的永恒线条。

「金字塔！」卢基乌斯应声道，仿佛被一种神秘的印象慑住了。

===Original===
On the other side, Heliopolis was now more clearly outlined, standing high on a hill, with the temple of the bull Mnevis. Babylon, a suburb of Memphis, swarmed on the riverbank and, with the battlements of its forts; was visible through a sycamore avenue. And suddenly, after a grove of palms, Memphis loomed into view.

“Memphis!” cried Thrasyllus.

And Uncle Catullus, appearing from his cabin, pointed and repeated:

“Memphis!”

The old Egyptian capital lay Cyclopean, like some extinct monster, with heavy lines of squat, bleaching sanctuaries and, on the river, a portico of giant pylons. Behind these age-old, massive buildings the Pyramids showed spectrally.

===Chinese===
另一侧，赫利奥波利斯的轮廓此刻更清晰了，高踞山丘，带着公牛姆奈维斯（Mnevis）的神庙。孟斐斯的近郊巴比伦在河岸上熙熙攘攘，透过西克莫无花果的林荫道，望得见它堡垒的雉堞。忽然，掠过一片棕榈树林，孟斐斯赫然映入眼帘。

「孟斐斯！」特拉叙卢斯喊道。

卡图卢斯叔父从舱房里出来，指着一迭声道：

「孟斐斯！」

古老的埃及都城匍匐着，巨石垒垒，像一头绝种的巨兽，一行行低伏、泛白的圣所沉重排列；河岸边立着巨塔门构成的柱廊。在这些亘古沉重的建筑后面，金字塔显出鬼影般的轮廓。

===Original===
Thrasyllus pointed his long, crystal spyglass towards the horizon:

“*There!*” he said, with a shiver. “The most sacred monument in Egypt! The great Sphinx, the immense Neith, the ever-silent wisdom! Next to the second pyramid: that looming figure of a gigantic, motionless animal!”

The barge hove to and was moored. Caleb proposed that they should go on shore.

Here, even on the quays, the riotous bustle of agriculture and commerce had ceased to reign. Under the palms there was not the metropolitan press and throng of Alexandria, the world’s marketplace and emporium. Only a few fruit-sellers squatted beside their wares and uttered their cries, now that they saw strangers, offering sliced melons and coconut milk. Here and there an Egyptian cowered, dreaming, with long, split eyes. The quays were old, grey, wide and deserted. Even the foreigners’ barge roused but little curiosity. A few children at play assembled when the two litters were carried on shore.

===Chinese===
特拉叙卢斯把长长的水晶望远镜指向地平线：

「*那儿！*」他说，打了个寒战。「埃及最神圣的遗迹！伟大的斯芬克斯，巨大的奈斯，永远沉默的智慧！在第二座金字塔旁边：那巨兽般一动不动、隐隐耸现的身影！」

游船停稳，系了缆。卡莱布提议大家上岸。

这里连码头上，农业与商业的闹嚷喧腾也已绝迹。棕榈树下，没有亚历山大里亚——世界的市场与商埠——那种都会的拥挤人潮。只有几个水果贩子蹲在货摊旁，见了外乡人便吆喝起来，兜售切好的甜瓜和椰子汁。偶尔有个埃及人蜷坐着做梦，长目微眯。码头古老、灰白、宽阔而空落。连外乡人的游船也引不起多少好奇。两乘肩舆抬上岸时，才聚来几个玩耍的孩子。

===Original===
Caleb found it difficult to hire two camels, for himself and Thrasyllus, but he succeeded. The cavalcade started; Caleb’s armed guards﻿—for an escort was needed here, because of the robbers in the desert﻿—surrounded the litters. And the strangers proceeded along the quays, under the palms, to the city. Caleb rode ahead, for he knew the city and the way.

The city was gloomy, huge and empty, but Lucius, ever sensitive to impressions, underwent the enchantment of that past. For Memphis was the eternal past. The town had once numbered six hundred thousand inhabitants. It now haply numbered a few thousands; the rare figures in the wide streets were dwarfed and lost. Sometimes a woman’s face peeped out from the half-opened, vermilion shutters of some great grey, dilapidated house.

===Chinese===
卡莱布好不容易才为自己和特拉叙卢斯雇到两峰骆驼，但终究雇到了。队伍出发了；卡莱布的武装护卫——这里需要护卫，因为沙漠里有盗匪——环卫着肩舆。外乡人沿着码头、在棕榈树下朝城里行去。卡莱布骑马在前引路，因为他认得这座城和路。

这座城阴郁、巨大而空落，可素来对印象敏感的卢基乌斯，仍被那往昔的魔力攫住了。因为孟斐斯就是永恒的往昔。这座城从前有过六十万居民。如今大约只剩几千人；宽阔街道上稀疏的人影显得渺小而零落。偶尔有一张女人的脸，从某座灰色大宅半开的朱红窗板后探出来。

===Original===
Ye gods, what dimensions! What lines, what spaciousness of deserted squares, what heaven-high rows of pylons! The Serapeum yonder, at the endless end of an avenue of six hundred sphinxes, six rows of a hundred sphinxes, the ever-silent incarnations of wisdom, the lion-women who were the wisdom of Neith! What colossal statues, hewn out of one block of stone and towering to the sky, with the pschent crowns of their diadems! And everywhere the deathly silence and under the feet of the Libyan bearers the dust of ages, which flew up on high in one dense cloud after another!

Caleb rode ahead, by the sphinxes in the avenue. They stood in rows, the wise lionesses with fixed women’s faces, eternal guardians of the secret. Some of them were already sinking in the sandy ground, disappearing with their stretched forepaws. Others shelved to one side, borne down by the pressure of the centuries. Here the Pharaohs themselves had passed in sacred processions! Here Moses had walked and Hermes Trismegistus; here Joseph had wandered, the interpreter of dreams; here, lastly, Cambyses, with his Persian hordes, had ridden sacrilegiously! This was Memphis, thrice-sacred Memphis, profaned long centuries ago and now dead and sinking in the devouring sands of the desert, which approached from the west, out yonder! The city would be swallowed up by the sands! That past would sink back into the lap of the earth!

Suddenly Lucius shuddered with the mystic awe of what has been. And his own life and grief seemed small to him.

===Chinese===
诸神啊，何等的规模！何等的线条，何等空旷的广场，何等齐天的塔门行列！那边的萨拉皮雍，在一条六百尊斯芬克斯大道的无尽尽头——六列，每列百尊，永远沉默的智慧化身，狮身人面、身为奈斯之智慧的母狮！何等巨大的雕像，整块巨石凿成，高耸入天，头戴普申特（pschent）双王冠！而四处是死一般的寂静，利比亚抬夫脚下扬起亘古的尘土，一团又一团，飞上半空！

卡莱布骑马在前，沿着大道旁的斯芬克斯。它们成列而立，睿智的母狮，凝固的女人面容，秘密的永恒守望者。有些已经陷进沙土，连同伸展的前爪一起没去。另一些向一侧倾侧，被几个世纪的重压压弯。法老们曾在这里列圣驾巡行！摩西曾在这里行走，三重伟大的赫尔墨斯（Hermes Trismegistus）也是；解梦人约瑟（Joseph）曾在这里徜徉；最后，冈比西斯（Cambyses）曾带着他的波斯部落，渎神地驰骋过这里！这就是孟斐斯，三倍神圣的孟斐斯，许多个世纪前遭亵渎，如今死了，正陷进吞食一切的沙漠之沙，那沙从西边、从那边逼近！这座城将被黄沙吞没！那往昔将沉回大地的怀抱！

忽然，卢基乌斯为那「曾经存在」的神秘敬畏而战栗。他自己的生命与悲伤，在他看来都渺小了。

===Original===
They approached the sanctuary. It rose as a huge shadow. And from every door swarmed serving-priests of Serapis, minor priests and doorkeepers﻿ ﻿… because they saw the strangers. They ranged themselves in front of the entrance and stood waiting.

Caleb said:

“These are distinguished Latin lords, cousins of the divine Caesar Tiberius, blessed be his name. They wish to see the sacred bull.﻿ ﻿…”

“Apis﻿ ﻿…” said the oldest priest.

“Who is Osiris, in the sacred shape of the bull﻿ ﻿…” added other priests.

And others again, oracularly:

“And who drew the plough through the fields of sacred Egypt when he disguised himself with the other gods, under the forms of animals.﻿ ﻿…”

“From the eyes of Jupiter Ammon, who wished to reign alone.”

===Chinese===
他们走近圣所。它像一片巨大的阴影耸起。每道门里都涌出萨拉皮斯的执事祭司、低级祭司和守门人……因为他们望见了外乡人。他们在入口前列队站定，候着。

卡莱布说：

「这几位是尊贵的拉丁大人，神圣的恺撒·提比略（Tiberius）——愿他的圣名受福——的表亲。他们想瞻仰神牛。……」

「阿皮斯（Apis）……」最年长的祭司说。

「就是俄西里斯，以神牛的圣形显现……」别的祭司补充道。

另一些则神谕般地说：

「当年他与众神化作兽形避祸时，是他拉着犁，耕过神圣埃及的田地。……」

「他出自朱庇特·阿蒙（Jupiter Ammon）的眼目——出自那位要独自为王的神。」

===Original===
“The same,” said Caleb, flinging himself from his camel.

The priests arranged themselves in processional order while the travellers alighted and Thrasyllus also slid from his camel. And they sang the Hymn of Apis, as they were wont to do when visitors came. For in the huge dead city of Memphis, inhabited by hardly a few thousands, who were dwarfed and lost in the spaces of the ancient, mystic capital of ancient, mystic Egypt, in truth the worship of Apis was still maintained only because all the travellers came to see the sacred bull. The fees which the travellers paid to the priests formed the principal revenue of their brotherhood. The temple was falling in ruins; the enormous pylons seemed to totter, the gigantic architraves leaned forward; the giant statues were bruised by the rains and eaten away, as though the centuries themselves had mutilated them; the sphinxes were sinking into the sand. But still the worship of the bull Apis was maintained, because of the strangers and their fees.

A young priest who spoke a little Latin was allotted to the travellers and took his place by Lucius’ side, respectfully:

“It is a pity,” he said, smiling cheerfully, “that Serapis did not bring you to Memphis a month earlier. For then, my noble lord, you would have beheld the death of Apis and his return to life.”

===Chinese===
「正是。」卡莱布说着，翻身下了骆驼。

祭司们排成仪仗的行列，旅人们下了轿，特拉叙卢斯也从骆驼上滑下来。祭司们唱起《阿皮斯颂歌》，这是他们每逢来客时的惯例。因为在孟斐斯这座巨大而死寂的城里——居民寥寥数千，湮没在古老而神秘的埃及那古老而神秘的都城的空旷里——阿皮斯的崇奉之所以延续，实在只因一切旅人都要来瞻仰神牛。旅人们付给祭司的香资，是他们祭司团的主要进项。神庙正在倾颓；巨大的塔门仿佛摇摇欲坠，庞大的大梁前倾；巨像被雨水打得斑驳、侵蚀得残缺，仿佛岁月亲手将它们毁伤；斯芬克斯陷进沙里。但阿皮斯神牛的崇奉仍延续着，为了外乡人，也为了他们的香资。

一位会说几句拉丁语的年轻祭司被派来照应旅人，恭恭敬敬地站到卢基乌斯身旁：

「真遗憾，」他快活地笑着说，「萨拉皮斯没有早一个月把您带到孟斐斯。那样的话，尊贵的大人，您就能亲眼见到阿皮斯的死与他的重生了。」

===Original===
“What is this, then?” asked Lucius.

“The incarnation of the god in the sacred bull lasts a quarter of a century,” the young, pleasant, smiling priest explained. “After being incarnated in the bull for five-and-twenty years, the god disappears out of the bull and the bull is marked down for death. The priests drown him solemnly in the Nile and embalm his sacred body and celebrate his obsequies with special ceremonies. What a pity, my lord, that you have come too late! After the obsequies they seek the young Apis, they seek him throughout sacred Egypt. As a rule they find him immediately, for the godhead immediately becomes reincarnated in a newborn bull; and, if it omit to do so, the calamity is so great that the country is plunged into mourning and the disaster foretells universal plagues. But Serapis Osiris loves his Egypt and but seldom delays the new incarnation. This time, after Apis’ obsequies, we were able at once to celebrate his blessed advent.”

“And where was Apis found?” asked Lucius.

===Chinese===
「那这是什么？」卢基乌斯问。

「神在神牛里的化身，历时四分之一个世纪，」年轻和善、满面笑容的祭司解释道。「在牛身上化生二十五年之后，神便离牛而去，牛就被注定要死。祭司们庄严地把它溺入尼罗河，为它的圣体涂香防腐，以特别的仪式为它举哀下葬。多么可惜，大人，您来迟了！葬仪之后，人们便寻找新的小阿皮斯，遍寻整个神圣的埃及。通常立刻就能找到，因为神会立刻化生进一头新生的小牛；倘若神迟迟不化生，那灾祸就大了，全国将陷于哀恸，灾难预示着大瘟疫的降临。但萨拉皮斯·俄西里斯爱他的埃及，很少延误新的化身。这一次，阿皮斯的葬仪一完，我们当即就能庆祝他的福临。」

「那阿皮斯是在哪里找到的？」卢基乌斯问。

===Original===
“On the farm belonging to my father, who is a landowner,” replied the pleasant young priest, smiling mischievously. “I am a landowner’s son; and, when Apis was born in our stables, my father dedicated me to Osiris, that I might take care of the god. I came here with him; I have been here hardly a month; I came with him.”

And he smiled, glad, young and happy; his fresh, young cheeks were still bronzed with the sun and his arms and hands were sturdy as those of a young peasant and shepherd.

The singing priests drew themselves up before a secos, a square plot of grass surrounded by columns.

“My lords,” said the pleasant-looking priest, “this is the secos of the mother of Apis and we are going to show her to you.”

===Chinese===
「在我父亲的农庄上，他是个庄园主。」和善的年轻祭司答道，调皮地笑着。「我是庄园主的儿子；阿皮斯在我们家牛棚里降生时，我父亲把我奉献给俄西里斯，好让我照料这位神。我随他一起来的；来了还不到一个月；我随他一起来的。」

他笑着，欢快、年轻而幸福；他新鲜年轻的面颊还带着阳光晒出的古铜色，胳膊和手像年轻庄稼汉和牧人的一样结实。

唱着颂歌的祭司们在一座内苑前站定——那是一方围着柱廊的草地。

「大人们，」和善的祭司说，「这是阿皮斯母亲的内苑，我们这就带诸位去见她。」

===Original===
“So she also comes from your father’s farm?” asked Uncle Catullus.

“Most certainly, my lord,” replied the priest, roguishly.

“That of course goes without saying,” commented Uncle Catullus.

The young priest opened the gate of the secos. At the far end was the sacred stall, like the wide interior of a temple. The priest, vanished in the shadow.

And, when he reappeared, he was leading, merely by pressing his hand against her snow-white flank, a handsome, sleek cow.

===Chinese===
「这么说，她也出自令尊的农庄？」卡图卢斯叔父问。

「千真万确，大人。」祭司答道，狡黠地。

「这个么，自然不在话下。」卡图卢斯叔父评论道。

年轻祭司推开内苑的门。苑尽头是神圣的牛厩，像神庙宽阔的内堂。祭司的身影消失在阴影里。

等他再出现时，只把一只手贴着那雪白的胁腹，就牵出来一头俊美的母牛，毛色油光水滑。

===Original===
He led her to the strangers. She shone, well-tended and well-fed. She had placid eyes of bluish gold, beautiful, large, soft and womanly, the eyes of Hera herself. Her horns were gilded and her hoofs were painted red.

The pleasant-looking priest led her to the strangers and was glad and happy because Apis’ mother was so comely:

“Is she not handsome?” he asked proudly.

The strangers smiled and agreed that she was very handsome; and the priest, with respectful familiarity, stroked her snow-white flank and pointed out that she had one black foot. Then he kissed her, fondly and reverently, on her moist muzzle and led her back, with the pressure of his hand, to the temple that was her stall. She went, solemnly, as though aware of her high, sacred dignity, which existed only because of the strangers and their fee.

===Chinese===
他把她牵到外乡人跟前。她容光焕发，被照料得周到，喂养得肥壮。她有一双蓝金色的温驯眼睛，美丽、大而柔软，带着女性的神情，是赫拉（Hera）本人的眼睛。她的角贴着金箔，蹄子涂成了红色。

和善的祭司把她牵给外乡人看，因为阿皮斯的母亲这样俊美而满心欢喜：

「她俊不俊？」他骄傲地问。

外乡人微笑着，都承认她非常俊美；祭司带着恭敬的亲昵，抚着她雪白的胁腹，指给大家看她有一只黑蹄。然后他亲了亲她湿润的鼻嘴，又爱又敬，用手掌贴着她，把她牵回那做她牛厩的殿堂。她庄严地走去，仿佛自知她那崇高神圣的尊严——那尊严，只因外乡人和他们的香资而存在。

===Original===
The priest, still smiling, returned; and the other priests sang their hymn.

And, by the priest’s pleasant manner, Lucius seemed to observe that he ought to pay. He beckoned to Caleb; and there were mutual, smiling, roguish negotiations between Caleb and the priest. For Caleb always tried to pay the fees which he distributed on Lucius’ behalf a little less liberally than he set them down on the long papyrus scroll of his bill; and he generally succeeded.

But the priest was not only roguish, but very crafty and polite; and the transaction, conducted in mysterious and jocular whispers, lasted a long time﻿ ﻿… until Lucius said, impatiently, but still smiling:

“And may we now see Apis himself?”

===Chinese===
祭司仍笑着走回来；其他祭司唱起他们的颂歌。

从祭司那和气的做派里，卢基乌斯似乎觉察到该付钱了。他向卡莱布招手；卡莱布与祭司之间便展开了一场笑眯眯、鬼精精的谈判。因为卡莱布替卢基乌斯支付的香资，总要设法比他记在那卷长长的纸草账单上的数目稍微克扣一点；而且他一般总能得手。

可这祭司不仅鬼精，还十分精明客气；这笔交易在神秘而打趣的耳语中进行了很久……直到卢基乌斯不耐烦地说——却仍带着笑：

「现在可以让我们看看阿皮斯本尊了吗？」

===Original===
So Caleb paid, grudgingly. But the priest remained pleasant and the other priests sang while conducting the strangers to Apis’ own secos.

This sanctuary was even bigger and more impressive than that of the white bull-mother. There was a square in front of it, with obelisks; and the pleasant-looking priest entered between two sphinxes. But the pillars, the obelisks, the sphinxes seemed to totter, to slant, to burst with old age.

The priests sang the hymn; and suddenly, like a whirlwind, a young bull came trotting out of the stable over the grass-plot. It was Apis; and the priests lifted their hands in adoration as they sang.

===Chinese===
卡莱布终于付了钱，老大不情愿。可祭司依旧和气，其他祭司唱着颂歌，把外乡人引向阿皮斯自己的内苑。

这座圣所比白牛母亲的更大、更慑人。圣所前有个广场，立着方尖碑；和善的祭司从两尊斯芬克斯之间走进去。只是那些石柱、方尖碑、斯芬克斯，仿佛都因年迈而摇摇欲坠、歪斜、迸裂。

祭司们唱着颂歌；忽然，像一阵旋风，一头小牛从厩中撒着欢跑上草地。那就是阿皮斯；祭司们边唱边高举双手礼赞。

===Original===
But, if his mother was stately and aware of her dignity, Apis himself carried his divinity with the recklessness of his hot youth. He ran across the lawn, glad to have escaped from his stable; and the pleasant priest, laughing, ran after him. But he could not catch him by his gold collar; and, panting for breath, the little priest said, proudly:

“Isn’t he beautiful and playful? Isn’t he most delightful, our Apis?”

===Chinese===
不过，母亲端庄而知自尊，阿皮斯本尊却以他火热少年的莽撞背负着自己的神性。他跑过草坪，为逃出牛厩而欢天喜地；和善的祭司笑着追他。可他抓不住那金项圈；小个子祭司气喘吁吁，骄傲地说：

「他漂亮不漂亮？淘气不淘气？我们的阿皮斯，多惹人爱呀？」

===Original===
He was beautiful and playful and most delightful, the visitors granted. He was a splendid bull-calf. His coat gleamed black as jet; and he was painted in accordance with the sacred prescript without which there is no incarnation: a white moon, like a snowy little crown, shone like a sickle between his gilded horns; and two other little white crowns adorned him on either side above the forelegs. His eyes blazed as might carbuncles with a light kindled behind them; and he stared from under his curly forehead with an almost human glance. His neck already fell into powerful, heavy folds; his chest was broad; and he lashed his tail like a whip. His hoofs were vermilion. And he trotted round his grass-plot and pushed out the sods with his horns and scratched with his red hoofs. The pleasant-looking priest now went up to him, laughing, and took him, respectfully and yet firmly, by the gold collar and talked to him and laughed; and Apis shook himself; and the priest laughed; and now all the priests began to laugh and the strangers laughed and Caleb roared and Uncle Catullus held his sides. Even Lucius had to laugh and Thrasyllus too; they all laughed at Apis, because he was such a delightful, pretty, playful bull-calf, just like a merry boy, with his human eyes which looked at you naughtily and watchfully and archly﻿ ﻿… until all of a sudden he tore himself loose from his little priest and ran, ran like a whirlwind, till the clods of earth flew all around.

===Chinese===
他确实漂亮、淘气、惹人爱，来客们公认。他是头出众的小公牛。他的皮毛黑得像煤玉般发亮；他依照神圣的规约涂饰着规定的记号——没有这些规约便没有化身：一轮白色的月亮，像雪白的小冠，像一弯银镰，在他镀金的角间发亮；另有两只白色小冠缀在他前腿上方的两侧。他的眼睛像后面燃着火的红宝石般灼灼发光；他从卷曲的额发下注视人，目光近乎人类。他的颈项已隆起有力而厚重的褶；他的胸膛宽阔；尾巴像鞭子一样甩动。他的蹄子是朱红色的。他在他的草地上小跑，用角拱起一块块草皮，用红蹄刨地。和善的祭司这时笑着走近他，又恭敬又坚定地抓住金项圈，对他说话，笑着；阿皮斯抖了抖身子；祭司笑了；于是所有祭司都笑起来，外乡人也笑了，卡莱布放声大笑，卡图卢斯叔父捧着肚子。连卢基乌斯也不得不笑，特拉叙卢斯也是；大家都笑阿皮斯，因为他是这样一头可爱、漂亮、淘气的小公牛，活像一个快活的男孩，长着一双人的眼睛，顽皮、机警、促狭地望着你……忽然他猛地挣脱他的小祭司，跑啊跑啊，像一阵旋风，直跑得土块四下乱飞。

===Original===
