“He *is* so pretty and playful!” said the little priest, glad and happy as a boy, when he came back panting, after locking up the little bull again in the sanctuary. “But he is wild, he is very wild: as a rule, we only show him through the windows of his secos; but, when such very distinguished strangers come to look at him, we let him out for a trot, now and again. Yes, then he may come for a trot, once in a way!﻿ ﻿… And he himself thinks it great fun, to come for a trot, now and then, in the presence of strangers.”

Then the pleasant-looking little priest went up to Caleb, who was still laughing aloud because Apis was such a very delightful little bull. And there was a long and protracted discussion, mysterious, jocular and yet weighty. For Caleb was taken aback; but then the little priest knew what it cost to make Apis trot about so prettily for such very distinguished strangers.

===Chinese===
「他*真*是又漂亮又淘气！」小个子祭司说，欢喜得像个男孩；他刚把小牛重新关进圣所，喘着气跑回来。「不过他野，他野得很：平常，我们只隔着内苑的窗子让人看他；可要是来了这样尊贵的外乡人要看他，我们偶尔也放他出来遛一遛。是啊，那他就可以出来遛遛，难得一回！……他自己也觉得顶好玩呢，当着外乡人的面，时不时出来遛一遛。」

随后，和善的小个子祭司走向卡莱布——他还为阿皮斯这头如此可爱的小牛放声笑着呢。于是一场旷日持久的谈判开始了，神秘、打趣而又郑重。卡莱布被吓了一跳；可小个子祭司心里有数：要让阿皮斯为这样尊贵的外乡人这样漂亮地撒欢，得值多少钱。

## XVI / 十六

===Original===
They took the repast provided by Caleb outside the town, in a farmstead beside a canal, under a cluster of palm-trees. There were no dainty dishes, there were no purple-coloured wines thick as ink; but there were omelettes and there was cestreus, the sea-fish that swims up the Nile in certain months: fried in cici-oil, this is a popular, homely dish, it is true, but nevertheless toothsome for hungry travellers picnicking in the grass. There was foaming beer and hydromel, or honey-water; and Uncle Catullus, spoilt though he was, thought the simple meal anything but unpalatable and considered that an idyll of this sort was good for the stomach, once in a way.

Lucius told Caleb to have his luncheon with them; and Caleb, after much deprecation and many salaams at the honour shown him, squatted down and crossed his legs and ate with relish and kept on laughing at the thought of dear little Apis trotting round his secos for the strangers who paid so generously. The travellers were to rest under the palm-trees and allow the midday heat to pass before going on to the pyramids. For Caleb had sent the litters back to the barge and had now hired four good camels at the farm, including two with comfortable saddles of bright tapestry, for his two noble clients.

The farmer and his wife, glad at the visit that brought them in money, spread awnings under which the travellers could enjoy their siesta and laid mats on the ground; and Uncle Catullus called for a fly-net, which he wound round his head. And, while he slept and Caleb also closed his eyes, Lucius, with Thrasyllus by his side, gazed at the wonderful, divinely geometrical lines in the distance, the lightly-traced triangles against the golden noonday sky.

===Chinese===
他们在城外用餐，吃卡莱布备下的饭食，在运河旁一座农庄的棕榈树丛下。没有精美的菜肴，没有稠得像墨汁的紫红酒；但有摊鸡蛋饼，还有鲻鱼（cestreus）——那种在某些月份溯尼罗河而上的海鱼：用蓖麻油（cici-oil）煎了，诚然是道大众的家常菜，可对坐在草地上野餐的饥饿旅人来说，照样可口。有泛泡的啤酒，还有蜜水酒（hydromel）；卡图卢斯叔父虽然娇生惯养，却觉得这顿便饭毫不难吃，还认为这样的田园野趣偶尔来一回，于肠胃有益。

卢基乌斯叫卡莱布同他们一起用午餐；卡莱布受宠若惊，推辞了半天、行了一堆礼，才盘腿蹲坐下来，吃得津津有味，一想到可爱的小阿皮斯为出手这么大方的外乡人在他的内苑里撒欢，就笑个不停。旅人们要在棕榈树下歇晌，等正午的酷热过去，再去看金字塔。卡莱布已把肩舆打发回船，此刻又在农庄雇了四峰好骆驼，其中两峰配有织锦鲜亮的舒适鞍座，给他那两位高贵的主顾。

庄主和妻子为这笔送上门的进项而欢喜，张起凉篷好让旅人歇晌，地上铺了席子；卡图卢斯叔父要来一顶防蝇网，缠在头上。他睡着后，卡莱布也阖了眼；卢基乌斯则与特拉叙卢斯并肩，凝望远方那奇妙而神圣如几何的线条，那映在金色正午天空下轻轻描出的三角。

===Original===
“The base is square,” said Thrasyllus, “and the summit is square, but looks pointed.”

“To me they seem strange, mysterious embodiments of vastness,” said Lucius. “What are they actually?”

“We don’t altogether know,” replied Thrasyllus. “Some of the pyramids were sepulchres of kings and sacred animals. Those are the pyramids of Cheops, or Khufu, of Chephren and of Mencheres; and we shall see the kings’ chambers inside them. They were built twenty, perhaps thirty centuries ago. Herodotus says that the pyramid of Cheops, which is the biggest, took thirty years to build with a hundred thousand slaves, who were changed every three months. The name is derived from πῦρ fire, because, like a flame, the pyramid ends in a point. Many were used as storehouses in the long years of famine; others were dykes against the sands of the desert, which blew towards Memphis and threatened to bury the city, in a succession of ages. Many pyramids have already been swallowed up in the sands.”

===Chinese===
「底座是方的，」特拉叙卢斯说，「顶也是方的，只是看上去像尖的。」

「在我看来，它们像是宏大之物的奇异而神秘的化身，」卢基乌斯说。「它们到底是什么？」

「我们不很清楚，」特拉叙卢斯答道。「有些金字塔是国王和神圣动物的陵墓。那边是基奥普斯（Cheops）——或称胡夫（Khufu）——齐夫伦（Chephren）和门凯雷斯（Mencheres）的金字塔；我们会看到里面的国王墓室。它们建于二十个、也许三十个世纪之前。希罗多德（Herodotus）说，最大的基奥普斯金字塔动用了十万个奴隶、耗时三十年建成，奴隶每三个月换一批。金字塔之名源于 πῦρ（火），因为金字塔像火焰一样，收于一点。许多金字塔在漫长的饥年用作粮仓；另一些则充当阻挡沙漠之沙的堤堰——那些沙一代又一代地吹向孟斐斯，威胁着要把城埋掉。许多金字塔已经被黄沙吞没了。」

===Original===
“What are those ruined palaces over there?” asked Lucius, pointing to crumbled rows of pylons and pillars, surmounted by cracked architraves, impressive ruins which stood on a hill at the outskirts of the town and seemed to be tumbling into the Nile.

“The old palaces of the Pharaohs,” said Thrasyllus. “They were ten in number. Joseph, the Jewish interpreter of dreams, was a powerful governor under one of them; Moses, who knew Hermes Trismegistus and learned the occult wisdom from him, all the wisdom that can be known, was saved, as a babe, by the daughter of a Pharaoh, where his sister had exposed him in a basket made of bulrushes at the place where the princess was wont to bathe: she was the daughter of Amenophis III, who saw his people smitten with ten plagues sent over Egypt by Jahve, the God of the Jews, because the Pharaoh would not suffer them to leave the country. This Pharaoh was drowned in the Red Sea and was the father of Sesostris.﻿ ﻿… I have written on these scrolls everything that is more or less interesting.”

And Thrasyllus, glad to see that Lucius’ attention was attracted, handed him the scrolls. Lucius began reading:

===Chinese===
「那边那些倾颓的宫殿是什么？」卢基乌斯指着成行坍塌的塔门和石柱问道，柱顶架着迸裂的大梁，那片慑人的废墟立在城郊的山丘上，仿佛正要倾倒进尼罗河。

「法老们的旧宫殿，」特拉叙卢斯说。「共有十座。犹太解梦人约瑟曾在其中一位治下做过权倾一时的总督；摩西——他结识过三重伟大的赫尔墨斯，从他那里学得隐秘的智慧、一切可知的智慧——还是婴孩时，被一位法老的女儿救起，他姐姐把他放进纸莎草编的篮子，弃在公主惯常沐浴的地方；她是阿蒙诺菲斯三世（Amenophis III）的女儿——那法老亲眼见他的百姓被十场瘟疫击打，那是犹太人的神雅赫维（Jahve）降在全埃及的，因为法老不肯放他们出境。这位法老溺死在红海，正是塞索斯特里斯的父亲。……凡是比较有趣的事，我都写在这些纸卷上了。」

特拉叙卢斯见卢基乌斯被引动了兴味，很是欢喜，把纸卷递给他。卢基乌斯读了起来：

===Original===
“This all happened here!” he said, startled and arrested. “This is all﻿ ﻿… the past! The age-old past, which is gone, which was swallowed up by the sands﻿ ﻿… thousands of years ago!﻿ ﻿… How small we are when we look into the past﻿ ﻿… and when we gaze into the centuries, the centuries that have buried themselves so deeply!”

“My son,” said the old tutor, “I am so thankful that your mind is once more capable of receiving these impressions. For the beauty of the past is a comfort for the future; and the sick soul is healed in that beauty when it understands that its own grief is but a grain of sand in a desert which blows in the wind and conceals all things.”

Lucius made no further reply, absorbed in what he was reading about Joseph and Moses, about Jahve and Pharaoh Amenophis, who was the father of Sesostris.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「这些全都发生在这里！」他说，又惊又迷。「这全是……往昔！亘古的往昔，它逝去了，被黄沙吞没了……在几千年前！……当我们望向往昔，我们多么渺小……当我们凝视那些世纪，那些把自己埋得这样深的世纪！」

「我的孩子，」老导师说，「你的心灵又能领受这些印象了，我是多么感激。因为往昔之美是对未来的慰藉；病着的灵魂在那美里得着医治，当它明白自己的悲伤不过是流沙蔽物的沙漠中的一粒沙。」

卢基乌斯没有再回答，全神读着约瑟与摩西、雅赫维与塞索斯特里斯之父法老阿蒙诺菲斯的故事。……

## XVII / 十七

===Original===
The golden noonday sky paled; the blinding topaz of the heavens melted away into amber honey; and the sands of the desert stretched out wide, far and endless to the last glittering streak of the horizon, on which the sun had set. Behind the group formed by the travellers﻿—four camels surrounded by drivers and guards, Arabs and Libyans﻿—between the darkening palm-trees the gigantic city of Memphis sank into shadow like some vast extinct monster; and the crumbling palaces of the kings sloped down the hill, as it were tumbling into the Nile, and mirrored their ruins in the clear sapphire of the stream, where the pools lay pink and gold among the tall reeds and the lotuses closing on the face of the water. The last fallen pillars lay, round and immense, in the luxuriant grass, amid a riot of scarlet and crimson poppies. Mysteriously carved with hieroglyphics, they were as felled Titans of rose-red granite; and they pressed heavily on the ground wherein they were sinking. They were of a melancholy majesty, those huge overthrown pillars which had supported the golden roofs above the might of the Pharaohs.

Caleb rode his camel with a swagger, as though he were bestriding his Sabaean mare. He dug his heel into the camel’s side; and the startled brute took great strides, snorting and grunting; Caleb roared with delight. The Libyans, big-limbed and powerful, went silently; the Arab drivers yelled and shouted.

Forty stadia from Memphis rose a broad, hilly dyke, on which the pyramids stood. And Caleb, who, as the guide, also knew a thing or two, cried:

===Chinese===
金色的正午天空淡了下去；天上炫目的黄玉色融成了琥珀的蜜色；沙漠的沙向外铺展，宽远无尽，直铺到地平线最后一道闪光——太阳已经落在那上面。旅人一行——四峰骆驼，围着驼夫和护卫，阿拉伯人和利比亚人——身后，在渐渐暗下来的棕榈树之间，巨大的孟斐斯城沉入阴影，像一头庞大的绝种巨兽；倾颓的王宫顺着山丘滑下，仿佛要跌进尼罗河，把废墟映在河水清亮的蓝宝石色里，高高的芦苇间、阖拢在水面上的莲花间，水洼泛着粉红与金色。最后几根倒下的石柱，圆浑而巨大，卧在丰茂的草里，四围猩红绛紫的罂粟开得如火如荼。它们刻满神秘的象形文字，像玫瑰红花岗岩凿成的被斩落的泰坦；沉重地压着正把它们吞陷的地面。这些巨大的倾覆石柱，昔日支撑过法老威权之上的金色殿顶，自有一种忧郁的庄严。

卡莱布神气活现地骑着他的骆驼，仿佛胯下是他萨巴的骏马。他把脚跟往骆驼肋上一磕；那受惊的畜生喷着鼻息、哼哧着迈开大步；卡莱布乐得大吼。利比亚人肢体魁伟，默默赶路；阿拉伯驼夫们又叫又嚷。

离孟斐斯四十斯塔迪亚（stadia），隆起一道宽阔多丘的堤冈，金字塔便立在上面。向导卡莱布也颇懂几手，喊道：

===Original===
“My lord, two of the pyramids yonder, the largest, belong to the seven wonders of the world! They are a stadium high; and the length of their sides is equal to their height. They are the two tombs of the Pharaohs; but the smaller pyramid, higher up on the hill and, as you see, built entirely of black stone, was the costliest of all.”

He trotted on his startled camel around the others and cried:

“Master Thrasyllus won’t deny it, learned though he is!”

Thrasyllus smiled; and Caleb, glad at being allowed to speak, continued:

“That black stone comes from Southern Ethiopia and is heavier than any other stone and incredibly hard! That is why the pyramid cost so much. But then it was erected by all the lovers of Queen Cleopatra; and it is she who is buried there!”

===Chinese===
「大人，那边两座最大的金字塔，名列世界七大奇迹！它们高齐一斯塔迪亚；边长与高度相等。那是法老们的两座陵墓；而山坡更高处那座小些的，您瞧，通体用黑石砌成，是所有金字塔里耗资最巨的。」

他骑着受惊的骆驼绕众人小跑，喊道：

「特拉叙卢斯大师学问再大，也不会否认！」

特拉叙卢斯微微一笑；卡莱布得了说话的许可，喜滋滋地接着说：

「那黑石产自埃塞俄比亚南部，比任何石头都重，硬得难以置信！所以那金字塔才那么费钱。再说，它是由克娄巴特拉（Cleopatra）女王的所有情人合力建起来的；葬在那里的正是她！」

===Original===
“Caleb,” cried Master Thrasyllus, “what you have been telling about the black stone I accept; but Cleopatra, who died in Alexandria, was not buried at Memphis.”

“Cleopatra, Cleopatra!” Caleb insisted, vigorously; but he now rushed away on his bewildered camel, because he wanted to warn the priest-custodian of the pyramids that there were great lords approaching.

“Caleb is wrong,” said Thrasyllus, as the three camels stepped along sedately, among the gigantic Libyans and shouting Arabs, while Caleb tore fantastically over the sands. “The black pyramid yonder is really not the tomb of Cleopatra. The historians speak of Doricha, an hetaira mentioned by Sappho, the famous poetess, as the mistress of her brother Charaxus, who was a wine-merchant at Lésbos and travelled constantly to Naucratis. This costly black tomb is said to have been dedicated to Doricha, who died young, by her lovers.﻿ ﻿…”

The cavalcade had drawn near; the camels, in obedience to the drivers’ orders, knelt down; the travellers slipped to the ground. And Caleb at once came to meet them, smiling, at the head of six priest-custodians, whose business it was to keep up the interior of the pyramids and show the shrines to foreigners.

===Chinese===
「卡莱布，」特拉叙卢斯大师喊道，「你讲的黑石我认账；可克娄巴特拉死在亚历山大里亚，并没有葬在孟斐斯。」

「克娄巴特拉，克娄巴特拉！」卡莱布执拗地嚷；可他随即催着晕头转向的骆驼飞奔而去，他要去通报金字塔的祭司看守：贵人大人们来了。

「卡莱布说错了，」特拉叙卢斯说；三峰骆驼在魁伟的利比亚人和喧嚷的阿拉伯人中间从容行进，卡莱布却在沙地上狂态百出地飞驰。「那边那座黑金字塔实在不是克娄巴特拉的墓。史家们提到过多里卡（Doricha），一位名妓，著名女诗人萨福（Sappho）提过她，说她是她兄弟卡拉克索斯（Charaxus）的情妇——那兄弟在莱斯博斯（Lésbos）做酒商，常年来往诺克拉提斯。这座耗资巨大的黑陵，据说便是多里卡的情人们献给早逝的她的。……

一行人走近了；骆驼听驼夫的号令跪下；旅人们溜下驼背。卡莱布立刻带着六名祭司看守含笑迎上来——这些人的职事，是维护金字塔内部、领外邦人参观圣祠。

===Original===
“Do many foreigners come here?” Uncle Catullus asked of the oldest priest.

“Not a week passes in this present month,” said the old priest, “but foreigners come to admire the sacred pyramids. You are Latins, but we receive visits also from Greek lords and Persians and Indians. When the Nile has subsided to its lowest gauge, however, when the autumnal winds blow and the sandstorms begin, then no more foreigners come. For then death and destruction blow out of the desert, as the hurricanes of fate which one day will cover Memphis with a sandy shroud. See these few sphinxes, whose heads alone still project above these downs. Once they numbered hundreds; and an avenue stretched between their silence to the Pyramids. But the desert swallowed them up, the hurricanes spread them with dust, the sandy shroud covered up the wisdom of Neith. One day the shroud will cover up all Egypt and veil all her wisdom. What was known will be known no longer. That will be the punishment of the gods, inflicted upon unworthy man, who will be plunged into a night of ignorance and the bestiality of primitive desire. The centuries will turn about!”

===Chinese===
「来这儿的外邦人多吗？」卡图卢斯叔父问最年长的祭司。

「眼下这个月，没有哪个星期不有外邦人来瞻仰神圣的金字塔，」老祭司说。「你们是拉丁人，可我们也接待希腊大人、波斯人和印度人。不过，等尼罗河落到最低水位，秋风刮起、沙暴开始，就再没有外邦人来了。因为那时死亡与毁灭会从沙漠里刮出来，像命运的风暴，终有一天要用沙的裹尸布把孟斐斯罩住。瞧这几尊斯芬克斯，只剩头还探在这些沙冈上面。从前它们数以百计；一条大道在它们的静默之间直通金字塔。可沙漠把它们吞了，风暴给它们披了尘沙，沙的裹尸布盖住了奈斯的智慧。终有一天，这裹尸布会罩住整个埃及，遮蔽她全部的智慧。曾经被知道的，将不再被知道。那将是诸神的惩罚，降在不配的人类身上，他们将被掷进无知的黑夜和原始欲望的兽性里。世纪将倒转！」

===Original===
The priests in attendance, with a simple pressure of the hand, had caused a heavy monolithic door to turn on its hinges in the largest of the pyramids. They lighted torches and went through the syrinx, a winding tunnel painted with gigantic figures of gods and with hieroglyphics. It was strange, but there was a humming and murmur of voices, though the pyramid was uninhabited. It was as though a swarm of ghosts were whirling around like a gale of wind. The impression was given immediately; and, when the travellers exchanged glances, they saw in one another’s eyes that they were all four thinking the same thing; and Caleb muttered saving incantations and repeatedly kissed his amulets.

The priests led the way, while the flames of the torches blew and blew in the mysterious draught, as though ghosts were hovering around. The travellers entered an enormous square room; huge statues were sculptured in the stone walls; and, though the room was empty, there was a smell of spices, as if the smells of old had lingered eternally. Two bats fluttered to the ceiling and whirled round in a circle.

===Chinese===
随行的祭司们只用手一推，便让最大那座金字塔上一扇沉重的整石门在户枢上转开。他们点起火把，穿过甬道（syrinx）——一条蜿蜒的隧道，绘着巨大的神像和象形文字。奇怪的是，金字塔里明明无人居住，却有嗡嗡的人语与低吟。仿佛一群鬼魂像一阵狂风似的绕着人打旋。这印象当下就有了；旅人们交换眼色时，从彼此眼里看出，四个人想的是同一件事；卡莱布喃喃念着禳灾的咒文，一遍遍亲吻他的护符。

祭司们在前引路，火把的火焰在神秘的气流里晃了又晃，仿佛有鬼魂盘旋在旁。旅人们走进一间巨大的方形殿室；石壁上雕着巨大的神像；殿室虽空，却有一股香料的气味，仿佛远古的气味恒久不散。两只蝙蝠扑向穹顶，绕着圈子飞旋。

===Original===
“This is the king’s chamber of Cheops,” said the old priest. “Once upon a time it contained a sarcophagus of azure granite, with the embalmed body of the great King Cheops, or Khufu; and it was surrounded by the sarcophagi of his brothers. He wore out his people with taxes and heavy labour, in order to found this mausoleum for himself. Where is he now? Where is his embalmed body? Where is his azure sarcophagus? Where are the sarcophagi of his brothers Chefren and Schafra? Where are they? Where are they? They are scattered and vanished as grains of sand, the mummies of the proud rulers, covered with scented wax and tightly swathed in narrow bandages; and scattered and vanished are their sarcophagi; and one day these pyramids themselves will be scattered and vanished, swallowed up in the lap of the earth! Everything vanishes, all is vanity: thy wisdom alone, O Neith, is needful to man!”

“Thy wisdom alone, O Neith, is needful to man!” echoed the priests.

“And we no longer possess it!”

“Alas, alas, we no longer possess it!” echoed the priests, mechanically, indifferently, while they led the way back through the tunnel; and their words blew away in the strange, mysterious draught, because of the invisible ghosts that hovered.

===Chinese===
「这是基奥普斯的国王墓室，」老祭司说。「从前里面放着一具青色花岗岩的石棺，殓着伟大的基奥普斯王——或称胡夫——涂香防腐的遗体；四围是他兄弟们的石棺。他用赋税和苦役耗尽了子民，只为给自己建起这座陵寝。他如今何在？他防腐的遗体何在？他那青色的石棺何在？他的兄弟齐夫伦（Chefren）和沙夫拉（Schafra）的石棺又何在？它们在哪？在哪？它们散了、没了，如一粒粒沙——那些骄傲君主的木乃伊，敷着香蜡、裹紧窄窄的尸布；它们的石棺也散了、没了；终有一天，这些金字塔自己也终将散了、没了，被吞进大地的怀抱！一切消散，万事皆虚：哦奈斯，唯独你的智慧，是人所需的！」

「哦奈斯，唯独你的智慧，是人所需的！」祭司们应声道。

「而我们已不再拥有它！」

「呜呼，呜呼，我们已不再拥有它！」祭司们应声道，机械而淡漠，一边领路往回穿行隧道；他们的话语在那奇异神秘的气流里被吹散了，因为有看不见的鬼魂在盘旋。

===Original===
But, when they were outside, the priests kept their torches alight; and they led the travellers to the small, black pyramid. They pushed open the stone door; and the old priest went in first. There was a long tunnel, followed by a room with smooth, black, polished walls, in which the torches and the shadows of the travellers and priests themselves were reflected curiously.

“The pyramid of Cleopatra,” whispered Caleb to Thrasyllus.

“The pyramid of Doricha,” Thrasyllus corrected him, with a smile.

But the old priest shook his head gently and, in a low and fond voice, said:

“The pyramid of Rhodopis. She lived at Naucratis and was incomparably beautiful and chaste. One day, when she was bathing, an eagle flew through the open ceiling of the bathroom and plucked from her maid’s hands the sandal which she was just about to lace on her mistress’ foot.”

===Chinese===
可到了外面，祭司们的火把仍点着；他们领旅人走向那座小小的黑金字塔。他们推开石门；老祭司先进去。先是一条长隧道，接着是一间四壁乌黑光滑的殿室，火把和旅人与祭司们自己的影子，奇异地映在壁上。

「克娄巴特拉的金字塔。」卡莱布悄悄对特拉叙卢斯说。

「多里卡的金字塔。」特拉叙卢斯含笑纠正他。

可老祭司轻轻摇头，用低柔的声音说：

「是罗多庇斯（Rhodopis）的金字塔。她住在诺克拉提斯，美得无与伦比，而且贞洁。一天，她正在沐浴，一只鹰穿过浴室敞开的天顶飞进来，从侍女手中叼走了那只她正要给女主人系上脚的凉鞋。」

===Original===
Lucius suddenly turned very pale. But the priest continued:

“The eagle flew to Memphis, where the king was administering justice in one of the courts of the palace; and, flying above the king, the eagle dropped the sandal, so that it fell into the folds of the king’s garment. The king was much surprised; and he examined the sandal, which was as small as a child’s and yet was the sandal of a woman. And he bade his servants search all Egypt to find the woman whom so small a sandal would fit. His servants then found Rhodopis at Naucratis and carried her to the king and he married her; and, when she died, after a few months’ happiness, the disconsolate king dedicated to her the black pyramid﻿ ﻿… which is the costliest of all the pyramids.﻿ ﻿… Rhodopis’ scented mummy vanished; her sarcophagus vanished. But the sandal, which the king ever worshipped, was preserved by a miracle. Behold it.”

And the priests, with their torches, lighted in the middle of the jet-black room a crystal shrine, standing on a black-porphyry table. And in the crystal shrine lay a little sandal, like a child’s and yet a woman’s, a little red-leather sandal with gold ornaments, arabesques that glittered incredibly fresh.

“The sandal kept for tourists,” murmured Uncle Catullus, with a sceptical smile. “We shall pay for it presently, Caleb, just as we did for the little Apis.”

“But still it is very pretty, my lord,” whispered Caleb, with a smile.

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯的脸色霎时惨白。可祭司继续说：

「鹰飞到孟斐斯，国王正在王宫一座庭院里听政断案；那只鹰在国王上方飞过，丢下凉鞋，凉鞋正落进国王衣袍的褶里。国王大为惊异；他端详那只凉鞋，小得像孩子的，却分明是女人的鞋。他命仆从们搜遍全埃及，找出穿得上这么小的鞋的女人。仆从们于是在诺克拉提斯找到了罗多庇斯，把她带到国王面前，国王娶了她；数月幸福之后她死了，悲恸欲绝的国王为她建了这座黑金字塔……那是所有金字塔里最昂贵的一座。……罗多庇斯敷香的木乃伊不见了；她的石棺不见了。可那只国王永远供奉的凉鞋，奇迹般地保存了下来。请看。」

祭司们举着火把，照亮殿室正中一座水晶神龛，立在黑斑岩的桌上。水晶龛里躺着一只小小的凉鞋，像孩子的，却又分明是女人的，红色小皮凉鞋，缀着金饰，卷草纹样闪着难以置信的鲜亮。

「专给游人看的凉鞋。」卡图卢斯叔父带着怀疑的笑嘟哝道。「回头咱们又要为它掏钱了，卡莱布，跟看小阿皮斯一个样。」

「可它真漂亮，大人。」卡莱布含笑悄声道。

===Original===
But Lucius was trembling in every limb. And he said to Thrasyllus:

“This is an omen. I had never heard of this legend. This sandal, in this shrine!﻿ ﻿… I would be alone with the priest!”

The request of so distinguished a noble could not be gainsaid. The others withdrew, after fixing two torches in sconces. Lucius remained alone with the old priest, by the shrine of Rhodopis’ sandal. And then he produced Ilia’s little sandal from his breast and said:

“Wise priest and holy father, you possess wisdom, you assuredly still know the past. I have confidence in you: you shall tell me where the girl Ilia is, whom I have lost; you shall tell me who stole her from me. See, this sandal is the only trace that she left behind her. Tell me the past and I will reward you richly.”

===Chinese===
可卢基乌斯四肢都在发抖。他对特拉叙卢斯说：

「这是个预兆。我从没听过这个传说。这只凉鞋，在这座龛里！……我要单独同祭司待一会儿！」

这样显赫的贵人的请求无法回绝。众人把两支火把插上壁座后便退了出去。卢基乌斯独自与老祭司留在罗多庇斯凉鞋的神龛旁。于是他从怀里取出伊利娅的小凉鞋，说：

「智慧的祭司，圣洁的父，您拥有智慧，您一定还知晓过去。我信得过您：请您告诉我，我失去的姑娘伊利娅在哪里；请您告诉我，是谁把她从我身边偷走的。瞧，这只凉鞋是她留下的唯一痕迹。告诉我过去，我会重重酬谢您。」

===Original===
The priest took the sandal and pressed it to his head, while his other hand trembled above the crystal shrine:

“May the spirit of Rhodopis enlighten me,” said the old priest. “I see Ilia.﻿ ﻿…”

“Dead?”

“No, alive.”

“Alone?”

“No, with her kidnapper.”

===Chinese===
祭司接过凉鞋，按在头上，另一只手在水晶龛上方颤抖：

「愿罗多庇斯的灵启明我，」老祭司说。「我看见伊利娅了。……」

「死了？」

「不，活着。」

「独自一人？」

「不，同绑架她的人在一起。」

===Original===
“Do you see her kidnapper?”

“Yes.”

“Describe him to me!”

“Give me your hand, here, above Rhodopis’ sandal.”

Lucius stretched out his hand to the priest, above the sandal:

“Describe him to me!” he repeated.

===Chinese===
「您看得见绑架她的人？」

「看得见。」

「把他描绘给我听！」

「把你的手给我，这儿，放在罗多庇斯的凉鞋上方。」

卢基乌斯朝祭司伸出手，悬在凉鞋上方：

「把他描绘给我听！」他重复道。

===Original===
And in his tortured mind he saw before him the image of one of his own sailors, of whom he had been thinking lately, who at that time used to prowl about the villa at Baiae: a Cypriote whom he had once caught talking to Ilia in the oleanders; she had never been able to explain what he was doing.

There was a pause. The priest’s lean hand trembled violently in Lucius’ firm grasp. And at last the priest said, with his eyes closed and his other hand still pressing Ilia’s sandal to his forehead:

“I see him, plainly, plainly! Rhodopis’ spirit is enlightening me! I see the kidnapper! I see Ilia’s kidnapper!”

===Chinese===
在他备受煎熬的心里，眼前浮现出他一名水手的身影——近来他一直想着此人——那时此人常在巴伊亚（Baiae）的别墅周围转悠：一个塞浦路斯人，他曾在夹竹桃丛里撞见此人与伊利娅说话；她始终没能解释那人在做什么。

一阵沉默。祭司瘦削的手在卢基乌斯坚实的握持里剧烈发抖。终于，祭司闭着眼说道，另一只手仍把伊利娅的凉鞋按在额上：

「我看见他了，清清楚楚，清清楚楚！罗多庇斯的灵在启明我！我看见那绑架者了！我看见伊利娅的绑架者了！」

===Original===
“Is he tall?”

“He is tall.”

“Broad?”

“He has broad shoulders﻿ ﻿… and a coarse face; he is of a coarse beauty which women sometimes like, which unworthy women prefer to noble beauty, because they prefer rude passion to love.﻿ ﻿… Rhodopis’ chaste spirit is over me! I see the kidnapper.”

===Chinese===
「他高吗？」

「他高。」

「壮吗？」

「他肩膀宽……脸粗犷；他有种粗野的俊，女人有时喜欢这种俊，不配的女人宁要它不要高贵的俊美，因为她们宁要粗暴的情欲不要爱。……罗多庇斯贞洁的灵覆在我身上！我看见那绑架者了。」

===Original===
“How is he clad? As a slave?”

“No.”

“As a freedman?”

“No.”

“As a freeman?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「他穿着什么？像奴隶？」

「不像。」

「像被释奴？」

「不像。」

「像自由民？」

「像。」

===Original===
“As a patrician? A knight?”

“No.”

“As a soldier?”

“No.”

“As a sailor?”

“No. Yes, he is clad as a sailor, I think, my lord. But I no longer see him,” said the priest, opening his eyes. “And I shall never be able to tell you anything more.”

===Chinese===
「像贵族？像骑士？」

「不像。」

「像士兵？」

「不像。」

「像水手？」

「不像。像，我想他是穿得像个水手，大人。可我再也看不见他了。」祭司说着睁开眼睛。「而且我永远无法再告诉你什么了。」

===Original===
He gave Lucius back the sandal. The other priests returned, took up the torches. Quivering with suppressed rage, Lucius walked out of the black pyramid. Uncle Catullus was already sitting on his camel.

Lucius also mounted his. The Cypriote’s image now stood clearly before his eyes. But he said nothing; his lips were tightly shut, his forehead frowned; his grief seemed to be restrained and subdued in his heart by his outraged pride.

And, while Caleb paid the lordly fee, as he always did, Lucius slipped into the old priest’s hand a purse heavy with gold.

===Chinese===
他把凉鞋还给卢基乌斯。其他祭司回来了，举起火把。卢基乌斯强抑着狂怒，浑身颤抖着走出黑金字塔。卡图卢斯叔父已经骑上了他的骆驼。

卢基乌斯也上了驼。那塞浦路斯人的模样此刻清清楚楚立在他眼前。可他一言不发；双唇紧闭，眉头紧锁；他的悲伤仿佛被受辱的骄傲克制着、压服在心里。

当卡莱布像往常一样付着昂贵的香资时，卢基乌斯悄悄塞进老祭司手里一袋沉甸甸的黄金。

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===
The short twilight had deepened to purple over the desert; night came gliding along the firmament; the stars began to peep. And Caleb, who suspected Lucius’ emotion at each fresh divination, considered that new impressions would be the best medicine for him. After a short deliberation with Uncle Catullus and Thrasyllus, he said:

“My noble lord, before the night has quite fallen, I should like to take you to the great Neith﻿ ﻿… for the sake both of the statue itself and of the Jewish prophet, a hermit, who dwells in a cave hard by.”

Lucius nodded his approval. And in the falling night he sat erect on the saddle-pad of his camel and raised his head towards the stars. Had he guessed the truth? Had the truth gradually been revealed within him? Or had the sibyl, Amphris, the oracle and the priests whom he had consulted really shown him the way to that truth? He did not know, he had so many vague memories that it all grew confused in his seeking, solving brain.﻿ ﻿… But he certainly was the Cypriote, the sailor, Carus﻿ ﻿… who, shortly before Ilia’s disappearance, had himself disappeared from the crew of the quadrireme﻿ ﻿… and whom he had once found with Ilia among the oleanders! A thing which she had never been able to explain! Carus! A sailor! Not a slave, it was true, but one of his meanest servants! A Cyprian sailor, to have robbed him of the woman who reigned as queen over his house, whom he dressed like a goddess, whom he covered with everything that was precious! And she must have been kidnapped﻿—it could not be otherwise﻿—with her own consent, her own, infatuated consent.

Had he guessed the truth? Had his groping brain at last divined the truth? Or had the priests and the oracles and Amphris and the sibyl indeed revealed the truth to him? He decided that they must have done so. His soul was inclined to accept the supernatural. And he knew, he *knew*, thanks to the wise knowledge of the priests and the oracles.

===Chinese===
短暂的暮色已沉成沙漠上的紫色；夜沿着天穹滑来；星辰开始眨眼。卡莱布察觉卢基乌斯每经一次新的占卜便激动一分，认为新的印象于他就是最好的药。同卡图卢斯叔父和特拉叙卢斯略作商议后，他说：

「尊贵的大人，趁夜还没完全落定，我想带您去看伟大的奈斯……一来为那座石像本身，二来为那位犹太先知——一个隐士，就住在近旁的洞里。」

卢基乌斯点头应允。夜色四合里，他挺直地坐在驼鞍上，仰头望向星辰。是他猜着了真相？还是真相在他心里渐渐自行显明？抑或真是那女先知、安弗里斯、神谕和他求问过的众祭司，给他指点了通往真相的路？他不知道；他的记忆太多太乱，在那寻索求解的头脑里全搅成了一团。……可那人确实就是那个塞浦路斯人，那个水手，卡鲁斯（Carus）……就在伊利娅失踪前不久，他从四列桨船（quadrireme）的水手名册上自行消失了……而他曾在夹竹桃丛里撞见此人与伊利娅在一起！那件事她始终解释不清！卡鲁斯！一个水手！不是奴隶，不错，可也不过是他最卑微的仆役之一！一个塞浦路斯水手，竟夺走了他家中王后一般的女人——他把她装扮得像女神，用一切珍宝把她堆罩起来！而她必定是自愿被劫走的——不可能不是这样——带着她自己的同意，她自己的、鬼迷心窍的同意。

是他猜着了真相？是他摸索的头脑终于参透了真相？还是祭司们、神谕们、安弗里斯和女先知果真向他揭示了真相？他断定必是后者。他的灵魂倾向于接受超自然。他知道了，他*知道了*，托祭司与神谕的智慧之福。

===Original===
So she had been able to leave him, *him*, for his hired sailor! He raised his head towards the stars. His lips were tightly clenched, his forehead frowned. But never, he resolved, would his lips utter to anyone, not even to Thrasyllus, the secret truth which the oracles had revealed to him. He would be silent and his pride would suppress his grief.

“Look, my lord,” said Caleb, while Lucius still stared straight before him, up, towards the stars.

Lucius lowered his eyes. And suddenly he gave a start. The Sphinx loomed before him in the night. In the immense starry night, with the sands glittering all around like a silver sea, loomed the immense Neith, the omniscient wisdom. It was more gigantic than any sphinx that he had ever seen.

It had been shaped by Nature herself out of an immense monolith. Human hands had only reshapen it more plainly for human eyes﻿ ﻿… into the Sphinx. It was not the veiled Isis of Sais; it was the unveiled, silent knowledge, which had known everything from the beginning of time. It raised its head towards the stars﻿ ﻿… as he had done. It was resting: its lioness-body rested and sank into the sand; its forepaws projected like walls. Its superhuman breasts seemed to heave in the night. Its fixed eyes stared upwards and its granite veil stood out upon its lioness-body. It was awesomely beautiful in the starry night.

===Chinese===
这么说，她竟舍得离开他，离开*他*，为了他雇来的一个水手！他仰起头望向星辰。双唇紧咬，眉头深锁。但他决意，他的唇永不对任何人吐露神谕向他揭示的秘密真相，连特拉叙卢斯也不告诉。他要沉默，让骄傲压住悲伤。

「看，大人。」卡莱布说；卢基乌斯仍直望前方，望向上方的星辰。

卢基乌斯垂下眼来。忽然他一惊。斯芬克斯在夜里赫然耸现在他面前。在浩瀚的星夜里，四围的沙闪亮如银色的海，巨大的奈斯赫然耸现，那全知的智慧。它比他见过的任何斯芬克斯都更巨大。

它是大自然亲手从一整块巨岩里塑成的。人手只是为人类的眼睛把它修琢得更分明……修成斯芬克斯。它不是塞易斯那蒙纱的伊西斯；它是揭了面纱的、沉默的知识，自太初便知晓一切。它仰头朝向星辰……正如他方才那样。它在歇息：母狮的身躯歇在沙里，沉进沙中；前爪像两道墙壁般探出。它超人的双乳仿佛在夜里起伏。它凝固的眼睛向上凝望，花岗岩的面纱凸现在母狮的身躯上。星夜里，它美得令人敬畏。

===Original===
The travellers had alighted. And Caleb had fetched the Jewish hermit from the cave in which he lived, opposite the Sphinx.

“I believe he’s mad,” said Caleb, timidly, a little alarmed by Lucius’ frown. “But it doesn’t matter if he is mad. He is the Jewish hermit; and all distinguished foreigners, such as your lordship, listen to him﻿ ﻿… because he says strange things.”

“He too!” muttered Lucius.

The Jewish hermit came up to them in the fallen night. He was of giant stature and incredibly old; his beard fell in waves down to his waist. His grey robe trailed over the sand. And he exclaimed, in a loud voice:

“I am Tsafnath-Paeneach, ‘he who reveals mysteries!’ I am of the tribe of Joseph himself, who took to wife Asenath the daughter of Potipherah priest of On! In me was the wisdom of Joseph, who interpreted dreams, and the wisdom of the priests of On! But all wisdom is dead in me, Jahve be praised, since I beheld *Him*!”

===Chinese===
旅人们下了驼。卡莱布已从那洞里请来犹太隐士——他就住在斯芬克斯对面。

「我看他是疯了，」卡莱布怯怯地说，被卢基乌斯的眉头吓了一跳。「不过疯了也不要紧。他是犹太隐士；凡是尊贵的外邦人，比如大人您，都听他讲……因为他说的事情稀奇。」

「他也是！」卢基乌斯咕哝道。

犹太隐士在落定的夜色里向他们走来。他身量如巨人，老得难以置信；胡须一波一波直垂到腰。灰袍在沙上拖曳。他放声喊道：

「我是撒发那忒-巴内亚（Tsafnath-Paeneach），『揭示奥秘的人』！我出于约瑟本支，约瑟娶了安城（On）祭司波提非拉（Potipherah）的女儿亚西纳（Asenath）为妻！在我里面，曾有解梦人约瑟的智慧，也有安城众祭司的智慧！但自从我亲眼得见*他*，我里面一切的智慧都死了，雅赫维当受赞美！」

===Original===
“Whom?” asked Lucius, dismayed by the prophet’s booming voice.

“It was a night of twinkling stars!” cried the prophet. “It was thirty years ago! I lived in my cave, as I do now! And I knew everything and I looked Neith in the face and in the eyes.﻿ ﻿… Along the road, yonder, through the sands﻿ ﻿… they came! They came, they came, they drew near.﻿ ﻿… On an ass that stumbled with fatigue sat a woman. A greybeard, staff in hand, led the stumbling beast. Then I saw that the woman held, pressed to her breast, in the folds of her mantle, a Child! And the woman was like Heva and like Isis; and the Child was like Habel and like Horus. When they came before the mighty Neith, the ass could stumble no farther through the sands of the desert. And the woman alighted and smiled upon the Child through her tears. But the greybeard led the woman to the mighty Neith and helped her to climb into its deep granite lap. There the woman rested against Neith’s bosom and the Child rested against the woman’s bosom. And then﻿ ﻿… then I saw, I, Tsafnath-Paeneach, I who reveal mysteries, that the Child that was like Habel and Horus was radiant in the night, in the folds of the woman’s mantle! The Child was radiant; a wreath of rays, a halo of light shone about the Child! The mother slept, the radiant Child slept, the greybeard slept﻿ ﻿… and the mighty Neith watched over their sleep in the starry night! Then, O Jahve, I knew that I had beheld Thy Son; and this happiness was my last wisdom. Since then I know nothing more, O Jahve, be praised! Since then I have discovered no mysteries! Since then the knowledge of Joseph has died away within me and that of the priests of On! For I have seen Jahve’s Son, there, there, in the lap of Neith﻿ ﻿… and since then I have seen nothing but that vision! And I shall die with the vision of the radiant Child before my eyes!”

===Chinese===
「见谁？」卢基乌斯问，被先知洪钟般的声音震住。

「那是一个星光闪烁的夜！」先知喊道。「那是三十年前！我住在我的洞里，就像如今一样！我无所不知，我直面奈斯的容貌和眼睛。……沿那条路，那边，穿过沙漠……他们来了！他们来了，来了，走近了。……一头疲惫得直打趔趄的驴上，坐着一个女人。一位白须老者，手拄着杖，牵着那踉跄的牲口。于是我看见，那女人把一个婴孩搂在怀里，掩在斗篷的褶里！那女人像夏娃（Heva），又像伊西斯；那婴孩像亚伯（Habel），又像荷鲁斯。他们来到伟大的奈斯面前时，驴再也无力在沙漠的沙里趔趄前行了。女人下了驴，含泪对着婴孩微笑。白须老者把女人领到伟大的奈斯跟前，扶她攀进那深深的花岗岩怀抱。女人偎着奈斯的胸膛安歇，婴孩偎着女人的胸膛。于是……于是我看见了，我，撒发那忒-巴内亚，我这揭示奥秘的人，看见那像亚伯又像荷鲁斯的婴孩，在夜色里，在女人斗篷的褶间，放着光！那婴孩放着光；一圈光环，一团光轮，罩着那婴孩发亮！母亲睡了，放光的婴孩睡了，白须老者睡了……而伟大的奈斯在星夜里守护着他们的睡眠！那时，哦雅赫维，我知道我亲眼见了你的儿子；这幸福便是我最后的智慧。从那以后我再无所知，哦雅赫维，当受赞美！从那以后我再没发现过什么奥秘！从那以后，约瑟的知识在我里面死了，安城祭司们的知识也死了！因为我看见了雅赫维的儿子，就在那儿，就在奈斯的怀抱里……从那以后，我眼中便只剩那异象！我将带着那放光婴孩的异象瞑目而死！」

===Original===
The prophet’s loud, booming voice had risen to a cry of joy; and Caleb repeated to Lucius, in a whisper:

“You see, my lord, he’s mad.”

But Thrasyllus, on the other side, whispered:

“He’s not mad, Lucius.﻿ ﻿… He is a seer.﻿ ﻿… He has seen.﻿ ﻿… He has perhaps seen the new God of Whom all the sibyls speak.﻿ ﻿…”

“Which new God?” asked Lucius.

===Chinese===
先知洪亮的声音已高扬成一声欢呼；卡莱布悄悄对卢基乌斯重复道：

「您瞧，大人，他疯了。」

可在另一边，特拉叙卢斯悄声道：

「他没有疯，卢基乌斯。……他是先见。……他看见过了。……他也许看见了那位新神——众女先知都在说着的那位。……」

「哪位新神？」卢基乌斯问。

===Original===
“I don’t know His name,” said Thrasyllus.

But Uncle Catullus spoke:

“My dear nephew, that great monstrous fellow frightens me, here in the dark, in the desert, in front of this awful statue. Egypt gives me too many impressions. I feel like a sponge full of water, so soaked am I with impressions. Egypt will be the death of me, Lucius, you’ll see it will! Meanwhile I propose to mount my camel.”

And Uncle Catullus called his guards and drivers and bade them make his camel kneel down for him.

===Chinese===
「我不知道他的名字。」特拉叙卢斯说。

卡图卢斯叔父开口了：

「我亲爱的侄儿，这个巨人怪物，在这黑地里、在沙漠上、在这尊骇人石像跟前，把我吓坏了。埃及给我的印象太多了。我觉得自己像一块吸饱了水的海绵，印象把我泡透了。埃及会断送我这把老骨头，卢基乌斯，你瞧着吧，一定会的！眼下呢，我提议上我的骆驼。」

于是卡图卢斯叔父唤来护卫和驼夫，叫他们让骆驼为他跪下。

===Original===
But Lucius went to the prophet and drew him aside:

“Do you know the past?” he asked, anxiously.

“The past?” echoed the Jewish seer, in an uncertain voice; and his eyes were as though blind.

“Do you see and can you tell me if that which I think has happened﻿ ﻿… is undoubtedly true?”

“I no longer see either the past or the future,” said the seer. “I see nothing but the present. And the present for me is nothing but﻿ ﻿… the radiant Child yonder!”

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯却走到先知跟前，把他拉到一旁：

「你知道过去吗？」他急切地问。

「过去？」犹太先见应声道，语声游移；他的眼睛仿佛盲了一般。

「你看得见吗？能不能告诉我，我心想发生过的那件事……是不是确凿无疑是真的？」

「我看不见过去，也看不见未来了，」先见说。「我只看得见现在。而对我来说，现在无非是……那边那个放光的婴孩！」

===Original===
“Who is He?” asked Lucius.

“I do not know, unless He be Jahve’s Son!” cried the seer. “He was like Habel, he was like Horus. But I do not know, unless He be Jahve’s Son!”

Thrasyllus approached:

“Lucius,” he said, “let us go. The night is falling and the guards have warned us against wild animals and robbers.”

“Let Caleb give the prophet a gold piece,” said Lucius.

===Chinese===
「他是谁？」卢基乌斯问。

「我不知道，除非他就是雅赫维的儿子！」先见喊道。「他像亚伯，他像荷鲁斯。可我不知道，除非他就是雅赫维的儿子！」

特拉叙卢斯走近前来：

「卢基乌斯，」他说，「我们走吧。夜深了，护卫们警告过我们，当心野兽和盗匪。」

「让卡莱布给先知一枚金币。」卢基乌斯说。

===Original===
Caleb produced a stater; but the prophet’s laugh of thunder sent him staggering back:

“Gold!” cried the prophet, laughing like thunder. “What do I want with dead gold! I have seen living gold; I have seen the Child That was radiant gold as the sun itself, radiant as the burning bush! What do I want with dead gold!”

“He’s mad! He’s mad!” cried Caleb. “He doesn’t want gold!”

And, terrified, Caleb slipped back the stater﻿—but into another purse, in which he collected his savings﻿—and rushed to his camel, which was already kneeling in the sand.

In the light of the stars that twinkled over the sea of sand the travellers rode back to Memphis.

===Chinese===
卡莱布掏出一枚斯塔特金币；可先知那雷鸣般的笑声吓得他踉跄后退：

「金子！」先知喊道，笑声如雷。「死金子要来何用！我见过活的金子；我见过那婴孩，放光的金子，像太阳本身，亮得像燃烧的荆棘！死金子要来何用！」

「他疯了！他疯了！」卡莱布喊道。「他连金子都不要！」

卡莱布吓得把斯塔特塞了回去——却塞进了另一只钱袋，他攒私房钱的那只——飞奔向他的骆驼，那骆驼已在沙里跪好了。

在沙海之上闪烁的星光里，旅人们骑驼返回孟斐斯。

## XX / 二十

===Original===
Uncle Catullus lay under the awning of the thalamegus and asked Cora to come and sit by him:

“Sing and play me some cheerful songs, Cora,” he said. “Be kind to me even though I be not your master. For I feel bored here, on this Nile boat, at Memphis. I have been bored ever since Lucius went to the oracle of Ammon, through the barren desert. What an idea, what a mad idea! They have been gone five days now; they will probably arrive tomorrow.﻿ ﻿… I am bored, Cora, I am horribly bored. Egypt will be the death of me! First I am saturated with new impressions, like a sponge with water, and then Lucius abandons me to unlimited boredom. He’s an egoist; he never thinks of his old uncle.﻿ ﻿… Cora, be amiable to me and sing and play me some cheerful songs, won’t you?”

This was the burden of Uncle Catullus’ complaint. As he said, Lucius had gone through the desert to the oracle of Ammon, with Caleb, Thrasyllus and Tarrar, with guards and drivers, and Uncle Catullus had remained behind on the barge, under the care of Rufus the under-steward, with all the other slaves, male and female.

===Chinese===
卡图卢斯叔父躺在泰拉美戈斯（thalamegus）游船的天篷下，唤科拉来坐在自己身边：

“给我唱几支快活的歌吧，科拉，”他说，“哪怕我不是你的主人，也请你对我好一点。因为在这儿，在这条尼罗河船上，在孟斐斯，我觉得闷得慌。自从卢基乌斯穿过荒凉的沙漠去阿蒙神谕所，我就一直发闷。这算什么主意，多疯的主意！他们已经走了五天，多半明天就到。……我闷，科拉，我闷得要死。埃及会要了我的老命！起初新奇的印象把我灌得满满的，像海绵吸足了水，然后卢基乌斯就把我扔给无穷无尽的烦闷。他是个自私鬼；从来不想想他这老叔叔。……科拉，你对我亲热些，给我唱几支快活的歌，好不好？”

卡图卢斯叔父的抱怨，翻来覆去就是这么几句。正如他所说，卢基乌斯带着卡莱布、特拉叙卢斯和塔拉尔，带着卫兵和赶驼人，穿过沙漠到阿蒙神谕所去了；卡图卢斯叔父则留在船上，由副管家鲁弗斯照料，还有其余所有的男女奴隶。

===Original===
A track led from Memphis through the desert to the oasis where the oracle of Ammon resided. It ran through the sands marked with granite posts, like small obelisks, nothing more. It was a chain of signposts rather than a road. The summer sun beat down implacably upon the scorching sands, which lay blown against the rocky range of mountains along the south of which the road was traced.

The caravan had now been travelling five days through the sands. Lucius, on an elephant, lay in a spacious, square litter, with blue and yellow curtains to keep out the light, and had expressed a wish that Thrasyllus should sit by his side. Caleb, swathed in white muslin, which left only his gleaming eyes and flashing teeth visible, sat upon a powerful dromedary, on leather cushions, under a great parasol fixed to his saddle-gear, and occasionally swaying gently to and fro. Elephant and dromedary were surrounded with long fly-nets, from which dangled many-coloured fringes. Tarrar, also swathed in linens of many colours, squatted like a little monkey on a camel and defied the sun of his native land, the glare of his Libyan desert. The guards and drivers rode mules; and ponies carried the travellers’ luggage, their tents, their provisions and their still swollen water-bags.

For five days now they had been marching on their monotonous journey through the desert. At break of day the caravan started; at noon a halt was made under the tents; in the evening the procession moved on again, until darkness and fatigue urged the travellers to rest. It was an endless journey. It seemed as if the goal would never be reached. It was an unrelieved alternation of gold-glittering sands, under implacable, blazing skies, and fading sands, under endless skies of nocturnal blue. It was an unrelieved alternation of rosy sunrises and orange sunsets. It was an unrelieved alternation of the peeping, the radiant awakening and the duller waning of the stars. Sometimes the south wind rose and blew for hours. Silently the caravan plodded through the rising whirls of sand. Sometimes the faint track of posts seemed to have disappeared; the obelisks stood aslant, sunk into the sands. A melancholy descended over man and beast.

===Chinese===
从孟斐斯有一条小径穿过沙漠，通向阿蒙神谕所所在的绿洲。它蜿蜒于沙海之间，以花岗岩界标为记，那些界标像小小的方尖碑，仅此而已。那与其说是路，不如说是一串路标。夏日的骄阳无情地炙烤着灼热的沙地；道路沿着一道岩石山脉的南麓延伸，飞沙便被风吹得堆积在这道山岭上。

商队此刻已在沙海中走了五天。卢基乌斯骑在一头大象上，偎在方形宽敞的轿舆里，挂着蓝黄两色的帘子遮光，他表示希望特拉叙卢斯坐在自己身边。卡莱布裹着白细麻布，只露出灼灼放光的眼睛和闪亮的牙齿，骑在一头健壮的单峰驼上，垫着皮坐垫，撑着一把固定在鞍具上的大阳伞，身子不时轻轻摇晃。大象和单峰驼四周罩着长长的防蝇网，网上垂着五彩缤纷的流苏。塔拉尔也裹着五颜六色的麻布，像只小猴子似的蹲在骆驼上，硬顶着故土的太阳，顶着那利比亚荒漠刺眼的强光。卫兵和赶驼人骑着骡子；小马驮着旅行者的行李、帐篷、口粮和仍然鼓胀的水袋。

五天来，他们就这样在沙漠里单调地行进。破晓时分商队启程；正午在帐篷下歇晌；傍晚队伍再度上路，直到黑暗与疲惫催促旅行者歇息。这是一场无穷无尽的旅程。目标仿佛永远到不了。那是无休止的交替：金光闪烁的沙海顶着无情燃烧的天空，又渐渐黯淡下去，顶着无垠的夜蓝色天穹。那是无休止的交替：玫瑰色的日出与橙红的日落。那是无休止的交替：星辰的初现、灿然的苏醒与黯然的凋落。有时南风刮起，一吹就是几个钟头。商队默默地在腾起的沙旋中跋涉。有时那条依稀的标径仿佛消失了；方尖碑似的界标歪斜着，半埋进沙里。一阵忧郁笼罩了人与牲畜。

===Original===
The midday meal, taken under a tent, Lucius shared with Thrasyllus, Caleb, and Tarrar. It consisted always of broiled mutton, dates and an unvarying ration of water, with a dash of palm-wine in it. Strange to say, Lucius was almost cheerful and declared that Uncle Catullus had done well not to accompany them to the oasis of Ammon, as such meals would certainly have been a sore trial for him. And, strange again, Caleb, usually so merry and cheerful, became despondent and sad. At least, he exclaimed, now that Lucius began to jest:

“I wonder, my lord, that you can be gay in these godforsaken Libyan sands! They weigh upon my chest, O my lord, as though I were already sinking under them, like the obelisks and sphinxes! O my noble lord, O my princely lord, what a desperate idea of your lordship’s to wish to undertake this awful journey, to wish to go to the oracle of Ammon, which is quite ruined and deserted, whither perhaps for two centuries past no noble lord like your lordship has ever travelled! O my lord, O my lord, if only this horrible journey ends well! The drivers and guards are not yet complaining; there is still water in the bags for men and beasts; we have not yet experienced any other adventure beyond the appearance of one lion, who stood proudly on the point of a rock but fled when he saw my burnous flapping in the distance, while our hunters tried to shoot him with poisoned arrows. But my lord, if more lions appear, or if robbers suddenly come in sight, or if those terrible ghosts loom up: the sphinxes with the human heads and the giants with faces of animals, which, people say, fill the desert; or if we meet the giant snake, who has a forest growing on his back and who makes his nest underground and who, when hungry, bores his terrible body right through the earth’s flat disk and swallows towns and villages, O my lord, then I doubt, alas, whether my flapping burnous and the bows and arrows of our hunters and guards will save us! O my lord, O my gracious, noble lord, shall I ever see Saba again, my dear country blessed by the gods!”

===Chinese===
帐篷下用的午饭，是卢基乌斯与特拉叙卢斯、卡莱布和塔拉尔同吃的。饭食总是烤羊肉、椰枣和一份定量的水，水里掺一点棕榈酒。说来奇怪，卢基乌斯几乎兴致高昂，还宣称卡图卢斯叔父没有跟他们同来阿蒙绿洲是明智的，因为这样的饭食对他肯定是一场苦刑。而同样奇怪的是，平日那么快活开朗的卡莱布，却变得垂头丧气、愁眉苦脸。至少，当卢基乌斯开始说笑打趣时，他嚷道：

“我真奇怪，主上，您在这片神明抛弃的利比亚沙海里居然还能快活！它们压在我的胸口上，噢主上，我仿佛已经在它们底下往下沉了，像那些方尖碑和斯芬克斯一样！噢我高贵的主上、王侯一般的主上，大人是怎么想出这么个绝望的主意，要走这趟可怕的旅程，要去阿蒙神谕所——那地方早已倾颓荒废，也许两百年来就没有一位像大人这样的贵主去过！噢主上，主上，但愿这可怕的旅程结局平安！赶驼人和卫兵还没有抱怨；人畜的水袋里还有水；我们迄今除了遇见一头狮子，还没碰上别的历险——那狮子骄傲地立在岩尖上，可它远远望见我的布努斯（burnous）飘动就逃了，那时我们的猎手还想用毒箭射它。可是主上，若是再来更多的狮子，或是强盗突然出现，或是那些可怕的鬼影赫然现形：什么人首的斯芬克斯、兽面的巨人，据说满沙漠都是；或是我们遇上那条巨蛇——它背上长着一座森林，在地底下做窝，饿了便把它那可怕的身躯笔直地钻穿大地的圆盘，吞掉城镇和村庄——噢主上，那我就要怀疑，唉，我这件飘动的布努斯和我们的猎手卫兵的弓箭，还能不能救我们了！噢主上，噢我仁慈高贵的主上，我还能再见到萨巴吗，我那蒙诸神祝福的亲爱的故国！”

===Original===
Thus ran Caleb’s complaint; but Lucius said:

“Tarrar is seeing his country again, aren’t you, Tarrar?”

“Yes, my lord,” said the little slave, “but I come from the seacoast, not from the desert, and I was not happy in my country and my parents gave me no food to eat and the country is not beautiful either, as Saba is, and I would much rather be with you, in Rome, for that is the loveliest country in the world, and in your house, which is the loveliest house in the world.”

===Chinese===
卡莱布的抱怨就是这些；可卢基乌斯说：

“塔拉尔可是在重见故国了，是不是，塔拉尔？”

“是啊，主人，”小奴隶说，“可我是海边来的，不是沙漠来的；我在自己的国家过得不好，爹妈不给我饭吃；那地方也不美，不像萨巴；我更愿意跟着您，在罗马，因为那是世界上最可爱的国家，还要住在您的宅子里，那是世界上最可爱的宅子。”

===Original===
After the midday rest the journey was resumed and the sun sank slowly: the sky was like a glowing copper dome, which dulled and cooled; and the stars came out; and over the rocky crests that rose on ridges along the road appeared the flying figures of wild animals. Startling roars sounded in the night, to the great alarm of Caleb, who said that he did not mind lions or hyenas but that he was afraid of the giants and the colossal snake and the ghosts of the desert, which lured travellers to the magic cities which are nothing but hallucination, enchantment and destruction. And all the drivers and guards, sturdy Libyans and Arabs, were like him and said that they did not fear the tangible lions and would hunt them if need be, but that they did fear the intangible lions of the desert, all the haunting, shadowy visions of wrath with which Typhon lures the caravans into Hell.

Then great fires were kindled, to ward off the lions and the ghosts; and they glowed in the still glowing night; and the guards and drivers danced fantastic dances round the fires; and Caleb, to forget his alarm, joined in the dance.

But Thrasyllus told his master about Alexander the Great. When Alexander founded Alexandria, the oracle of Ammon was the most celebrated in Egypt; and Callisthenes and Plutarch relate how the great Macedonian started from Paraetonium, on the coast, to make his way through the desert by way of the oasis. Violent south-winds attacked his retinue; but he did not give in, though sandstorms nearly swallowed him up, with his elephants and camels. Suddenly, however, kindly showers fell, at the bidding of the gods, and the winds abated and the sandstorms dropped. Two crows flew beside the great Alexander and guided him to the oasis.

===Chinese===
午休之后旅程继续，太阳缓缓西沉：天空像一座烧红的铜穹顶，渐渐黯淡、冷却下来；星星出来了；沿着大路脊线隆起的岩峰上，现出野兽飞掠的身影。黑夜里响起吓人的咆哮，把卡莱布吓得魂不附体；他说他倒不怕狮子和鬣狗，他怕的是那些巨人和巨蛇，怕沙漠里的鬼魅——它们把旅行者诱向那些魔城，那无非是幻象、蛊惑与毁灭。所有赶驼人和卫兵——那些健壮的利比亚人和阿拉伯人——都跟他一样，说他们不怕摸得着的狮子，必要时还要猎它们，怕的是沙漠里那些摸不着的狮子，怕堤丰用来把商队诱进地狱的一切作祟的、阴森的怒影。

于是燃起一堆堆大火，用来驱避狮子和鬼魅；火光在仍然灼热的夜里熊熊燃烧；卫兵和赶驼人围着火堆跳起奇异的舞蹈；卡莱布为了忘掉惊恐，也加入了跳舞。

而特拉叙卢斯给主人讲起亚历山大大帝的旧事。亚历山大建亚历山大里亚之时，阿蒙神谕所是埃及最负盛名的神谕所；卡利斯忒涅斯（Callisthenes）和普鲁塔克（Plutarch）都记述过，这位马其顿伟人如何从海边的帕拉托尼乌姆（Paraetonium）出发，取道绿洲穿越沙漠。狂暴的南风袭击他的随行队伍；但他不肯屈服，尽管沙暴险些把他连同大象和骆驼一起吞没。然而，应诸神之命，忽然降下温润的阵雨，风势平息，沙暴止歇。两只乌鸦飞在大亚历山大身边，引他到了绿洲。

===Original===
At the first ray of dawn, after a refreshing sleep, the journey was resumed, the monotonous journey, the endless journey. It was the last day but one; and, when the halt was called, it appeared that the drivers and guards had cut open the water-bags and drunk their fill of the water. Caleb grew furious and instantly drew his dagger and wanted to fling himself on the Libyans and Arabs; but they also drew their daggers and everybody shouted and screamed and yelled. Then Lucius intervened and quieted them all and gave them money; and they fell on their knees and sobbed and begged his pardon for drinking up the water, but they had been so thirsty and they accused Caleb of being too sparing with the ration. And Caleb defended himself and said that in the desert one had to be sparing and not gulp down all the water at once, without thinking of the morrow, of the animals and of the noble lord, who now had not a drop of water left. But the noble lord caused a heavy basket full of lemons to be let down from his elephant and gave each of the guards and drivers one lemon and told them that they must now hold out, by sucking this lemon, until they reached the oasis. And they kissed his hands and abased themselves before him and caressed his legs and called him Osiris and Serapis and Ammon-Râ and their life’s benefactor.

Men and animals were exhausted, but they allowed themselves hardly any rest that night and no one slept and all wanted to go on, ever on, in the last spurring of their energies.

Was it, after the sleepless night, because of that exhaustion and that last spurring, an atmospheric phenomenon, an hallucination, an illusion, a fata morgana? In the first rosy glimmers of the dawn, reflected from the east to the west, there rose in the west as it were a dream, a nebulous dream-vision of unsubstantial forms, the vague paradisial vision of barely-outlined, rose-tinted trees, slender, shadowy trunks and palm-crowns suffused in rosy light; and then the straight lines, no more than an azure shadow, of walls, of roofs, terraces, domes.

===Chinese===
破晓的第一线曙光里，一觉解乏之后，旅程又开始了，单调的旅程，无穷的旅程。这是倒数第二天；歇脚的号令一下，人们才发现，赶驼人和卫兵早已割开水袋，把水喝了个够。卡莱布勃然大怒，立刻拔出匕首，要扑向那些利比亚人和阿拉伯人；可他们也拔出匕首，人人叫嚷、嘶喊、咆哮。于是卢基乌斯出面调停，安抚了众人，给了他们钱；他们跪倒在地，抽泣着求他宽恕喝光了水，可他们实在太渴了，还抱怨卡莱布配给得太抠。卡莱布为自己辩护，说在沙漠里就得节省，不能一口气把水喝个精光，不想想明天，不想想牲口，不想想贵主——如今连一滴水也不剩了。可这位贵主吩咐从大象上卸下一大筐沉甸甸的柠檬，给每个卫兵和赶驼人发了一个，告诉他们如今必须靠吮着这个柠檬，撑到绿洲。他们亲吻他的手，在他面前匍匐，抚抱他的腿，唤他俄西里斯、萨拉皮斯、阿蒙-拉，唤他救命恩人。

人和牲畜都已精疲力竭，可那晚他们几乎不让自己歇息，谁也不睡，人人都想往前走，一个劲儿往前走，凭着最后一股狠劲。

那不眠之夜过后，是因为那份疲惫和那最后一股狠劲吗？是大气的幻景，是幻觉，是错觉，是海市蜃楼吗？黎明最初的玫瑰色微光从东方映到西方，西方便仿佛升起了一个梦，一场朦胧的梦影，虚浮不实的形体，一个隐约的乐园之景——淡淡的、玫瑰色的树木，纤细朦胧的树干，浸在玫瑰色光晕里的棕榈树冠；然后是笔直的线条——墙垣、屋顶、露台、穹顶，不过是一道天青色的影子。

===Original===
Was it a vision, was it a dream? No, it was real; and Caleb jubilantly pointed and shouted:

“Ammon-Râ! Ammon-Râ!”

“Ammon-Râ! Ammon-Râ!” repeated the guards and drivers, yelling wildly and cheering like madmen, for the oasis took colour, the trees became more clearly marked and the temple, large as a town, now stretched its walls impressively.

The horses sniffed the air and neighed, the elephants waved their trunks, the camels swung out their legs, the men thrust forward their throats and inhaled the fragrance of verdure and the coolness of running wells; and the inhabitants of the oasis, poor natives in the service of the priests of the temple, poured out of their huts to meet the caravan and knelt in the road, offering split coconuts, juicy oranges and scarlet fruits, of strange shapes and juicy pulp, and earthenware dishes full of water limpid as flowing crystal.

===Chinese===
那是幻影吗，是梦吗？不，是真的；卡莱布欣喜若狂地指着前方高喊：

“阿蒙-拉！阿蒙-拉！”

“阿蒙-拉！阿蒙-拉！”卫兵和赶驼人跟着狂喊，疯了似的欢呼，因为绿洲已经有了颜色，树木的轮廓渐渐分明，那座大得像城镇的神庙，此刻巍然展开它的垣墙。

马儿嗅着空气嘶鸣，大象挥舞长鼻，骆驼迈开大步，人们伸长脖颈，吸着草木的芬芳和活水井的清凉；绿洲的居民——神庙祭司们手下的穷苦土人——涌出棚屋迎接商队，跪在道旁，献上剖开的椰子、水灵的橙子和猩红的果子——形状奇异、果肉多汁——还有盛满清水的陶盘，那水清得像流动的水晶。

## XXI / 二十一

===Original===
There was a dense wood of palm-trees through which the travellers made their way to the temple of Ammon-Râ, whose walls lay spread like a town.

“See, my lord,” said Caleb, walking ahead and pointing, “these are male palm-trees; and those more slender ones are female; and they marry one another, my lord, and feel love for one another; they grow towards one another, see, my lord, like these two; and they wave to one another and the male fructifies the female; and it is only when they love each other that the fruits are luxuriant and their honey and wine pleasant to the taste.”

“It is as Caleb says,” Tarrar assented. “The palm-trees in my country marry one another and they are the most excellent in the whole world.”

“They also marry in Saba,” said Caleb, in pique. “We have sweeter honey and date-wine in Saba than you have here in Libya.”

A heated discussion arose between Caleb and Tarrar upon the respective merits of the Sabaean and Libyan palm-trees. But the travellers were now entering the first gate of the temple.

===Chinese===
旅行者们穿过一片茂密的棕榈树林，向阿蒙-拉神庙走去，神庙的垣墙如同一座城镇般铺展开来。

“看，主上，”卡莱布走在前面指点着说，“这些是雄棕榈；那些更纤细的是雌棕榈；它们彼此成婚，主上，彼此怀着爱恋；它们朝着对方生长，看哪，主上，就像这两棵；它们互相挥动枝叶，雄树使雌树结实；只有两情相悦时，果实才丰美，它们的蜜和酒才可口。”

“卡莱布说得对，”塔拉尔附和道，“我家乡的棕榈也彼此成婚，它们是全世界最出色的棕榈。”

“萨巴的棕榈也成婚，”卡莱布赌气地说，“我们的蜜和椰枣酒，比你们利比亚这儿的甜。”

卡莱布和塔拉尔就萨巴棕榈与利比亚棕榈的高下争执起来，愈争愈热。但旅行者们这时已走进神庙的第一道门。

===Original===
There was a triple row of walls round the old sanctuary, but they were falling into ruins, the obelisks were sinking away, the sphinxes were covered with luxuriant, flowering creepers, tall grasses shot up between the flagstones of the dromos and all the doors were open. There was a deep shade from the leafy tops of the turpentine-trees, which were fragrant with heavy perfumes in the sunshine. The fleshy aloes drove their sword-like leaves over the walls; and their long stalks blossomed with huge scarlet flowers which smoked as though with incense. But it was above all the daturas whose pendant alabaster goblets poured forth a giddiness, an intoxication of heavy scents, around which the great Atlas moths flew slowly hovering.

There were no doorkeepers; and the travellers walked on, through the endless dromos. The monolithic colossi rose on either hand; but they also were shelving to one side, or sinking away. Lastly, from out of the vista of the pylons, which stood in endless row after row, a group of priests approached the travellers. It was the high-priest of Ammon-Râ, accompanied by eleven other priests; and they were all very old and grey. They all had grey locks and they all wore long, grey beards. They all wore long, fiery red robes; and, when they drew near in procession, they were like gods in their placid dignity.

They did not wish to betray their surprise to the travellers. The oracle of Ammon was no longer visited as it had been visited two centuries ago. It was no longer held in honour; the temple was fallen into decay; summers would pass without the advent of a single pilgrim. But Lucius had wanted to consult the oracle of Ammon just because its historic past gave it a poetic charm for him. And, when he saw the high-priest approach, he stretched out his hands in reverence to the ground and knelt and bowed his head; and Thrasyllus, Caleb and Tarrar knelt and bowed behind him.

===Chinese===
这座古老圣所四周环绕着三重垣墙，但都已倾颓坍坏；方尖碑渐渐陷落；斯芬克斯身上爬满繁花盛开的藤蔓；圣道（dromos）的石板缝里长出高高的草，所有的门都敞开着。笃耨香树（turpentine-tree）茂密的树冠投下浓荫，在阳光下散发着沉甸甸的香气。肉质的芦荟把剑一样的叶子探过墙头；长长的花梗上开出硕大的猩红花朵，像熏香似的冒着烟气。而尤其是那曼陀罗（datura），悬垂的雪花石膏杯盏倾泻出令人晕眩、令人沉醉的浓香，巨大的皇蛾绕着香气缓缓盘旋。

没有看门人；旅行者们继续前行，穿过无尽的圣道。整块巨石雕成的巨像在两侧耸立；可它们也在向一旁倾侧，或渐渐下陷。最后，从重重无尽、一列又一列的塔门（pylon）深处，走来一群祭司。为首的是阿蒙-拉的大祭司，身后跟着十一位祭司；他们都已高龄而灰白。人人一头灰发，人人飘着灰白的长须。人人穿着火焰般通红的长袍；当他们列队走近时，那安详的威仪宛如神明。

他们不愿向旅行者们显露自己的惊奇。阿蒙神谕所已不再像两个世纪前那样受人朝拜。它不再受人尊崇；神庙已然倾颓；常常一连几个夏天不见一个香客临门。而卢基乌斯偏要来求问阿蒙神谕，正是因为它往昔的历史于他有一种诗意的魅力。当他望见大祭司走近，便满怀敬意地向地面伸出双手，跪下叩首；特拉叙卢斯、卡莱布和塔拉尔也跪在他身后俯首行礼。

===Original===
“What do you seek, my son?” asked the centenarian high-priest.

“The truth,” replied Lucius.

“Then enter into the House of the Sun,” the high-priest ordained.

And the travellers rose; and the priests gladly led the way. They led their visitors through the pronaos and naos to the secos, to the holy of holies. And, pointing in the golden shade of midday dusk, between pillars like tree-trunks, to the enormous statue of Ammon-Râ, old as time, the sun-god with the bull’s head, the high-priest continued:

“The Sun reveals the truth to him who is worthy to hear it, even as ages ago he revealed the truth to Alexander of Macedon. Before his coming, the deity uttered himself only by moving his brows and wrinkling his bull forehead between his divine horns. But the deity addressed Alexander of Macedon with the sound of his lowing voice and told him, in words plainly audible to the king and all his following, that he was the son of the Sun, the son of Jupiter Ammon-Râ.”

Lucius looked up at the statue. In the golden twilight of the temple, where the noontide daylight filtered in and broke between the pillars in a shimmer of dust, he saw the supreme god, who was no longer held in honour, wrapped in shadow, paintless wood and colourless basalt, blind and pock-pitted where his bull head and his human neck had been robbed of the jewelled eyes and the precious stones with which he had once been inlaid. And Lucius felt so deep a compassion within himself for the fading god, once all-honoured and now forgotten in his distant, sinking temple in the Libyan desert, that he bent his knees in pity and reverence.

===Chinese===
“你寻求什么，我的孩子？”年逾百岁的大祭司问。

“真理，”卢基乌斯答道。

“那就进太阳之宫来吧，”大祭司吩咐道。

于是旅行者们起身；祭司们欣然引路。他们领着客人穿过前殿（pronaos）和正殿（naos），走进内殿（secos），走进圣中之圣。大祭司指向如树干般的巨柱之间、正午昏暗的金色光影里那尊巨大的阿蒙-拉神像——古老如时间本身的太阳神，生着公牛的头颅——继续说道：

“太阳向配得上聆听真理的人揭示真理，正如许多世代以前，他曾向马其顿的亚历山大揭示真理。在那位君王驾临之前，这位神明只以动眉、以在神角之间皱起牛额来示意。但当神明对马其顿的亚历山大说话时，他发出了哞鸣般低沉的声音，用国王和他全体随从都清楚听得见的话语告诉他：他是太阳之子，是朱庇特·阿蒙-拉（Jupiter Ammon-Râ）之子。”

卢基乌斯仰望神像。在神庙金黄的幽光里，正午的天光渗进来，在廊柱间碎成一星一星的微尘，他看见这位至高的神——已不再受人尊崇的神——裹在暗影之中，木头褪尽了彩绘，玄武岩失了色泽，牛首与人颈曾被挖去宝石镶成的眼睛和一度嵌满的贵重石头，只余瞎了的眼窝和斑驳的凿痕。卢基乌斯对这黯淡的神心生深切的怜悯——这位曾经备受尊崇的神，如今在利比亚荒漠中他那遥远而沉沦的神庙里被人遗忘——怜悯与敬畏之情使他屈下了双膝。

===Original===
The Jewish seer, who lived in the cave of Neith, had haply seen the new god, the Son of Jahve, crowned with light for days and days. Here, in the immensity of his ruined sanctuary, Lucius beheld the fading of the god who was forgotten, but whom, centuries ago, Alexander of Macedon had travelled through whirlwinds and sandstorms to seek.

When Lucius looked up, he was alone with the old high-priest:

“Father,” he said, kneeling, “I would know the truth. I would know if what I believe to be the truth, revealed to me by oracle after oracle, is the truth to Jupiter Ammon-Râ.”

“My son,” said the priest, “the truth does not shine forth until after meditation, after contemplation and pious prayers, after days and nights of communing with the deity. I will be your intermediary. And you shall know what you would know, if you have faith.”

“Father,” said Lucius, “I lay my forehead, heavy with care and suffering and doubt, in your holy hands.”

And he bowed his head towards the priest’s open palms.

===Chinese===
那位住在奈斯（Neith）洞窟里的犹太先知，或许已经见到了新的神、雅赫维（Jahve）之子，一连许多时日头戴光冠。而在这里，在他倾颓圣所的宏阔之中，卢基乌斯目睹的是一位被遗忘之神的黯淡——然而许多世纪以前，马其顿的亚历山大曾穿越旋风与沙暴来寻他。

卢基乌斯抬起头来时，身边只剩老祭司一人：

“神父，”他跪着说，“我想知道真理。我想知道，我相信是真理的东西——那一个又一个神谕启示给我的东西——在朱庇特·阿蒙-拉看来，是否真是真理。”

“我的孩子，”祭司说，“真理要经过沉思，经过观想与虔诚的祈祷，经过昼夜与神明相通，才会显现。我愿做你的中介。只要你心怀诚信，你便会知道你想知道的一切。”

“神父，”卢基乌斯说，“我这颗满载忧患、痛苦与疑惑的头颅，就交付在你神圣的手中了。”

于是他向祭司摊开的双手俯下了头。

---

===Original===
He remained five days and nights together with the priest. In the temple, the golden shadows of the day changed into the blue shadows of the night and the glittering of the sun into the flickering of the lamps. There was prayer and fasting and the touch of soul to soul.

===Chinese===
他与祭司同住了五天五夜。在神庙里，白昼金色的影子化作夜晚蓝色的影子，太阳的辉光化作灯焰的摇曳。有祈祷，有斋戒，有灵魂与灵魂的相触。

## XXII / 二十二

===Original===
After five days and nights, Lucius knew. Pale, tired and enlightened, he sought out his followers, Thrasyllus, Caleb and Tarrar, who were staying in the great, cavernous rooms of the temple. And he was calm, peaceful and dignified. He bathed and ate and slept. And at night, in the silence of the temple-grounds, which wove itself into one mystic atmosphere with the golden gleam of the stars, he woke Tarrar and said:

“Take this sycamore box.”

It was a small casket of delicate workmanship, which had always accompanied him wherever he went.

Tarrar, heavy with sleep, took up the little box.

===Chinese===
五天五夜之后，卢基乌斯明白了。他面色苍白，疲惫而澄明，去寻他的随行——特拉叙卢斯、卡莱布和塔拉尔，他们住在神庙高大幽深如洞穴的殿堂里。他平静、安详而有威仪。他沐浴，进食，安睡。夜里，在神庙庭院的寂静与星辰的金光交织成的神秘氛围中，他唤醒了塔拉尔，说：

“拿着这个西克莫无花果木（sycamore）匣子。”

那是一口做工精致的小匣，无论他走到哪里，它总是随身相伴。

塔拉尔睡意沉重，捧起了那只小匣。

===Original===
“Follow me,” said Lucius.

The little slave, in astonishment, followed his master. Lucius passed through the shadow-haunted temple-precincts, which stretched endlessly in every direction. He went through the parks, which were haunted with sphinxes and obelisks and thick with the sultry heat of datura-scent. He went through the whole oasis, under the grove of palm-trees and past the huts of the natives.

Tarrar followed him. The little slave felt, inquisitively, that the sycamore casket was not locked. He opened it for an instant; and by the flickering starlight Tarrar saw a small woman’s sandal, which he knew. The little slave wondered and wondered. But he continued to follow his master, faithfully; he would have followed him to the death.

They came to the desert. The master entered the desert; and Tarrar continued to wonder. The starry night now spread its dome over their heads; the silvery sands lay outstretched before him.

===Chinese===
“跟我来，”卢基乌斯说。

小奴隶惊讶地跟着主人。卢基乌斯穿过幽灵出没般幽影幢幢的神庙辖区，它向四面八方无尽延伸。他穿过园苑，那里斯芬克斯与方尖碑森然罗列，曼陀罗的浓香闷热稠密。他穿过整片绿洲，走过棕榈树林，经过土人的棚屋。

塔拉尔跟在后面。小奴隶好奇地摸出，那无花果木匣没有上锁。他打开看了一眼；借着闪烁的星光，塔拉尔看见一只小小的女式凉鞋，他认得的。小奴隶纳闷又纳闷。但他仍旧忠实地跟着主人；哪怕跟到死，他也会跟着。

他们来到了沙漠。主人走进沙漠；塔拉尔还在纳闷。星夜在头顶张开穹隆；银色的沙海在他面前铺展。

===Original===
“Dig,” Lucius commanded, suddenly turning round.

Tarrar gave a start. He put down the casket in the sand and dug a hole with his hands.

“Deeper,” Lucius commanded. “Dig deeper.”

The little slave dug; quickly, like a little monkey, he dug the hole deep with his two hands.

“Put the casket in the hole,” Lucius commanded.

===Chinese===
“挖，”卢基乌斯忽然转过身，命令道。

塔拉尔吓了一跳。他把匣子放在沙地上，用双手挖起坑来。

“再深，”卢基乌斯命令道，“再挖深些。”

小奴隶挖着；快得像只小猴子，用两只手把坑挖得深深的。

“把匣子放进坑里，”卢基乌斯命令道。

===Original===
Tarrar did so and looked at his master.

“Cover the casket up with sand.”

Tarrar did as his master commanded. Then Lucius said:

“Now come back.”

And he went back to the oasis; but Tarrar, before following him, stamped down the sand under which the casket lay buried and overwhelmed it, amid violent gestures of delight, with native curses, curses not to be averted, in the Libyan tongue.

===Chinese===
塔拉尔照办了，望着主人。

“用沙子把匣子埋上。”

塔拉尔遵命照办。于是卢基乌斯说：

“现在回来吧。”

他转身回绿洲去了；而塔拉尔在跟上之前，把埋着匣子的沙土狠狠踩实，又用利比亚土话冲它发出一连串土人的诅咒——种种无从禳解的诅咒，一面诅咒，一面狂喜地手舞足蹈。

## XXIII / 二十三

===Original===
The travellers had returned to Memphis and Caleb displayed the skin of a lion which had been shot in the desert and told the people in the thalamegus terrible tales of desert ghosts and dread visions. The barge was now gliding up the Nile in the night; the sky was softly blue, like dark byssus; the water was a pale blue, like rippling silk; and the waning moon hung above the palm-clusters and country-mansions on the riverbank like a great, overripe fruit which threatened to burst in the sky and whose juice was already trickling in thick orange drops that flowed far over the Nile.

And, while the rowers’ monotonous chant resounded with the regular beat of the oars, Thrasyllus, sitting beside Lucius, gave way to melancholy and said:

“Egypt is Egypt no longer. Alexandria is a commercial town; Memphis is a decaying greatness; and the priests are venal and no longer know the Hermetic wisdom. I have sought for five days among the dusty papyri of the neglected library in the temple of Ammon; it is as though all that is worth knowing were hiding itself.”

“The priests must be hiding the Hermetic wisdom on purpose,” said Lucius.

“They used to do so in other days for Plato and Pythagoras, when their souls were lofty and incorruptible. Nowadays they show what they have and tell what they know for money. But what they have is not more than we in Rome possess in the temple of Isis; and what they know is not the key to happiness. And yet﻿ ﻿… and yet I believe in a sacred word, handed down in the wisdom of the Kabbala by word of mouth, from father to son. But I have not yet received it from any priest, neither at Memphis nor in the oasis. And yet I have hopes. There is Thebes; and there are the secrets of Ethiopia﻿ ﻿… down to the pillars of Sesostris.”

===Chinese===
旅行者们已回到孟斐斯；卡莱布展示了一张在沙漠里射杀的狮子皮，给泰拉美戈斯游船上的人们讲述沙漠鬼魂和可怕幻象的骇人故事。此刻游船正趁夜溯尼罗河而上；天空是柔和的蓝色，像深色的细麻绢（byssus）；河水是淡淡的蓝，像起了涟漪的绸缎；残月悬在河岸的棕榈丛和乡间宅邸上空，像一颗熟透欲裂的巨大果子，仿佛随时要在天穹里迸开，汁液已经化作浓稠的橙色点滴，汩汩流泻在尼罗河上，流得很远很远。

桨手们单调的号子应和着整齐的桨声回荡着；特拉叙卢斯坐在卢基乌斯身旁，任忧郁涌上心头，说道：

“埃及已不再是埃及了。亚历山大里亚是座商业城市；孟斐斯是正在朽坏的伟业；祭司们见钱眼开，不再通晓赫尔墨斯（Hermetic）的智慧。我在阿蒙神庙那座无人照管的藏书室里，在积尘的纸草卷中寻了五天；仿佛一切值得知道的东西都在躲藏着自己。”

“祭司们一定是故意藏起了赫尔墨斯的智慧，”卢基乌斯说。

“从前，当他们的灵魂还高尚不可收买的时候，他们对柏拉图（Plato）和毕达哥拉斯（Pythagoras）就是这样做的。如今他们拿出自己有的东西，说出自己知道的东西，都为换钱。可他们有的，并不比我们罗马伊西斯神庙里的更多；他们知道的，也不是幸福的钥匙。然而……然而我相信一个神圣的词语，它凭卡巴拉（Kabbala）的智慧口口相传，从父亲传给儿子。可我还没有从任何祭司那里得到它，在孟斐斯没有，在绿洲也没有。但我仍存指望。还有底比斯（Thebes）呢；还有埃塞俄比亚（Ethiopia）的秘密……直到塞索斯特里斯石柱。”

===Original===
Lucius smiled gently:

“The word,” he said, “the secret of happiness﻿ ﻿… Thrasyllus, is happiness not an illusion of the brain? Does happiness not lie in resigning one’s self piously to one’s fate and is the secret word not the proud ‘Be a god unto yourself’?”

The old man started. And he whispered:

“You also? Have you also heard that word, as *I* heard it at Sais? I took no account of it, it did not satisfy me.”

“It satisfied me in the oasis, because it is a proud, strong word and I have needed pride and strength﻿ ﻿… since I have *known*, Thrasyllus.”

“Known what, Lucius?”

“That Carus stole Ilia from me.”

===Chinese===
卢基乌斯温文地微笑：

“那个词，”他说，“幸福的秘密……特拉叙卢斯，幸福难道不是头脑的一种幻觉？幸福难道不在于虔诚地把自己交付给命运，而那个秘密的词，不正是那句傲然的『做自己的神』吗？”

老人一震。他低声道：

“你也？你也听到过那个词，像*我*当年在塞易斯（Sais）听到的那样？我没把它放在心上，它没有满足我。”

“在绿洲它满足了我，因为那是一个高傲、有力的词，我需要骄傲和力量……自从我*知道*了以后，特拉叙卢斯。”

“知道什么了，卢基乌斯？”

“知道卡鲁斯从我身边偷走了伊利娅。”

===Original===
The old man started violently:

“You know?” he exclaimed. “You know? *Who* told you? *Who* betrayed the secret?”

“The voice itself within my own soul, which the oracles caused to speak to me. My own thoughts, tossing this way and that, which the oracles guided. From the sibyl of Rhacotis, who merely guessed my own thoughts, down to the old high-priest of Ammon-Râ, who spoke to me like a father﻿ ﻿… and who said to me the word, ‘Be a god unto yourself!’ ”

“As Nemu-Pha said to me, at Sais. I paid for it in gold.”

“I paid for it in gold in the oasis. But what does that matter, Thrasyllus? The word gave me strength and pride.”

“O my son, if you could be cured of your sorrow, of your grief!”

“They are no longer in me. I no longer have any grief, no longer any sorrow. I am a god unto myself.”

===Chinese===
老人猛地一震：

“你知道了？”他失声道，“你知道了？是*谁*告诉你的？是*谁*泄露了这秘密？”

“是我自己灵魂里的那个声音，是神谕使它对我开口。是我自己翻来覆去的思绪，是神谕引导了它们。从拉科提斯的女巫赫罗菲拉（Herophila）——她不过是猜中了我自己的心思——直到阿蒙-拉那位待我如父亲的老大祭司……他对我说出了那个词：『做自己的神！』”

“就像内穆-法（Nemu-Pha）当年在塞易斯对我说的那样。我为它付了黄金。”

“在绿洲我也为它付了黄金。可那有什么要紧，特拉叙卢斯？这个词给了我力量和骄傲。”

“噢我的孩子，但愿你能从你的伤痛、你的悲哀中痊愈！”

“它们已不在我心里了。我不再有悲哀，不再有伤痛。我是自己的神。”

===Original===
“The gods suffer. Isis suffered because of Osiris. All the gods suffer.”

“I suffer no longer. My grief has departed from me. The world and life are beautiful. See, the colours and the light are beautiful. The sky is softly blue, like dark byssus; the water ripples like blue silk; and the moon is like a great, overripe fruit which bursts in the sky and whose juice trickles over the Nile. Tomorrow the day will bring another beauty. In these successive beauties, Thrasyllus, I will be a god unto myself.”

“O my son, though I did not tell you the word myself, I am so happy that you yourself found the word!”

In the night there sounded the high, rising tones of a harp, followed by Cora’s crystal-clear voice, which was accompanied by other harps and other voices.

“The word of pride, the word of strength, Thrasyllus,” said Lucius, calmly; and the old tutor saw a tranquil smile on his young master’s face as he added, “The word that almost makes me happy.”

===Chinese===
“神明们也在受苦。伊西斯为俄西里斯受过苦。所有的神都受苦。”

“我不再受苦了。我的悲哀已离我而去。世界和生活是美的。你看，这色彩和光是美的。天空是柔和的蓝，像深色的细麻绢；河水像蓝绸般泛着涟漪；月亮像一颗熟透的巨大果子，在天穹里迸裂，汁液淌过尼罗河。明天白昼会带来另一种美。在这一连串的美里，特拉叙卢斯，我要做自己的神。”

“噢我的孩子，虽然不是我亲口把这个词告诉你，你能自己找到它，我多么高兴！”

夜色里响起竖琴高亢悠扬的琴音，接着是科拉水晶般清亮的嗓音，别的竖琴和别的歌声伴和着她。

“骄傲之词，力量之词，特拉叙卢斯，”卢基乌斯平静地说；老太傅望着年轻主人脸上安详的微笑，听他接着说，“那个几乎让我幸福的词。”

## XXIV / 二十四

===Original===
After the abundant dews of the night came the delight of the cool-warm summer day. The clustering trees now pressed their way forward more richly and luxuriously along the banks of the Nile. Here, on the Libyan side, lay the town of Acanthus, with its temple of Osiris in a spreading wood of Theban acanthus-trees, of which the natives tap the fragrant gums. Next, on the Arabian bank, came Aphroditopolis, the second town of the name, with the temple of the White Cow; and then the travellers reached the Heracleotic nome, a big island in the Nile, from which a canal cuts through the Arsinoic nome, the most fertile in the whole country. Here and here alone the olive-tree flourished in dense, silvery woods; but here also there twisted and twined, in close festoons, the vine-branches, on which the grapes were beginning to swell; here the fruit-trees bent under their heavy load and the orchards stretched; here the sickles of the husbandmen waved through the abundant ears of corn. Here the fat soil yielded wealth and prosperity; here the innumerable sheep spread in a wave of wool over the hills, like a shadowy white sea. Here, between margins of sands, Lake Moeris stretched exquisite and crystal blue to the horizon, as it were a freshwater inland sea. In earlier ages, the ocean must have extended to those margins and stolen the whole northern land of Lower Egypt, that gift of the Nile, as Herodotus had called it long ago. Here the double lotus-flowers were trained to blossom in the waters; and here the sacred scarabs were bred and worshipped upon the white flowers.

===Chinese===
夜里落过丰沛的露水，随之而来的是凉爽与温暖交织的夏日白昼的愉悦。沿尼罗河两岸，成簇的树木如今愈发丰茂繁密地探出身来。这一边，在利比亚一侧，坐落着阿坎图斯城（Acanthus），城里有一座俄西里斯神庙，藏身于一片底比斯莨苕树的广阔树林中，土人从那些树上割取芬芳的树胶。接着，在阿拉伯一侧的河岸，是阿佛洛狄忒城（Aphroditopolis）——同名的第二座——有白母牛神庙；随后旅行者们抵达赫拉克勒奥提斯诺姆（nome，古埃及的州），尼罗河中的一座大岛，一条运河由此切开阿尔西诺伊诺姆——全国最肥沃的地方。唯有在这里，橄榄树成林成片地繁茂，闪着银光；也唯有在这里，藤蔓的枝条紧密地盘绕垂缀，葡萄正在膨胀；这里果树被沉甸甸的果实压弯了腰，果园绵延伸展；这里农夫的镰刀在丰硕的麦穗间挥动。这里膏腴的土地出产富足与兴旺；这里数不清的绵羊如一波羊毛漫过丘陵，像一片朦胧的白海。这里，在沙岸之间，莫伊利斯湖（Lake Moeris）晶莹湛蓝，一直铺展到天边，宛如一片淡水的内海。在更古老的年代，大洋必定曾延伸到那些沙岸，吞没整个下埃及的北疆——那件尼罗河的赠礼，很久以前希罗多德便这样称呼它。这里，双色的莲花被驯养得在水中绽放；这里，圣甲虫被饲养供奉在白色的花朵上。

===Original===
Lucius would wander alone of a morning, strolling along the banks of the lake. It was so strangely calm here and so divinely beautiful; and a heaven-sent consolation filled the air. These were the regions blest by the gods; and it pleased Lucius to linger here. The thalamegus lay moored under acanthus-trees; the flowering reeds shot up to a man’s height around her. And every afternoon, at sunset, Lucius, sometimes accompanied by Thrasyllus, sometimes alone, walked to the labyrinth.

The road lay along the waterworks of the canal, where daily, under the supervision of the engineers, the quantity of water that flowed in and out of the lake through the canal was closely gauged. The tilled and inhabited lands around Lake Moeris, large as a sea, were never flooded. If the Nile increased, all that happened was that the blue crystal mirror of the lake rose. If the waters of the river fell, then those of the lake filled them up, by careful management of the sluices. The water was never other than a benevolent deity.

Along the waterworks ran the road to the labyrinth. In the sinking glory of the sun, in bloodred and orange splendours, Lucius saw it daily, the strange Titanic town of monoliths, the linked rows of palaces and courts, projecting their columns endlessly, endlessly, towards the sunlit horizon. Orange and bloodred gleams glowed over the flat stone tables of the roofs, which were not higher than a single pillared storey and which spread out their immense terrace like a paved desert. There were twenty palaces, each surrounded by twenty-seven monolithic columns; and all this wondrous architecture of past centuries was without a beam of wood, was without cement or masonry, was simply stone laid upon stone with faultless precision and column hewn beside column, absolutely circular, each column a single stone. At the end of the palaces, which were a stadium long, rose the square pyramid, the tomb of the builder, King Amenemha.

===Chinese===
清晨，卢基乌斯常独自徜徉，沿着湖岸漫步。这里出奇地宁静，美得如有神助；空气中弥漫着一种天赐的抚慰。这是蒙诸神祝福的地界；卢基乌斯乐于在此流连。泰拉美戈斯游船系泊在莨苕树下；开花的芦苇在她四周长得有一人高。每天下午，日落时分，卢基乌斯有时由特拉叙卢斯陪伴，有时独自一人，步行前往迷宫（labyrinth）。

道路沿着运河的水利设施延伸。每天，在工程师们的监督下，流经运河出入湖中的水量都被仔细测算。莫伊利斯湖周围耕种的、有人居住的土地，虽然大如海洋，却从不被洪水淹没。尼罗河若涨水，不过是湖水那蓝水晶般的明镜升高而已。河水若回落，便靠精心的水闸调度，用湖水把它填满。水从来都是一位仁慈的神明。

沿着水利设施，便是通往迷宫的路。在夕阳沉落的辉煌里，在血红与橙红的壮丽中，卢基乌斯每天都能望见它——那座整石垒成的奇异的泰坦之城，宫殿与庭院一列列相连，把廊柱无尽无尽地投向阳光朗照的地平线。橙红与血红的余晖照在屋顶平坦的石台上——那些屋顶不过一层柱廊高，却把巨大的露台铺展开去，像一片铺石而成的荒漠。这里有二十座宫殿，每座环绕二十七根整石雕成的巨柱；这整个往昔世纪的奇妙建筑，不用一根木梁，不用灰浆砌筑，只是石头精确无比地垒着石头，柱子挨着柱子凿出，浑圆无缺，每根柱子就是一整块石头。在宫殿的尽头——它们有一斯塔迪亚长——耸起方形的金字塔，那是建造者阿梅内姆哈王（King Amenemha）的陵墓。

===Original===
The holy place was guarded by priests, who led Lucius through the halls and crypts. The twenty palaces represented the former twenty Egyptian nomes, or provinces; and the emissaries of each province used to gather with their priests and priestesses in their palace or court and offer up sacrifices and discuss great questions of policy or local welfare. But nowadays the palaces were deserted, the crypts were deserted, and the priests led Lucius along endless, deserted, winding corridors which meandered from palace to palace. The torches smeared the walls with bloodred light, smeared the smooth stone walls of the corridors and halls and floors and ceilings, stone after stone upon stone of wonderful dimensions all resting one upon another without cement. And to Lucius it was one of the marvels of the world, even more marvellous, because of its sublime human architecture, than the pyramids had been.

Travelling on camels with Uncle Catullus and a great retinue, Lucius went a hundred stadia farther, to Arsinoe-Crocodilopolis. The trees flourished more richly, more luxuriantly, like a richly-wooded park around the travellers, till they came to the sacred lake where the sacred crocodile, named Such, was held in veneration.

“Well,” said Uncle Catullus, “here’s another of these little pets which are kept for the edification of foreigners!”

===Chinese===
圣所由祭司们看守，他们领着卢基乌斯穿行于殿堂与密室之间。那二十座宫殿代表从前埃及的二十个诺姆，即行省；从前，各行省的使节与他们的祭司、女祭司聚在各自的宫殿或庭院里献祭，商议大政或地方福祉。如今宫殿荒废了，密室荒废了，祭司们领着卢基乌斯走过无尽、荒凉、曲折的回廊，回廊蜿蜒着一宫接一宫。火把把血红的火光涂抹在墙上，涂抹在回廊、殿堂、地面与顶棚光滑的石壁上——一块块尺寸惊人的石头，就这样不用灰浆地彼此叠靠着。在卢基乌斯看来，这是世界的一大奇观，正因为它那崇高的人类建筑，甚至比金字塔更加奇妙。

卢基乌斯带着卡图卢斯叔父和一大队随从，骑着骆驼又行了一百斯塔迪亚，来到阿尔西诺伊-鳄鱼城（Arsinoe-Crocodilopolis）。树木愈发繁茂葱茏，像一座林木蓊郁的园林环拥着旅行者们，直到他们来到圣湖——圣鳄鱼苏赫（Such）就供奉在那里，受人崇敬。

“好啊，”卡图卢斯叔父说，“又是一个养着给外邦人开开眼的小宝贝！”

===Original===
And in fact the priests who came to meet the travellers in front of the dromos of the sacred lake, surrounded on every side with pillars, first amiably demanded a stater a head as entrance-fee, while Caleb, of course, had seen to a supply of rich provisions, as an offering to the deity. The slaves carried baskets with cakes, roast meats and jars of hydromel.

In the lake lay Such, the huge monster; but the priests had tamed the terrible deity: they were luring him from the middle of the lake, where his temple was, to the bank, because some Persian visitors happened to have arrived before Lucius and wished to present their offerings. On the edge of the lake the priests took Such fearlessly by his terrible jaws and made him swallow the cakes and meat and wine of the Persian grandees, who were greatly diverted and laughed aloud.

“They must be great noblemen,” said Caleb, “and are going from the pillars of Sesostris to Alexandria, even as you, my noble lords, are going from Alexandria to the pillars of Sesostris. My lord, if you permit me, I should much like to exchange a few words with the guide of the Persian lords.”

Lucius gave his permission; and the crocodile, who had swallowed his Persian presents, swam back to the middle of the lake. But the priests now quickly lured him to the other side, where Lucius was waiting; and the ever greedy crocodile approached; and the priests again took him fearlessly by his terrible jaws and the monster now swallowed Lucius’ gifts, the cakes, the roast meat, as though insatiable; and the priests, laughing, emptied a jar of hydromel into his maw.

===Chinese===
果然，祭司们到圣湖前的圣道来迎旅行者，圣湖四围列着廊柱；他们先客客气气地索要每人一斯塔特的入场费，而卡莱布当然早已备好丰盛的吃食，作为献给神明的供物。奴隶们抬着一篮篮糕饼、烤肉和一坛坛蜜水酒。

湖里卧着苏赫，那庞大的巨怪；但祭司们早已驯服了这位可怕的神明：他们正把它从湖心——它的神庙所在——诱向岸边，因为碰巧有几位波斯客人先卢基乌斯一步到了，想呈献他们的供品。在湖边，祭司们毫不畏惧地抓住苏赫那可怕的颚，让它吞下波斯贵人们的糕饼、肉和酒；那些贵人大为开心，放声大笑。

“他们想必是了不起的贵人，”卡莱布说，“正从塞索斯特里斯石柱前往亚历山大里亚，恰如诸位贵主从亚历山大里亚前往塞索斯特里斯石柱。主上，倘若您允许，我很想同波斯贵主们的向导攀谈几句。”

卢基乌斯应允了；那鳄鱼吞下波斯的礼物，游回湖心去了。可祭司们随即又把它诱向另一边，卢基乌斯正等在那里；那永远贪嘴的鳄鱼游近了；祭司们又毫不畏惧地抓住它可怕的颚，那巨怪吞下了卢基乌斯的礼物——糕饼、烤肉——仿佛永不餍足；祭司们笑着，把一整坛蜜水酒倒进它的喉咙。

===Original===
Meanwhile, Caleb, after a few words with the guide, had sunk in salaams before the Persian lords.

“He’s offering the Persians his diversorium,” said Uncle Catullus, jestingly.

And in fact Caleb, in a few minutes, came back happy and swaggering to his own travellers and said, in a mysterious whisper:

“I have recommended their Persian lordships to be sure to stop at the Hermes House at Alexandria; and I slipped a gold ptolemy into their guide’s hand. Yes, my lords, business is business; and, if we did no business at Alexandria, I could never hope to see my beloved Saba again. For there you must have *done* business, if you want to live in the country; there’s no business to be done *there*, my lords.”

===Chinese===
这时，卡莱布已同那位向导寒暄了几句，正对着波斯贵人们连连躬身行礼。

“他在向波斯人兜售他的客栈呢，”卡图卢斯叔父打趣道。

果然，没过几分钟，卡莱布便喜滋滋、大摇大摆地回到自家主人这边，神秘地悄声说：

“我向波斯贵主们举荐了亚历山大里亚的赫尔墨斯之家（Hermes House），请他们务必下榻；我还往他们向导的手心里塞了一枚托勒密金币。是啊，诸位主上，生意就是生意；我们在亚历山大里亚若是做不成生意，我就永远别想再见到我心爱的萨巴了。因为要想在那个国家过日子，你就必须先*做成*生意；在那儿可是没有生意可*做*的，诸位主上。”

===Original===
By way of Heracleopolis, where divine honours were paid to the ichneumon, the spotted rat that devours the eggs of the adders and attacks the adders themselves, after first rolling itself in the mud, which dries round its body and forms an armour, the travellers reached Cynopolis, where the dog is worshipped as Anubis, and Oxyrynchus, where the fish of the name is venerated. And it now appeared that, in this region of Heptanomis, where, on the Arabian side of the Nile, the strange battlements of the blinding white Alabastrites Mountains blaze against the sky, all animals received divine honours, as though the priests had instituted these popular forms of worship in great numbers everywhere, so that they might the more carefully keep to themselves the secret Hermetic wisdom. Cats and falcons, sheep and wolves, baboons and zebus, eagles and lions, goats and spiders: all the animals were worshipped in one or other town or village; all the animals had their temples and priests; and Uncle Catullus said that he grew weary of having to admire so many sacred animals, especially as Apis, the little bull, and Such, the crocodile, were after all the only ones that were really interesting to see. But all these cattle, all these birds and fishes, all these creatures, from carnivora down to insects, were worshipped, tended, fed and shown in the temples to strangers, each time at a piece of gold a head. No, it was really too silly, especially when, after the first Crocodilopolis, on the left, the second Crocodilopolis appeared on the right, on the bank of the Nile, with another Such!

===Chinese===
取道赫拉克勒奥波利斯（Heracleopolis）——那里供奉着埃及獴，那种吞吃蝮蛇的蛋、连蝮蛇本身也敢攻击的花斑鼠，攻击之前先在泥里打个滚，泥浆干在身上便成了铠甲——旅行者们又到了库诺波利斯（Cynopolis），那里把狗当作阿努比斯崇拜；还到了俄克喜林库斯（Oxyrynchus），那里供奉同名的神鱼。这时他们才明白：在赫普塔诺米斯（Heptanomis）地区——尼罗河阿拉伯一侧，炫目的白色阿拉巴斯特里特斯山（Alabastrites Mountains）那奇特的雉堞在天际灼灼放光——一切动物都享着神的尊荣，仿佛祭司们在各处大量设立这类大众化的崇拜，好把秘密的赫尔墨斯智慧更牢地攥在自己手里。猫与隼，绵羊与狼，狒狒与瘤牛，鹰与狮，山羊与蜘蛛：一切动物都在这个或那个城镇村庄里受着崇拜；一切动物都有各自的神庙和祭司；卡图卢斯叔父说，要他去赞叹这么多圣兽，他都腻烦了，尤其阿皮斯那头小公牛和苏赫那条鳄鱼，毕竟才是真正值得一看的。可所有这些牲畜，所有这些鸟和鱼，所有这些生灵，从食肉兽直到昆虫，都在神庙里受人崇拜、照料、喂养，展示给外邦人看，每次每人收一片金子。不，这实在太过愚蠢，尤其是，过了第一座鳄鱼城，左边刚过，右边尼罗河岸上又出现了第二座鳄鱼城，还有另一条苏赫！

===Original===
“Lucius,” said Uncle Catullus, “honestly, I’m not going to feed any more sacred crocodiles, nor any sacred goats or cats or beetles either. I’ve seen enough of all those pests, do you hear, nephew?”

And Lucius and Thrasyllus were inclined to agree with Uncle Catullus; and the barge sped past the wondrous purple of the Porphyrites Mountains, gold-ruddy crests in the orange evening sky, up to Ptolemais, the greatest town in the Thebais.

Ptolemais was a prosperous city, ruled like Alexandria by a municipal government founded on the Greek model; but after Ptolemais the travellers were especially charmed with Abydos. Here they saw the Memnonium, which was not so gigantic as the labyrinth, but which still was built of great single blocks of stone, according to that same marvellous system of lost ancient architecture. They also saw the underground well, which is reached by vaults and galleries, a subterranean miracle of monoliths, always fitted to one another and upon one another, without masonry. The temple of Apollo rose in a flowering acacia-wood, as in a sudden dream of swarming white, fragrant blossoms.

===Chinese===
“卢基乌斯，”卡图卢斯叔父说，“说实在的，我再也不喂什么圣鳄鱼了，圣山羊、圣猫、圣甲虫也一概不喂。这些劳什子我算是看够了，听见没有，侄儿？”

卢基乌斯和特拉叙卢斯都倾向于赞同卡图卢斯叔父；游船驶过斑岩山（Porphyrites Mountains）奇异的紫色——那一道道金红的山峰映在橙色傍晚的天空里——直抵托勒迈伊斯（Ptolemais），底比斯地区最大的城市。

托勒迈伊斯是一座繁华的城市，像亚历山大里亚一样，由希腊式的市政自治政府治理；但过了托勒迈伊斯，最令旅行者们着迷的是阿拜多斯（Abydos）。在这里他们看到了门农神庙（Memnonium），它不及迷宫那样巨大，却也是用巨大的整块石头筑成，依着那失传的古建筑术的同一套奇妙体系。他们还看了地下井，要穿过穹顶与廊道才能抵达，一座整石彼此相嵌、层层相叠、不用砌筑的地下奇迹。阿波罗神庙耸立在一片开花的金合欢林中，恍如白花簇簇、芬芳馥郁的一场骤然降临的梦。

===Original===
